---
title: Developers
description: "Build on Kortix: the CLI, git and change requests, the kortix.yaml reference, the REST API, Apps, self-hosting and the @kortix/sdk."
---

Everything a developer needs to build on Kortix, on one page: the CLI and git workflow, the `kortix.yaml` reference, the REST API, Apps, self-hosting, and the TypeScript SDK with its React hooks and UI components. For what Kortix is and how people use it, read the [Guide](/docs/guide).

## Quickstart

Go from an empty terminal to a merged change request. Every step is a git operation on your project's repository:

| Step | Command | What happens in git |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Create a project | `kortix init`, `kortix ship` | `git init`, commit, push to the project's repository |
| Get a project | `kortix projects clone` | `git clone` through Kortix |
| Run a session | `kortix sessions new` | The agent works on its own branch, named `<session_id>` |
| Review and merge | `kortix cr merge` | The session branch merges into the default branch |

### Install the CLI

```sh
curl -fsSL https://kortix.com/install | bash
```

The script supports macOS and Linux on x86_64 and arm64. On Windows, run it inside WSL. To pin a version or use the dev channel, see [CLI → Install](/docs/developers#install).

### Sign in

```sh
kortix login
```

Your browser opens. Approve the CLI there. The CLI then picks your account and a default project.

With one project, the CLI picks that project. With several, it asks. With none, it skips this step.

On a machine without a browser, sign in with a token: `kortix login --token kortix_pat_…`. The token kinds are in [Use any git client](/docs/developers#use-any-git-client).

### Create or get a project

A project is a git repository with a `kortix.yaml` at its root. Create one, or clone one a teammate made.

<Tabs>
<Tab title="New project">

```sh
kortix init my-agent
cd my-agent
kortix ship
```

`kortix init` creates the `my-agent` directory from the starter and runs `git init -b main`. It asks which local coding tools to wire up: Claude Code, Codex, pi or Cursor. Codex is selected by default. It ends with a prompt to paste into your coding tool.

`kortix ship` validates `kortix.yaml`, commits, offers to set missing secrets, pushes the current branch and connects declared connectors. In a folder with no link and no default project, it creates the project.

With no `origin`, Kortix creates and manages the repository. With a GitHub `origin`, Kortix links that repository. Then plain `git push` and `git pull` work. Details: [Commit and push](/docs/developers#commit-and-push).

<Callout type="warning">
If `kortix login` saved a default project, `kortix ship` in an unlinked folder ships to that project, secrets included. Run `kortix projects unset` before the first `kortix ship` to create a new project.
</Callout>

</Tab>
<Tab title="Existing project">

```sh
kortix projects ls --all
kortix projects clone <project_id> my-agent
cd my-agent
```

`kortix projects clone` clones the repository with your Kortix login. The directory name defaults to the project ID, so pass a name. The CLI writes `.kortix/link.json` in the clone and installs a credential helper, so plain `git pull` and `git push` work.

Cloning needs `project.gitops.push`, which the project **Manager** role holds. A **Member** cannot clone. A Member starts sessions in the web app, or from any folder after `kortix projects use <project_id>`. A Member also needs access to an agent: see [Teams](/docs/guide#teams).

Run the next commands from inside the clone. Outside it, commands use your default project.

</Tab>
<Tab title="Web app">

Open **Create a project** in the web app (`/new`). Type a **Project name**. With more than one account, pick the **Account**. Choose the repository under **Git account**:

| Choice | Result |
| --- | --- |
| **Kortix managed** | Kortix creates and manages a private repository. |
| A GitHub account, **Create a new repository** | Kortix creates a private repository in that GitHub account. |
| A GitHub account, **Import an existing repository** | Kortix links a repository you already have. It keeps its own files. |

Click **Create project**. To work on it from a terminal, run `kortix projects clone`.

</Tab>
</Tabs>

### What a new project contains

`kortix init` and a new repository from **Create project** start from the same starter:

```text
my-agent/
├── kortix.yaml        the manifest (kortix_version: 3): skills, agents, one trigger, secret names
├── agents/            the prompt of each agent: kortix.md, harness-reflector.md, session-reviewer.md
├── skills/            11 skills, one folder each, with a SKILL.md
├── memory/MEMORY.md   the project memory
├── README.md
└── .gitignore
```

- `kortix` is the default agent. Every grant it has is `all`.
- `harness-reflector` refines the agents' prompts, skills and tools from recent sessions, through one change request. Its daily trigger is off until you set `enabled: true`.
- `session-reviewer` is a subagent of `harness-reflector`.
- All three set `workspace: { project_repository: true }`, so each session gets a checkout of the repository on its own branch in `/workspace`.
- The Kortix skills (`kortix-*`) are always registered. The `skills` key in `kortix.yaml` lists the other 10.

`kortix init` also writes files for the coding tools you picked. Codex, pi and Cursor get `AGENTS.md`. Codex, Claude Code and pi get a link to `skills/` in `.agents/`, `.claude/` or `.pi/`. Every key is in the [kortix.yaml reference](/docs/developers#kortixyaml-reference).

### Run a session

A session is one unit of agent work. It runs in its own sandbox, on its own branch.

```sh
kortix sessions new --prompt "Add a skill that drafts the weekly status report"
```

The session runs the project's default agent, `kortix`. `--agent <name>` and `--model <id>` override the agent and the model. In a terminal, the CLI then prints the `kortix sessions chat` command for the new session. In the web app, type the prompt on the project page.

On the Credits plan or Enterprise, a session runs on the platform default model. On Free, connect your own provider key or a ChatGPT subscription first. See [Models](/docs/guide#models).

| Task | Command |
| --- | --- |
| Talk to the agent | `kortix chat` (picks your most recent running session) |
| Send one prompt | `kortix chat <session_id> --prompt "…"` |
| Open a shell in the session's sandbox | `kortix sessions shell <session_id>` |
| List sessions | `kortix sessions ls` |

### Review and merge

A change request proposes to merge the session branch into the default branch (`main` in a new project). Kortix does not open a change request by itself.

The Kortix skill `kortix-system` tells the agent to push, open one with `kortix cr open`, and wait. The starter agents may load it: their `skills` grant is `all`. If the agent did not open one, ask it to, or open it yourself:

```sh
kortix cr open --head <session_id> --session <session_id> --title "Weekly status report skill"
```

Then review and merge:

```sh
kortix cr ls          # open change requests
kortix cr diff 1      # the unified diff
kortix cr merge 1     # merge into the base branch
git pull              # bring the merged work into your clone
```

`1` is the change request number from `kortix cr ls`. To send the work back, run `kortix cr request-changes 1 --message "…"`. The note goes to the change request's session and wakes it if it sleeps. In the web app, use the project's **Review** page.

The starter `kortix` agent holds `kortix_permissions: all`, so the API lets it merge its own change request. The `kortix-system` skill tells it to wait until you ask. Who may merge: [Agents that merge](/docs/developers#agents-that-merge).

### Check your setup

```sh
kortix doctor
```

`kortix doctor` checks your login and the project. Then it starts a throwaway session, sends `ping`, waits for the agent's reply, and deletes the session. It exits `1` when a check fails. `--no-session` stops after the login and project checks.

## Git

Every Kortix project is one git repository. You clone it and push to it with any git client. You fetch the branch of each session, and land work on the base branch through change requests. This section is for everyone who works on project files outside the web app, and for admins who decide who may push.

The shortest path from a project to a reviewed change:

```bash
kortix projects clone <project_id> my-project
cd my-project
git checkout -b tighten-triage
# edit agents/, skills/ or kortix.yaml
git commit -am "Tighten the triage prompt"
git push origin tighten-triage
kortix cr open --head tighten-triage --title "Tighten the triage prompt"
```

### Your project's repository

A project has one of two kinds of repository. Both work the same way after the project exists.

| Kind | What it is | How you get it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Managed | Kortix creates and manages a private repository. You need no GitHub account. | Pick **Kortix managed** under **Git account** when you create a project. Or run `kortix ship` in a folder with no `origin` remote, or `kortix ship --origin managed`. |
| GitHub | A repository in your GitHub account or organization. Kortix reaches it through the Kortix GitHub App, which you install under **Account settings → Git**, or through a GitHub token. | Pick a GitHub account under **Git account**, then **Create a new repository** or **Import an existing repository**. Or run `kortix ship` in a folder whose `origin` is on GitHub. |

On a self-hosted instance, a platform admin sets up managed repositories in the admin console. `GET /v1/projects/managed-git/status` returns `configured: false` until then, and the new-project form says "Kortix-managed repositories are not available on this instance." See [Self-hosting](/docs/developers#self-hosting).

#### The git origin

Every project has one clone URL, whatever its kind:

```text
https://<api-host>/v1/git/<project_id>.git
```

On Kortix Cloud this is `https://api.kortix.com/v1/git/<project_id>.git`. Find it in two places:

- `kortix projects info <project_id> --json`, field `git_origin_url`.
- **Customize → Settings → Git repo → Use your own Git client → Clone address**.

Clone `git_origin_url`, not `repo_url`. `repo_url` is the upstream repository that Kortix forwards to.

- The origin speaks git over HTTPS only.
- You authenticate with a Kortix token. Kortix adds the upstream credential on the server. No GitHub or other provider credential reaches your computer or a sandbox.
- The audit log records each clone and fetch as `git.clone` and each push as `git.push`. See [Audit log](/docs/guide#audit-log).

### Clone a project

<Steps>
<Step title="Install the CLI and sign in">
Follow [CLI → Install](/docs/developers#install), then run `kortix login`.
</Step>
<Step title="Clone">
Run `kortix projects clone [<project_id>] [<dir>]`. `kortix projects ls` lists project ids. Without an id, the command clones the linked or default project. The directory defaults to the project id, not the project name.
</Step>
<Step title="Finish setup in the new folder">
Optional: run `kortix init --force` to configure your local coding agents in the checkout. Run `kortix env pull` to write a `.env` file with one line per project secret. It writes names only: secret values stay in Kortix.
</Step>
</Steps>

**Customize → Settings → Git repo → Work on this locally** shows the same three steps with the commands filled in.

The clone does three things:

1. It runs `git clone` with your token in the process environment. The token is never in the remote URL or in `.git/config`.
2. It writes `.kortix/link.json`, which binds the folder to the project for `kortix` commands. It adds the file to `.git/info/exclude`, so the link stays on your computer.
3. It installs a credential helper for the origin, in the repository's own `.git/config`:

```ini
[credential "https://api.kortix.com/v1/git/<project_id>.git"]
	helper =
	helper = !kortix git-credential
[credential]
	useHttpPath = true
```

The empty `helper =` line turns off every helper you configured elsewhere for this URL, so your keychain never stores the Kortix token. `kortix git-credential` gives git the token of your stored login each time git asks. After the clone, plain `git pull` and `git push` work. If that login is gone, git stops with `Kortix Git needs login. Run: kortix login`, plus `--host <name>` when the link names a host.

The clone writes the link and the helper only when the repository root has `.kortix/`, `kortix.yaml` or `kortix.yml`. In any other repository, create a `.kortix/` folder at the root, then wire them by hand as in [Wire the helper by hand](#wire-the-helper-by-hand).

### Use any git client

The origin accepts HTTP Basic authentication. The username can be anything; the convention is `x-access-token`. The password is a Kortix token.

```bash
git clone https://api.kortix.com/v1/git/<project_id>.git
# Username: x-access-token
# Password: kortix_pat_…
```

Plain git saves the password in the credential store you configured. To keep the token out of every store, send it as a header on each command instead:

```bash
git -c http.extraHeader="Authorization: Bearer $KORTIX_GIT_TOKEN" clone https://api.kortix.com/v1/git/<project_id>.git
```

| Token | What it can do |
| --- | --- |
| Personal access key, `kortix_pat_…`, from `kortix login`, **Settings → Personal access keys** or `kortix tokens new` | Acts as you. Kortix checks it against your role on the project. Membership of the account alone gives no access. |
| Project CLI token, `kortix_pat_…`, from `kortix projects cli-tokens new` | Acts as you, on one project only. |
| Session token, `KORTIX_TOKEN` inside a sandbox | Reads the repository and pushes the session's own branch. See [Who can clone and push](#who-can-clone-and-push). |
| Service account key (`kortix_sa_…`), OAuth token, gateway key, browser sign-in token | Refused with `401`. |

[Security](/docs/guide#security) explains how to create and revoke keys.

#### Wire the helper by hand

Run these at the root of the checkout. `kortix projects link` refuses a folder without `.kortix/` or `kortix.yaml`. The helper answers only for the URL that equals the project's `git_origin_url`.

```bash
ORIGIN=https://api.kortix.com/v1/git/<project_id>.git
git config --local --replace-all credential.$ORIGIN.helper ''
git config --local --add credential.$ORIGIN.helper '!kortix git-credential'
git config --local credential.useHttpPath true
kortix projects link <project_id>
```

`kortix projects link` writes `.kortix/link.json`. The helper reads it to find the project and the host.

#### From CI

Create a project CLI token with `kortix projects cli-tokens new` and store it as a CI secret. The token acts as the person who created it, so that person needs push access to push.

```bash
AUTH="Authorization: Bearer $KORTIX_GIT_TOKEN"
git -c http.extraHeader="$AUTH" clone https://api.kortix.com/v1/git/$PROJECT_ID.git project
cd project
# change files, then commit
git -c http.extraHeader="$AUTH" push origin HEAD:refs/heads/ci-update
```

#### Errors from the origin

| Status | Message | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `400` | `invalid project identifier` | Use the project id, a UUID, in the URL. |
| `401` | `authentication required`, with `WWW-Authenticate: Basic realm="Kortix Git"` | Send a token. Git prompts for one. |
| `401` | `git proxy requires a Kortix token` | The password is not a Kortix token. Use a `kortix_pat_` token. |
| `403` | `token is not authorized for this project` | You have no role on the project that allows the operation. See [Who can clone and push](#who-can-clone-and-push). |
| `403` | `token is scoped to a different project` | The token is bound to another project. |
| `404` | `Not found` | The project does not exist or was deleted. |
| `409` | `Repository changed during authorization; retry the request` | Someone replaced the repository while you connected. Retry. |
| `409` | `Kortix cannot reach <owner>/<repo>: …`, printed as `remote: …` | The repository is disconnected. See [Repository disconnected](#repository-disconnected). |
| `502` | `No git upstream is configured for this project` or `git upstream unreachable` | Kortix cannot reach the upstream repository. Check the connection under **Git repo**. |
| `503` | `git_credential_unavailable` | Kortix could not get a credential for the upstream. Retry when the body says `retry: true`. Otherwise check the connection. |

### Commit and push

After a clone, commit and push with plain git. `kortix ship` runs these steps in one command:

1. It runs the `kortix validate` checks. `--no-verify` skips them.
2. It runs `git add -A` and commits, with the message from `-m`. The default message is `kortix: ship`. `--no-commit` fails when the working tree has changes.
3. It offers to set each `env` secret that has no value. `--no-env` skips this.
4. It pushes the current branch to the branch of the same name.
5. It connects each declared connector that still needs sign-in. `--no-connect` skips this.

`-n` / `--dry-run` prints the plan and changes nothing. `kortix deploy` is an alias.

#### The first ship

A folder with no `.kortix/link.json` gets a new project, unless the CLI has a default project. `kortix login` and `kortix projects use` set one. Ship then pushes to the default project instead. `kortix projects unset` clears the default.

For a new project, ship picks the repository from the folder's `origin`, without a prompt:

| `origin` of the folder | What ship creates |
| --- | --- |
| A GitHub URL | A GitHub project through the Kortix GitHub App. If the App is not installed, ship prints an install link and asks you to retry after you install it. With `-y` it stops instead. `--github-token <PAT>` links without the App; the token needs repository Contents read and write. |
| Another URL (GitLab, Bitbucket, plain git) | Nothing. Kortix links GitHub repositories only, so ship stops before any request and suggests `--origin managed`. |
| None, or `--origin managed` | A managed repository. Ship sets `origin` to the git origin and installs the credential helper. |

For a GitHub URL, the first ship pushes to your existing `origin` with your own git credentials.

`--origin <github-url>` sets `origin` to that GitHub URL first. `--account <id|slug>` picks the account, and `--name` the project name.

Ship writes `.kortix/link.json` the moment the project exists, and `git add -A` commits it. A teammate with push access (`project.gitops.push`, the Manager role) who clones the repository can then run `kortix ship` too. Later ships set `origin` to the project's git origin and install the credential helper.

### Who can clone and push

Five permissions control git:

| Permission | Allows |
| --- | --- |
| `project.gitops.read` | Read commits, diffs and change requests. For a session: clone and fetch. |
| `project.gitops.push` | Push. Open change requests. |
| `project.gitops.merge` | Merge change requests. |
| `project.gitops.ref.any` | For a session: push a branch other than its own. |
| `project.gitops.ref.delete` | Delete a branch. |

A project **Member** holds `project.gitops.read` only. A Member reads the branches, commits and change requests of the sessions they may open. A Member reads nothing of another member's private session.

A clone serves every branch, so a person needs `project.gitops.push` to clone or fetch. A Member cannot clone from their own git client. A Member's sessions still clone and push their own branch.

A **Manager** holds all five. Account owners and admins act as Managers on every project in the account. See [Teams](/docs/guide#teams).

**People** with push access:

- may push any branch, including the default branch;
- need `project.gitops.ref.delete` to delete a branch;
- can never delete the default branch. Nobody can.

IP allowlist and MFA policies of the account apply to git requests too.

**Sessions** push with the session token:

- A session pushes only its own branch, the branch named after its `session_id`.
- Any other branch needs `project.gitops.ref.any` in the agent's `kortix_permissions`. Deleting a branch needs `project.gitops.ref.delete`.
- A push to the default branch also needs `project.agent.write`, `project.trigger.create`, `project.trigger.update` and `project.trigger.delete`.
- The agent's `kortix_permissions` must list the permission, or be `all`, and the agent's ceiling role must also hold it. Neither widens the other. The role of the person who started the session is not an input. An agent with no `kortix_permissions` key holds no project permission. See [What an agent may do](/docs/guide#agents).

By default, agents land work through [change requests](#change-requests). [Agents](/docs/guide#agents) shows how to grant permissions.

Kortix does not block a force-push. On a GitHub repository, add a ruleset that blocks force-pushes and deletions on the default branch. The ruleset does not block change-request merges, because a merge is a fast-forward or a merge commit.

#### Read a refused push

Kortix refuses the whole push when it refuses one ref. Git prints the reason for each ref:

```text
 ! [remote rejected] HEAD -> feature-x (a session may only push its own branch (<session_id>); commit there and open a change request to land this elsewhere)
```

| Reason | Cause |
| --- | --- |
| `<branch> is the default branch and cannot be deleted` | A delete of the default branch. |
| `deleting <ref> requires git ref-delete authority` | A person without `project.gitops.ref.delete` deleted a branch. |
| `a session may only push its own branch (…)` | A session without `project.gitops.ref.any` pushed another branch. |
| `pushing <branch> skips change-request review and needs agent and trigger write permission; …` | A session pushed the default branch without `project.gitops.ref.any` and the manifest permissions. |
| `pushing <branch> could change agent grants without review, and an agent grants only what it holds; …` | A governed agent pushed the default branch but does not hold every permission it could grant. |
| `a session may not delete its own branch` | A session without `project.gitops.ref.delete` deleted its branch. |

### Session branches

Each session works on its own branch. The branch name is the `session_id`, with no prefix. Kortix cuts the branch from the session's `base_ref`, which is the project default branch unless the session was created with another one.

A session gets the branch and a checkout in `/workspace` only when its agent sets `workspace: { project_repository: true }`. The starter agents set it. Without it, repository calls answer `403` `session_repository_access_denied`.

Inside the sandbox, the branch is `$KORTIX_BRANCH_NAME`. The agent commits there and runs `git push origin HEAD`. [Inside a session sandbox](/docs/developers#inside-a-session-sandbox) lists the other variables.

A pushed branch stays in the repository after the session stops and after the session is deleted. `/workspace` holds this session's files, kept for the life of the session. Work the agent did not push is gone when the session is deleted. See [Computers](/docs/guide#computers).

Get an agent's work on your computer:

```bash
git fetch origin <session_id>
git checkout <session_id>
```

- `kortix files branches` lists the branches with commits ahead of and behind the default branch.
- `kortix sessions info <session_id>` shows `branch` and `base_ref`.
- To start a session on another branch, pass `base_ref` when you create it, in `POST /v1/projects/<project_id>/sessions` or in `sessions.create()` of the [SDK](/docs/developers#sessions-from-code). `kortix sessions new` has no flag for it.

When the base branch moves, open the command palette in the session and run **Ask Agent: Sync Branch & Reload**. The agent merges `origin/<base_ref>` into the session branch, resolves conflicts, runs the relevant tests, and commits. Its last command is `kortix sessions reload … --no-repo --force --yes`, which restarts the agent runtime and ends the turn. Send `continue` when the runtime is back.

### Change requests

A change request merges one branch, the head, into another, the base. Kortix runs the merge, the diff and the conflict check as git operations on the project repository. So change requests work on every kind of repository.

```text
open ──merge──▶ merged          (final)
open ──close──▶ closed ──reopen──▶ open
```

#### Open one from your computer

```bash
git push origin my-branch
kortix cr open --head my-branch --title "Add the triage agent" --description "Why and what changed"
```

- The base is the project default branch. `--base <branch>` targets another branch.
- A head with no commits ahead of the base is refused with `CR_HEAD_NOT_AHEAD`. Push first.
- Each later push to the head branch updates the same change request.

#### Open one from a session

An agent follows this contract:

1. Commit on `$KORTIX_BRANCH_NAME`.
2. Push: `git push origin HEAD`.
3. Open: `kortix cr open --title "…" --description "…"`. Inside a sandbox, `--head` and `--session` fill themselves from `$KORTIX_BRANCH_NAME` and `$KORTIX_SESSION_ID`.
4. Report the change request number to the person, and stop.

A change request from a session targets the session's `base_ref`. An agent cannot retarget it: `--base` with another branch returns `400` `CR_BASE_NOT_SESSION_BASE`. A person can open a change request for the same head with `--base`.

#### Review and merge

Review in the web app on the [Review page](#the-review-page), or from a terminal:

```bash
kortix cr ls                     # open change requests
kortix cr diff 3                 # the unified diff
kortix cr merge-preview 3        # can it merge? lists conflicts
kortix cr merge 3
kortix cr request-changes 3 --message "Rename the flag first"
```

A merge runs these steps:

1. Kortix validates `kortix.yaml` on the head branch, with its `imports:`. An invalid manifest stops the merge with `422` `MANIFEST_INVALID`. A head with no manifest passes.
2. Kortix fast-forwards the base when the head is strictly ahead.
3. Otherwise it creates a merge commit, `Merge CR #<n>: <title>`, authored by `Kortix <noreply@kortix.ai>`. `--message` sets another message.
4. A conflict stops the merge with `409` and the list of conflicting files.

A merge can carry the commits of other open change requests on the same base. Example: you merge the top request of a stack, and the requests below it land with it. Kortix marks each of those requests `merged`, with the same merge commit. The response lists them in `also_merged`, and `kortix cr merge` prints `Also merged #<n>`.

After the merge, running sessions on that base pick up the new config, and Kortix syncs the project's connectors from the new `kortix.yaml`. See [Config comes from the base branch](#config-comes-from-the-base-branch).

**Request changes** records the note on the change request and sends it to the session that opened it as a new prompt. A sleeping sandbox wakes.

#### Agents that merge

- An agent that declares `kortix_permissions` merges only when the grant includes `project.gitops.merge`. An agent without `kortix_permissions` cannot merge.
- A session cannot merge a change request it opened unless its grant in `kortix.yaml` includes `project.gitops.merge`. Without it, the merge returns `403` `CR_SELF_MERGE_REFUSED`.
- A merge that changes `agents`, `default_agent` or `triggers` also needs the permission the direct edit needs: `project.agent.write`, or `project.trigger.create`, `.update` or `.delete`. The merging agent cannot grant any permission it does not hold.

A grant that names `project.cr.open` or `project.cr.merge` counts as `project.gitops.push` or `project.gitops.merge`. Write the `gitops` name in new grants.

#### Permissions

| Action | Needs |
| --- | --- |
| List, show, diff, merge preview | Read access to the project. |
| Open | `project.gitops.push` |
| Edit, close, reopen | Write access to the project. An agent also needs `project.gitops.push`. |
| Merge | `project.gitops.merge`, plus the manifest permissions above for an agent. |
| Request changes | `project.review.act` |

#### `kortix cr` commands

`<cr>` is the change request number, such as `3`, or its UUID.

| Command | Does |
| --- | --- |
| `kortix cr ls [--status <s>]` | Lists change requests. `<s>` is `open` (default), `merged`, `closed` or `all`. |
| `kortix cr show <cr>` | Shows one change request. |
| `kortix cr diff <cr>` | Prints the unified diff. |
| `kortix cr open --head <branch> --title <t> [--base <branch>] [--description <t>]` | Opens a change request. Aliases: `--from`, `--into`, `--body`. |
| `kortix cr merge-preview <cr>` | Shows whether it can merge, and the conflicts. |
| `kortix cr merge <cr> [--message <t>]` | Merges it. |
| `kortix cr request-changes <cr> --message <t>` | Sends the note to the agent that opened it. |
| `kortix cr close <cr>` | Closes it without a merge. |
| `kortix cr reopen <cr>` | Reopens a closed one. |
| `kortix cr version-diff --from <head> --into <base>` | Summarizes a merge before you open a change request. |

Each command takes `--project <id>` and `--host <name>`. `ls`, `show`, `diff`, `merge-preview`, `request-changes` and `version-diff` also take `--json`. The REST routes are under `/v1/projects/<project_id>/change-requests` in the [API reference](https://api.kortix.com/v1/docs).

`GET /change-requests` returns one page, newest number first: 50 change requests by default, at most 500 with `limit` (a larger `limit` answers `400`). Pass `next_cursor` back as `cursor` for the next page; it is `null` on the last page. `kortix cr ls` follows it and lists every change request.

#### Errors

| Code | Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `CR_HEAD_NOT_AHEAD` | `422` | The head has no commits ahead of the base. Commit your work on the head branch and push it. |
| `CR_BASE_NOT_SESSION_BASE` | `400` | An agent tried to target a branch other than its session's base. |
| `MANIFEST_INVALID` | `422` | The manifest on the head branch fails validation. The body lists the issues. Run `kortix validate`. |
| (conflict) | `409` | The merge conflicts. The body lists the files. |
| (not open) | `409` | The change request is merged or closed. |
| `CR_SELF_MERGE_REFUSED` | `403` | A session tried to merge its own change request without a merge grant. |
| `CR_GOVERNANCE_UNVERIFIED` | `503` | Kortix could not read the manifest to check an agent's merge. Nothing merged. Retry. |
| (manifest unreadable) | `502` | Kortix could not read the manifest from the head branch. |

### The Review page

The Review page is the project's inbox for everything that waits on a person. Open **Review** in the project sidebar, or go to `/projects/<project_id>/review`.

| Item | Id | Comes from |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Change | `cr:<id>` | A change request. |
| Approval | `call:<id>` | A connector call that a policy holds for approval. See [Connectors](/docs/guide#connectors). |
| Output (`output`), question (`decision`), finished batch (`batch`) | its own id | An agent submits it with `kortix review submit`. |

The lists are **Needs you**, **Waiting** and **Done**. The filters are **All**, **Changes**, **Approvals**, **Outputs**, **Questions** and **Finished**. Keys: `j` and `k` move, `Enter` opens, `a` approves, `e` asks for changes, `d` dismisses, `1` `2` `3` switch lists, `/` searches, `?` shows help.

Each list loads 50 items at a time, newest first. **Load more** shows the next 50. The filters and the search apply to the items loaded so far. The **Review** count in the sidebar, and the dot on a session that waits on you, count every item that needs you.

What a verdict does depends on the item:

| Item | `approve` | `reject` | `changes` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Change | Merges the change request. | Closes it. | Sends the note to the agent that opened it. A note is required. |
| Approval | Lets the call run. | Denies it. | Not available. |

An item an agent submitted takes every verdict: `approve`, `reject`, `changes`, `answer` and `dismiss`.

The Review page shows a verdict only to a role that holds its permissions. Every verdict on a change or on an agent's item needs write access to the project (`project.write`):

| Control | Needs |
| --- | --- |
| **Ship it** (web), **Merge** (mobile) | `project.write` + `project.gitops.merge` |
| **Solve with agent** (web), **Resolve conflicts** (mobile): it replaces the merge on a conflicted change | `project.write` + `project.gitops.merge` |
| Ask for changes, close, dismiss, answer, and every verdict on an agent's item | `project.write` + `project.review.act` |
| **Approve** and **Deny** on a connector approval | A project manager, or the person who launched the session. The API refuses anyone else. |
| **+** on mobile, to open a change request | `project.write` + `project.gitops.push` |

A built-in member holds none of these permissions. It sees changes and agent items without verdicts, and it decides a connector approval only in a session it launched.

The change request dialog on the Files page and on a session's change card follows the same rule. **Apply changes** and **Fix with agent** need `project.write` + `project.gitops.merge`. **Dismiss** and **Reopen** need `project.write`.

| Command | Does |
| --- | --- |
| `kortix review ls [--segment <s>] [--kind <k>]` | Lists items. Segments: `needs_you`, `waiting`, `done`. Kinds: `change`, `approval`, `output`, `decision`, `batch`. |
| `kortix review show <item>` | Shows one item, with a connector call's arguments. |
| `kortix review act <item> <verdict> [--message <t>]` | Decides one item. |
| `kortix review bulk <verdict> <id>…` | Decides several agent items. It skips changes and approvals, which each need their own review. |
| `kortix review submit --kind <k> --title <t>` | Submits an `output`, `decision` or `batch`. Options: `--summary`, `--risk` (`none`, `low`, `medium`, `high`), `--detail <json>`, `--agent`, `--session`. |

Reading needs `project.review.read`, a verdict needs `project.review.act`, and `submit` needs `project.review.submit`. A Member holds read and submit. A Manager holds all three.

The REST routes are under `/v1/projects/<project_id>/review`:

| Route | Returns |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /review/items` | One page of items: **Needs you**, then **Waiting**, then **Done**, newest first within each. Filters: `segment`, `kind`, `session_id`. `limit` is 50 by default; above 200 it answers `400`. Pass `next_cursor` back as `cursor` for the next page; it is `null` on the last page. A walk is not a snapshot: an item whose status changes between two pages can come back on a later page or be missed. `kortix review ls` follows the cursor and lists each item once, with its newest status. |
| `GET /review/items/<item>` | One item by its id, including `cr:<id>` and a pending `call:<id>`. |
| `GET /review/summary` | `{ needs_you_total, needs_you_by_session }`: the counts the sidebar shows. A connector call that you may not decide counts for the people who may. |

### Config comes from the base branch

A session runs the current config release of its base branch: `kortix.yaml` and the files it registers. Those are agent prompts, skills, tools, commands and extensions. It does not run the files in `/workspace`.

- An edit in `/workspace` or on a session branch takes effect only when it lands on the base branch, through a merge or a push.
- Each commit on the base branch is a new config release. A running session converges to it at the start of its next turn. A push or a merge also starts the convergence in the background.
- To apply it at once, select **Reload config** in the session menu, or run `kortix sessions reload <session_id>`. It restarts the agent runtime. `--force` reloads during a turn and ends that turn; it asks first unless you pass `--yes`. `--no-repo` skips the git pull in `/workspace`. `--status` only reports whether the session is behind.
- To test config before a merge, create a session with `base_ref` set to your branch. Its change requests target that branch too.
- Such a session runs the branch's agent behavior: each agent's prompt, model and permission rules, and the skills, tools, commands and extensions it enables. Governance still comes from the default branch: which agents are declared, and their secret, connector and permission grants. A branch cannot widen what an agent may use before it merges. When the project requires declared agents, an agent declared only on the branch is refused with `400` `AGENT_NOT_DECLARED`.

When a release fails to load, the session runs the last release that worked, or else the platform default config. The session header shows **Older config**. Kortix does not retry the failed release until the base branch changes. Fix the config on the base branch: `kortix validate` checks the manifest.

| Limit | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Config release | 32 MiB compressed, 128 MiB uncompressed |
| Project snapshot | 512 MiB compressed. Above it, running sessions stop picking up config changes until a new session starts. |
| `kortix validate` warning | One file of 10 MiB or more, or more than 512 MiB in total |

To keep a file in git but out of the release, mark it `export-ignore` in `.gitattributes`. A directory rule covers everything under it. The release, the `kortix validate` size check and session config downloads leave those files out. The manifest file always ships whole.

```text
fixtures export-ignore
datasets/raw export-ignore
```

### Manage the repository

**Customize → Settings → Git repo** shows the repository, its **Status**, the **Base branch** and the **Manifest file**. It also holds **Work on this locally**, **Use your own Git client** and **People with access**. The base branch is the branch new sessions and change requests start from.

From the CLI, `kortix projects set --branch <branch>` and `--manifest <path>` change the same fields. Both need `project.settings.write`.

#### GitHub connection

`GET /v1/projects/<project_id>/git/connection` returns the connection `state`:

| `state` | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `connected` | Kortix can reach the repository. |
| `reconnect_required` | The Kortix GitHub App installation is missing, unusable, or does not match the repository. Open the `install_url` in the response to install the App again. |
| `unavailable` | Kortix cannot reach the repository for a reason you cannot fix with the App. |
| `not_connected` | The project has no repository. |

#### Repository disconnected

Kortix reaches a GitHub repository through the Kortix GitHub App installed on the repository's owner. When that access goes away, the project is **disconnected**. `GET /v1/projects/<project_id>/git/connection` then returns `state: reconnect_required`, `reason: repository_disconnected`, a `disconnected_reason`, a `message` with the cause and the fix, and an `install_url`.

| `disconnected_reason` | Cause | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `app_uninstalled` | The Kortix GitHub App was uninstalled from the owner, or its connection was removed from your Kortix account. | A GitHub owner of the organization reinstalls the App: **Customize → Settings → Git repo → Reconnect GitHub**. |
| `app_suspended` | An owner suspended the App on GitHub. | A GitHub owner unsuspends it under GitHub **Settings → GitHub Apps**. |
| `repo_not_accessible` | The repository was deleted, renamed, or removed from the App's repository access. | A GitHub owner adds the repository again under GitHub **Settings → GitHub Apps → Kortix → Repository access**, or you replace the repository. |

While a project is disconnected:

- Every API route that reads the repository answers `409` with `code: repository_disconnected`, the `reason`, and the same message. The project detail still loads: its `config.manifest_version.unknown_reason` is `repository_disconnected` and `git_connection.status` is `error`.
- Session create and start fail at once with that `409`. A session that is already running keeps running.
- Scheduled triggers skip each run and record the message as the trigger's last error. They do not retry.
- `git clone`, `fetch` and `push` through the Kortix git origin print the message as `remote: …`.
- The web app shows **GitHub disconnected** in the sidebar, in **Git repo** and on a session that cannot start. `kortix projects info` prints the reason and the reconnect link.

Kortix checks GitHub again at most every 5 minutes. After the fix on GitHub, the project reconnects on the next check. Reconnecting through **Reconnect GitHub** checks at once, and a project that stored the old installation moves to the new one.

#### Replace the repository

Under **Git repo**, select **Change** and enter the new GitHub repository URL. Kortix verifies access through the GitHub App. The new repository needs the manifest file on its default branch.

Stop active sessions, and close or merge open change requests, first. New sessions use the new repository. Existing sessions cannot restart after the change.

The route is `PUT /v1/projects/<project_id>/git/repository`. It needs `project.settings.write` and `project.connector.write`.

#### Give people direct access

A managed repository lives on GitHub under Kortix. To let someone work on it on github.com, add their GitHub username under **People with access**, with **Can edit** or **Can view**. GitHub emails them an invite.

This needs `project.members.manage`, and works on managed repositories only. For a repository you own, manage access on GitHub.

#### A repository on another git host

To let Kortix reach a repository on another git host, store a token with `PUT /v1/projects/<project_id>/git-credential` and the body `{ "token": "…" }`. Kortix keeps it as a platform credential, not as a secret a sandbox can read. It needs `project.connector.write`. GitHub and managed repositories refuse it with `409`.

For a repository on another git host, `kortix sessions new` run in a checkout whose `origin` is that repository creates the session branch. It pushes your current branch with your own git credentials.

#### Browse without a clone

`kortix files` reads the committed repository. It acts on the default branch unless `--ref <branch|tag|sha>` picks another. Every subcommand needs `project.file.read`.

| Command | Does |
| --- | --- |
| `kortix files ls [<path>]` | Lists files. |
| `kortix files cat <path>` | Prints a file. |
| `kortix files search <query> [--content]` | Searches file names, or file contents. |
| `kortix files history <path>` | Lists the commits that changed a file. |
| `kortix files branches` | Lists branches, ahead and behind the default branch. |
| `kortix files commits [--path <p>]` | Lists commits on `--ref`. |
| `kortix files show <sha>` | Shows one commit and its files. |
| `kortix files diff <sha> [--path <p>]` | Prints a commit's patch. |
| `kortix files download -o <file.zip> [--path <p>]` | Downloads the repository or a subtree as a zip. |

For uncommitted files in a running sandbox, use `kortix sessions cp <session_id>:<path> <dst>`. See [Computers](/docs/guide#computers).

#### Delete

`kortix projects rm <project_id>` deletes a project. It needs `project.delete`. Deleting a project deletes its managed repository. A repository you connected yourself is left untouched.

Through the API, `DELETE /v1/projects/<project-id>` answers `{ "ok": true, "deleted": true, "repo_deleted": <bool> }`. `repo_deleted` says whether a managed repository went with it. The response also carries `archived: true` for older clients; it means the same as `deleted`, and there is no restore.

## kortix.yaml reference

`kortix.yaml` is the manifest at the root of a project repository. It defines the project's agents and everything each agent may use. This section lists every key of `kortix_version: 3`.

The smallest useful manifest:

```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://kortix.com/schema/kortix.v3.schema.json
kortix_version: 3
default_agent: assistant

agents:
  assistant:
    description: Answers questions about the project.
    prompt: You answer questions about this project. Read the files before you answer.
    skills: all                              # omitted: no skills
    workspace: { project_repository: true }  # omitted: no repository in /workspace
```

Every other key is optional. This agent may call every tool, and no tool call waits for a person: an omitted `permission` is `allow`.

### File and schema

Put `kortix.yaml` at the root of the project repository. Start it with the schema line, so a YAML editor validates and completes every key:

```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://kortix.com/schema/kortix.v3.schema.json
```

`kortix schema --version 3` prints the same schema. `kortix schema --version 3 --url` prints its URL.

Validate before you push:

```bash
kortix validate                                  # ./kortix.yaml and its imports
kortix validate --file config/kortix.yaml --json
kortix validate --scopes                         # every grantable kortix_permissions entry
```

| Flag | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `--file <path>` | Validate this file instead of `./kortix.yaml`. |
| `--json` | Print a JSON report: `valid`, `path`, `issues`. |
| `--scopes` | Print the grantable `kortix_permissions` entries and the renamed ones, then exit. |
| `--no-dockerfile-lint` | Skip the checks on sandbox template Dockerfiles. |

- `kortix validate` exits `0` with no errors, `1` with one or more errors, and `2` when the file is missing or unreadable.
- `kortix ship` runs the same checks before it commits.
- The change request merge runs the schema check on the merged manifest. An error blocks the merge with `422` `MANIFEST_INVALID`. A warning never blocks.
- An unknown key is an error. This holds at the top level and in agent blocks, commands, extensions, `workspace`, App blocks, trigger `on` and `run`, and imported files. The message names the key that replaces it, when one exists. An unknown key under `env` is a warning.

`kortix validate` and `kortix ship` also check the files around the manifest. Each of these is an error:

- an agent or command `prompt_file` that does not exist;
- a `skills` folder with no `SKILL.md`, or a `SKILL.md` whose frontmatter `name:` is not the skill name;
- a `tools` module or an `extensions.<name>.pi` repository path that does not exist;
- an agent `connectors` or `connectors_required` slug that `connectors` does not declare (`kortix_slack`, `kortix_teams`, `kortix_email` and `computer` always count as declared);
- an agent `secrets` name or a trigger `secret_env` that `env` does not declare.

They warn when the files in Git exceed 512 MiB, or when one file reaches 10 MiB.

<Callout type="warning">
`kortix.yaml` is in Git. Never write a secret value in it. Set values in **Secrets** and connector logins in **Connectors**. Connector `headers` are plain text.
</Callout>

### Example project

A support project: two agents, one skill, one tool, two commands, one extension, one connector with a read-only rule set, and a weekly trigger.

```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://kortix.com/schema/kortix.v3.schema.json
kortix_version: 3
default_agent: support

project:
  name: support-desk
  description: Answers help desk tickets.

env:
  required: [HELPDESK_TOKEN]
  optional: [STRIPE_API_KEY]

sandbox:
  default: python
  templates:
    - slug: python
      name: Python 3.12
      image: python:3.12-slim
      cpu: 2
      memory: 4

# Registries: what the project has. A registry entry gives nothing to any agent.
skills:
  refund-policy: skills/refund-policy

tools:
  lookup_order: tools/lookup_order.ts

commands:
  triage:
    description: Sort a ticket by urgency.
    prompt: "Read ticket $1 and reply with its urgency: low, normal or high."
  weekly-report:
    description: Write the weekly support report.
    prompt_file: commands/weekly-report.md

extensions:
  web-access:
    pi: npm:pi-web-access@0.30.0

connectors:
  - slug: helpdesk
    provider: http
    base_url: https://helpdesk.example.com/api
    auth:
      type: bearer
    policy_sets:
      read-only:
        description: Reads only.
        policies:
          - match: "get_*"
            action: always_run
          - match: "*"
            action: block

policy:
  default_mode: risk
policies:
  - match: "helpdesk.delete_*"
    action: block
approvals:
  ttl: 4h

# Agents: each one enables registered things by name.
agents:
  support:
    description: Answers help desk tickets.
    mode: primary
    prompt_file: agents/support.md
    permission:
      bash:
        "*": allow
        "git push *": ask
    skills: [refund-policy, kortix-cli]
    subagents: [researcher]
    commands: all
    extensions: [web-access]
    connectors: [helpdesk]
    secrets: [HELPDESK_TOKEN]
    kortix_permissions: [project.session.read, project.gitops.push]
    workspace: { project_repository: true }
  researcher:
    description: Looks up an order's history. Never edits files.
    mode: subagent
    prompt: You research orders and report what you find. Never edit files.
    permission:
      edit: deny
    tools: [read, glob, grep, lookup_order, web_search]
    connectors: [helpdesk]
    policy_sets:
      helpdesk: read-only

triggers:
  - slug: weekly-report
    name: Weekly report
    on:
      cron: "0 0 9 * * 1"
      timezone: Europe/Berlin
    run:
      agent: support
      prompt: Write the weekly support report for the last 7 days.
```

The repository also holds `agents/support.md` (the prompt), `skills/refund-policy/SKILL.md` (with `name: refund-policy`), `commands/weekly-report.md` and the tool module:

```ts
// tools/lookup_order.ts
export default {
  description: 'Look up an order by id.',
  parameters: {
    type: 'object',
    properties: { id: { type: 'string', description: 'The order id' } },
    required: ['id'],
  },
  async execute(args: { id: string }) {
    return `order ${args.id}: shipped`;
  },
};
```

`kortix validate` shows what each agent enables. The `researcher` agent omits most grants, so it gets none of them:

```text
✓  kortix.yaml is valid

What each agent enables:
  support  skills=[refund-policy, kortix-cli]  subagents=[researcher]  commands=[all]  extensions=[web-access]  connectors=[helpdesk]  secrets=[HELPDESK_TOKEN]
  researcher  skills=[none (default-deny)]  subagents=[none (default-deny)]  commands=[none (default-deny)]  extensions=[none (default-deny)]  connectors=[helpdesk]  secrets=[none (default-deny)]
```

### Registries and grants

The project registers what exists. Each agent enables what it uses, by name. A registered item reaches no agent until an agent enables it.

| The project declares | An agent enables it with |
| --- | --- |
| `skills`: skill folders | `skills` |
| `tools`: tool modules | `tools` |
| `commands`: slash commands | `commands` |
| `extensions`: agent runtime packages | `extensions` |
| `connectors`: apps and APIs | `connectors`, `policy_sets` |
| `env`: secret names | `secrets` |
| `apps`: Apps | `apps` |
| `agents`: other agents | `subagents` |

- **A grant** is `all` (or `*`), `none` (or `""`), or a list of names. A list that contains `*` means all.
- **Omitted means none.** An omitted `skills`, `subagents`, `commands`, `extensions`, `connectors`, `secrets`, `apps` or `kortix_permissions` grants nothing.
- **Three keys are open by default:** `tools` is `all`, `permission` is `allow`, and `codemode` is `on`.
- **`workspace.project_repository` defaults to `false`.** Set `true` on every agent that reads or changes the repository, runs its scripts, or opens change requests.
- **Names must exist.** A listed skill, command, extension, subagent or tool that is not registered or built in is an error.
- **Built in, with no entry.** The Kortix skills: `kortix-cli`, `kortix-memory` and the other `kortix-*` skills. The Kortix tools: `web_search`, `image_search`, `scrape_webpage`, `memory`, `show`. The built-in tools: `bash`, `read`, `write`, `edit`, `glob`, `grep`, `question`, `task`. An agent loads a Kortix skill only when its `skills` grant names it or is `all`.
- **Only declared agents run.** There are no built-in agents.
- **Extensions** are packages for the agent runtime. A package holds runtime extensions, skills, prompts or themes. The change request merge builds npm packages, and a session never installs them. A session loads its extensions when its runtime starts, so a change reaches a running session at its next start.

{/* generated:kortix-yaml:start */}

{/* Generated by apps/web/scripts/generate-kortix-yaml-doc.mjs from packages/manifest-schema/src/json-schema.ts. Edit the descriptions there, then run the script. */}

### Top-level keys

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `kortix_version` | `3` | required | `3`. A higher version is an error. |
| `imports` | list | — | YAML files or folders whose collections merge into this file. See [imports](#imports). |
| `default_agent` | string | required | The agent a session or trigger runs when it names none. A declared agent that is enabled and whose `mode` is `primary` or `all`. |
| `project` | map | — | `name`: the project's name. A push that changes it renames the project, and a rename in Kortix commits it here. `description`: text for people who read the repository. |
| `env` | map | — | The secret names the project expects. Values live in Secrets, never in this file. See [env keys](#env-keys). |
| `sandbox` | map | the platform image | Sandbox templates and the project's default template. See [sandbox keys](#sandbox-keys). |
| `triggers` | list | — | Schedules, webhooks and App events that start agent work. See [triggers keys](#triggers-keys) and [Automations](/docs/guide#automations). |
| `connectors` | list | — | Apps and APIs agents can call. See [connectors keys](#connectors-keys) and [Connectors](/docs/guide#connectors). |
| `apps` | map | — | App slug → App block. See [apps keys](#apps-keys). |
| `skills` | map | the Kortix skills | Skill name → repository folder. The Kortix skills (`kortix-*`) are always registered. See [skills entries](#skills-entries). |
| `tools` | map | the Kortix tools | Tool name → repository module. The Kortix tools are always registered. See [tools entries](#tools-entries). |
| `commands` | map | — | Slash command name → command. See [commands entries](#commands-entries). |
| `extensions` | map | — | Extension name → package. See [extensions entries](#extensions-entries). |
| `policy` | map | — | The decision for a connector call that no rule matches. See [policy keys](#policy-keys). |
| `policies` | list | — | Connector rules for the whole project. They run before every policy set and base rule. See [policy keys](#policy-keys). |
| `approvals` | map | — | How long a held connector call waits for a decision. See [policy keys](#policy-keys). |
| `agents` | map | required | Agent name → agent block. At least 1 agent, here or in an imported file. A name is lowercase letters, digits, `-` and `_`, at most 128 characters. See [Agent keys](#agent-keys). |

### Agent keys

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `description` | string | — | What the agent does. An agent that may start it as a subagent reads it to choose. |
| `model` | string | the project default | `provider/model`. See [Models](/docs/guide#models). |
| `mode` | `primary`, `subagent`, `all` | `all` | `primary`: people and triggers run it; no agent starts it. `subagent`: only another agent starts it; agent pickers hide it. `all`: both. |
| `variant` | string | — | The model's reasoning level: `minimal`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` or `max`, when the model offers it. |
| `temperature` | number | the model's | Sampling temperature. A model that refuses a custom temperature ignores it. |
| `top_p` | number | the model's | Nucleus sampling. |
| `steps` | integer, 1 or more | no limit | Model requests in one run. The request that reaches the limit gets no tools, so the model answers. |
| `color` | string or `primary`, `secondary`, `accent`, `success`, `warning`, `error`, `info` | — | Accepted. Sessions do not use it. |
| `hidden` | boolean | `false` | `true`: agent pickers do not list the agent. |
| `options` | map | — | Accepted. Sessions do not use it. |
| `permission` | `ask`, `allow`, `deny` or map | `allow` | Which tool calls run, ask a person, or never run. `skill` and `task` belong to `skills` and `subagents`. See [Permission rules](#permission-rules). |
| `prompt` | string | — | The system prompt. Set `prompt` or `prompt_file`, not both. |
| `prompt_file` | string | — | A repository path to a `.md` file. The whole file is the system prompt. |
| `enabled` | boolean | `true` | `false`: no session starts with the agent, and no agent starts it as a subagent. |
| `codemode` | `on`, `only`, `off` | `on` | `on`: the model gets every tool plus `codemode`, which runs one JavaScript script that calls the other tools. `only`: `codemode` alone. `off`: no `codemode`. A script's calls follow the agent's `permission`. |
| `tools` | `all`, `none`, list or map | `all` | The tools the agent may call: built-in tools, Kortix tools and registered tools. A list allows only those. `{ exclude: [bash] }` allows every tool but those. |
| `skills` | grant | `none` | Registered skills and Kortix skills the agent may load. |
| `subagents` | grant | `none` | Agents this agent may start with the `task` tool: declared, enabled, `mode` `subagent` or `all`, and not itself. A subagent cannot start another subagent. |
| `commands` | grant | `none` | Registered commands the agent runs. |
| `extensions` | grant | `none` | Registered extensions that a session of the agent loads. |
| `connectors` | grant | `none` | Connector slugs the agent may call. |
| `connectors_required` | list | — | Connector slugs, a subset of `connectors`. Sessions do not check it. |
| `policy_sets` | map | the base rules | Connector slug → the name of a policy set on that connector. See [Connector rules and approvals](#connector-rules-and-approvals). |
| `secrets` | grant | `none` | Secret names the agent's sandbox receives. |
| `apps` | grant | `none` | App slugs the agent may open when the App's access is restricted or private. |
| `kortix_permissions` | grant | `none` | Project permissions (`project.*`) the agent holds, whoever starts the session. `kortix validate --scopes` prints them. An account permission is an error. See [Agents](/docs/guide#agents). |
| `workspace` | map | — | What new sessions of this agent get in /workspace. Any other key under `workspace` is an error. |
| `workspace.project_repository` | boolean | `false` | `true`: new sessions check out the project repository in `/workspace`, on their own session branch. `false`: no checkout, and repository calls answer `403` `session_repository_access_denied`. Project tools still load. |
| `sandbox` | string | `sandbox.default` | A template slug, or `default` for the platform image. |
| `network_egress` | map | — | Only `{ version: 1, default: deny, rules: [] }`. It validates with a warning: Kortix does not enforce it. |

### Registry entries

#### skills entries

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `skills.<name>` | string | — | The repository folder that holds the skill's `SKILL.md`. The folder's last segment is the name: `skills/pdf` for `pdf`. The `SKILL.md` frontmatter has `name: <name>`. |
| `<name>` | string | — | Lowercase letters and digits in words joined by `-`, at most 64 characters. A name that starts with `kortix-` is an error. |

#### tools entries

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `tools.<name>` | string | — | A repository path to a `.ts`, `.js`, `.mts`, `.mjs`, `.cts` or `.cjs` module. The module default-exports `{ description, parameters, execute(args, context) }`. `parameters` is a JSON Schema object. `execute` returns a string or a JSON value. |
| `<name>` | string | — | snake_case: a lowercase letter, then letters, digits or `_`, at most 64 characters. A built-in tool name or a `pty_` name is an error. A Kortix tool's name replaces that Kortix tool. `kortix tools eject <name>` copies a Kortix tool into `tools/<name>.ts` and writes its entry. |

#### commands entries

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `commands.<name>` | map | — | One slash command. An agent runs it only when its `commands` grant names it. Any other agent gets `403` `{"error": "command_not_enabled", "command": "<name>", "agent": "<agent>"}`. |
| `<name>` | string | — | Lowercase letters and digits in words joined by `-`, at most 64 characters. In the `/` menu, a command hides a skill with the same name. |
| `commands.<name>.description` | string | — | Shown in the `/` menu. |
| `commands.<name>.prompt` | string | — | The template. `$ARGUMENTS` takes the text after the command. `$1`, `$2` and on take the words in order, and the highest one takes the rest. A template with neither gets the arguments appended. Set `prompt` or `prompt_file`, not both. |
| `commands.<name>.prompt_file` | string | — | A repository path to a `.md` file. The whole file is the template, frontmatter included. |

#### extensions entries

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `extensions.<name>` | map | — | One package: runtime extensions, skills, prompts or themes for the agent runtime. |
| `<name>` | string | — | Lowercase letters and digits in words joined by `-`, at most 64 characters. |
| `extensions.<name>.pi` | string or map | — | `npm:<package>@<x.y.z>` with an exact version, or a repository path such as `extensions/audit.ts` (no leading `./`), or `{ source, extensions, skills, prompts, themes }`. A version range is an error. Set it on every extension: one without it loads in no new session. |
| `extensions.<name>.pi.source` | string | — | The npm pin or the repository path. Required in the map form. |
| `extensions.<name>.pi.extensions` | list | every one | Load only the package's extension files that match these globs. `[]` loads none. |
| `extensions.<name>.pi.skills` | list | every one | Load only the package's skill files that match these globs. `[]` loads none. |
| `extensions.<name>.pi.prompts` | list | every one | Load only the package's prompt files that match these globs. `[]` loads none. |
| `extensions.<name>.pi.themes` | list | every one | Load only the package's theme files that match these globs. `[]` loads none. |

### Section keys

#### env keys

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `env.required` | list | — | Secret names the project needs: uppercase letters, digits and `_`, not starting with a digit. The dashboard and `kortix ship` ask for a missing value. A missing value does not block a session. |
| `env.optional` | list | — | Secret names the same prompts offer. Values live in Secrets, never in this file. |

#### sandbox keys

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `sandbox.templates` | list | — | Named sandbox images. |
| `sandbox.templates[].slug` | string | required | Unique. Lowercase letters, digits, `-` and `_`, at most 64 characters. `default` is reserved. |
| `sandbox.templates[].name` | string | the slug | The label in the template picker. |
| `sandbox.templates[].entrypoint` | string | the image's | Overrides the container entrypoint. |
| `sandbox.templates[].image` | string | — | An image with a tag or a digest. No tag is an error. `:latest` is a warning. Set `image` or `dockerfile`, not both. |
| `sandbox.templates[].dockerfile` | string | — | A repository path to a Dockerfile. `kortix validate` lints it: no `COPY` from the repository, no `RUN` heredoc, a Debian-family base image. |
| `sandbox.templates[].cpu` | integer, 1 or more | the platform default | vCPU cores. Above 32 is a warning, and Kortix uses 32. |
| `sandbox.templates[].memory` | integer, 1 or more | the platform default | GiB of memory. Above 128 is a warning, and Kortix uses 128. |
| `sandbox.templates[].disk` | integer, 1 or more | the platform default | GiB of disk. Above 500 is a warning, and Kortix uses 500. |
| `sandbox.templates[].container_runtime` | boolean | `false` | `true`: Docker runs inside the sandbox. |
| `sandbox.default` | string | `default` | A slug from `sandbox.templates`, or `default` for the platform image. |

#### triggers keys

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `slug` | string | required | Unique. Lowercase letters, digits, `-` and `_`, at most 128 characters. |
| `name` | string | the slug | The label in the dashboard. |
| `enabled` | boolean | `true` | `false`: the trigger does not fire. |
| `filter` | map | — | Payload path → a value (equals), or one operator: `not`, `in`, `exists`, `prefix`, `not_prefix`. A fire runs only when every path matches. |
| `max_runs_per_hour` | integer, 1 or more | `60` | Runs the trigger starts in any 60 minutes. |
| `max_runs_per_day` | integer, 1 or more | — | Runs the trigger starts in a UTC day. |
| `max_lateness` | string | — | Schedules only, `60s` or more. A missed fire later than this does not run. |
| `overlap` | `skip`, `allow` | `skip` for a schedule, `allow` for others | `skip` holds a fire while the previous run is active. Only with `run.session: fresh`. |
| `on` | map | required | What fires the trigger: a schedule (`cron`, `every` or `at`), a webhook, or an App event. |
| `on.cron` | string | — | A 6-field cron expression, seconds first: `"0 0 9 * * 1"` is 09:00 every Monday. A trigger fires at most once every 60 seconds. |
| `on.timezone` | string | `UTC` | An IANA time zone for `cron` and `at`. |
| `on.every` | string | — | A fixed period: an integer and `s`, `m`, `h` or `d`, at least `60s`. |
| `on.at` | string | — | One fire at an ISO-8601 time, such as `2026-06-01T09:00:00Z`. |
| `on.webhook` | map | — | Fire on a signed request to the trigger's webhook URL. |
| `on.webhook.secret` | string | required | The name of the secret that signs requests to the trigger's webhook. |
| `on.event` | string | — | With `on.app`: the App event name, or `"*"` for every event. |
| `on.app` | string | — | The App's slug. The trigger fires on that App's events. |
| `run` | map | required | What a fire runs: an agent turn, or a call to an App. |
| `run.agent` | string | `default_agent` | A declared agent. |
| `run.prompt` | string | — | The first message of the run. Required unless `run.app` is set. |
| `run.session` | `fresh`, `reuse`, `pinned`, `keyed` | `fresh` | `fresh`: a new session for each fire. `reuse`: the last session this trigger created, or a new one when none can continue. `pinned`: the session `run.session_id`. `keyed`: one session for each value of `run.session_key`. |
| `run.session_key` | string | — | With `keyed`: a template over the payload, such as `"{{ body.chat_id }}"`. A key that renders empty runs `fresh`. |
| `run.session_id` | string | — | The session to run in. Required with `pinned`. |
| `run.model` | string | the agent's model | `provider/model`. |
| `run.app` | string | — | The fire calls this App instead of an agent. |
| `run.path` | string | — | With `run.app`: the request path. Starts with `/`. |
| `run.method` | `POST`, `GET` | — | With `run.app`: the request method. |
| `run.payload` | map | — | With `run.app`: the request body. |
| `type` | `cron`, `webhook`, `app` | — | Required in the flat form: the trigger without `on` and `run`. `type` next to `on` is an error. |
| `agent` | string | — | Flat form of `run.agent`. |
| `session_mode` | `fresh`, `reuse`, `pinned`, `keyed` | — | Flat form of `run.session`. |
| `session_key` | string | — | Flat form of `run.session_key`. |
| `session_id` | string | — | Flat form of `run.session_id`. |
| `prompt` | string | — | Flat form of `run.prompt`. |
| `model` | string | — | Flat form of `run.model`. |
| `cron` | string | — | Flat form of `on.cron`, with `type: cron`. |
| `run_at` | string | — | Flat form of `on.at`, with `type: cron`. |
| `timezone` | string | — | Flat form of `on.timezone`. |
| `secret_env` | string | — | Flat form of `on.webhook.secret`, with `type: webhook`. |
| `app` | string | — | Flat form of `on.app`, with `type: app`. |
| `target` | map | — | Flat form of `run.app`, `run.path`, `run.method` and `run.payload`: `{ app, path, method, payload }`. |
| `every` | string | — | Flat form of `on.every`, with `type: cron`. |

#### connectors keys

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `slug` | string | required | Unique. Lowercase letters, digits, `-` and `_`, at most 128 characters. `kortix_slack`, `kortix_teams`, `kortix_email` and `computer` are reserved for the built-in connectors. |
| `name` | string | the slug | The label. |
| `enabled` | boolean | `true` | `false`: the connector is off. |
| `provider` | `pipedream`, `composio`, `mcp`, `openapi`, `postman`, `graphql`, `http`, `channel` | required | How Kortix reaches the app or API. Kortix writes the `channel` entry when you connect a channel. |
| `app` | string | — | `composio` and `pipedream`: the app. Required for them. |
| `url` | string | — | `mcp`: the server URL. Required. |
| `endpoint` | string | — | `graphql`: the endpoint. Required. |
| `base_url` | string | — | `http`: the base URL. Required. |
| `transport` | `http`, `sse` | `http` | `mcp` only. |
| `spec` | string | — | `openapi` and `postman`: the spec URL or repository path. Without it the connector does not load. |
| `authorization_strategy` | `project`, `user` | `project` | `project`: the connector's accounts belong to the project. `user`: each person connects their own account. |
| `sensitive` | boolean | `false` | `true`: a call that no rule matches needs approval, reads included. |
| `auth` | map | — | How Kortix sends the credential. The credential lives in Kortix, never in this file. |
| `auth.type` | `bearer`, `basic`, `custom`, `api_key`, `oauth1`, `hmac`, `aws_sigv4`, `mtls`, `none` | required | `oauth1` works on `openapi`, `postman` and `http` only. |
| `auth.in` | `header`, `query`, `cookie` | `header` | Where the credential goes. |
| `auth.name` | string | — | The header, query or cookie name. Required for `custom` and `api_key`. |
| `auth.prefix` | string | — | Text before the credential value, such as `Bearer`. |
| `headers` | map | — | Static request headers, at most 32. The values are plain text in Git. |
| `policies` | list | — | The base rules: `{ match, action, conditions }`. |
| `policy_sets` | map | — | Set name → `{ description, policies }`: a rule set an agent can be given with `agents.<name>.policy_sets`. A set name is lowercase letters, digits, `-` and `_`, at most 64 characters, and not `base`. At most 32 sets of 200 rules each. Not on `channel` connectors. |

#### policy keys

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `policy.default_mode` | `allow_all`, `risk` | `allow_all` | `allow_all`: the call runs. `risk`: a read runs, and a write or a delete waits for approval. |
| `policies[].match` | string | — | Required. A glob over `<connector-slug>.<tool>`, such as `gmail.send_*` or `*.delete*`. Case-insensitive. |
| `policies[].action` | `always_run`, `require_approval`, `block` | — | Required. What a matching call does. |
| `policies[].conditions` | list | — | Every condition must hold. Each is `{ arg, match, negate }`: `arg` is a dot path into the call's arguments, `match` is a glob or a `/regex/`, and `negate: true` inverts it. |
| `approvals.ttl` | string | `24h` | An integer and `m`, `h` or `d`, such as `30m`, `4h` or `7d`. Kortix clamps it to 5 minutes through 30 days. |

#### apps keys

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `apps.<slug>` | map | — | One App's deploy settings. Any other key is an error. Its keys: `path`, `type`, `image`, `dockerfile`, `command`, `port`, `root`, `output_dir`, `install_command`, `build_command`, `spa`, `readiness_path`, `idle_timeout_seconds`, `always_on`, `uses`, `resources`, `env`, `secrets`, `processes`, `http_service`, `services`, `mounts`, `vm`, `deploy`, `labels`, `display`. Full reference: [Configure in kortix.yaml](/docs/developers#configure-in-kortixyaml). |

{/* generated:kortix-yaml:end */}

### Permission rules

`agents.<name>.permission` decides which tool calls run, wait for a person, or never run. What a person sees when an agent asks is on [Agents](/docs/guide#how-an-agent-asks-before-risky-actions).

```yaml
permission:
  bash:
    "*": allow
    "git push *": ask
    "rm -rf *": deny
  edit:
    "*": allow
    "secrets/*": deny
  external_directory: deny
  webfetch: ask
```

- **Value.** One action for every tool: `allow`, `ask` or `deny`. Or a map of key → action, or of key → map of glob → action.
- **Keys.** A capability: `read`, `edit`, `glob`, `grep`, `bash`, `external_directory`, `question`, `webfetch`, `websearch`. Or a tool's own name, which wins over its capability.
- **Tools that follow a capability.** `write` follows `edit`. `memory` follows `edit`, or `read` when it only views. `web_search` and `image_search` follow `websearch`. `scrape_webpage` follows `webfetch`.
- **Action only.** `question`, `webfetch` and `websearch` take an action, not a glob map.
- **`bash` globs** match each command of a line. In `a; b`, `a | b` or `$(b)`, the strictest answer wins: `deny`, then `ask`, then `allow`.
- **Path globs** match the file, relative to `/workspace`. A path outside `/workspace` also answers to `external_directory`. A path rule does not govern `bash`.
- **Precedence.** The most specific matching rule wins, whatever the key order. The longest pattern is the most specific.
- `permission.skill` and `permission.task` are errors. Use `skills` and `subagents`.

### Connector rules and approvals

Kortix decides each connector call in this order. In each list, the first rule that matches decides.

1. The project `policies`.
2. The calling agent's policy set on that connector (`agents.<name>.policy_sets`). A set the connector does not define blocks every call of that agent on it.
3. The connector's own `policies`, the base rules.
4. No rule matched: a `sensitive` connector holds the call for approval. Otherwise `policy.default_mode` decides.

A set or a base rule cannot loosen a project rule. A set can loosen a base rule for its agent.

```yaml
policy:
  default_mode: risk
policies:
  - match: gmail.send_email
    action: require_approval
    conditions:
      - arg: to
        match: "*@example.com"     # a glob, or /regex/ in slashes
  - match: gmail.send_email
    action: block
approvals:
  ttl: 4h
```

- An agent `policy_sets` entry that names a set the connector does not define is an error. An entry for a connector the agent is not granted, or that `connectors` does not declare, is a warning: it does nothing.
- `kortix validate` does not check `policy` or `policies`. When Kortix syncs connectors, it skips a malformed rule and reports it as a connector sync error.
- A held call that nobody decides within `approvals.ttl` expires. Approving one is on [Connectors](/docs/guide#approving-a-held-action).

### Sandbox templates

A session picks its sandbox template in this order:

1. The template the session request names.
2. The `sandbox` of the agent the session runs.
3. `sandbox.default`.
4. The platform image.

A hardware value below 1 is an error. A value above the maximum is a warning, and Kortix clamps it. `gpu` is a warning and does nothing. An image key set directly on `sandbox` (`image`, `dockerfile`, `slug`, `cpu`, `memory`, `disk`, `entrypoint`, `context`, `context_dir`, `gpu`) is an error: put it in a template.

What each place on the sandbox keeps:

- `/workspace` holds this session's files, kept for the life of the session.
- `/drives` (Drive) holds your files and the company's files, shared across sessions and kept until deleted.
- Anything else (home directory, `/tmp`, installed packages) can be gone when the session reopens. Put tools in a sandbox template.

### imports

`imports` splits the manifest across several YAML files. Kortix merges them first. Validation, triggers, grants and connector sync all read the merged manifest.

```yaml
# kortix.yaml
kortix_version: 3
default_agent: assistant
imports:
  - triggers/          # every .yaml and .yml file below this folder
agents:
  assistant:
    prompt: You write reports.
```

```yaml
# triggers/reports/weekly.yaml
triggers:
  - slug: weekly-report
    on:
      cron: "0 0 15 * * 0"
    run:
      agent: assistant
      prompt: Build the weekly report.
```

1. A path is relative to the repository root. `..`, an absolute path and a glob are errors. A path that matches no `.yaml` or `.yml` file is an error.
2. A folder import takes every `.yaml` and `.yml` file below it, at any depth, sorted by path.
3. An imported file holds only `agents`, `apps`, `commands`, `connectors`, `extensions`, `skills`, `tools`, `triggers` and `imports`. Any other key in it is an error.
4. Imports nest up to 8 levels and 200 files. A cycle is an error. A file reached twice merges once.
5. One name, one file. A trigger slug, connector slug, agent name, App slug, skill, tool, command or extension declared in two files is an error that names both files.
6. Entries merge in order: the importing file first, then its imports as listed.
7. A Kortix edit to an entry rewrites the file that declares it. A new entry goes to `kortix.yaml`.

A broken import fails the whole manifest:

```text
error imports: triggers "weekly-report" is declared in both triggers/a.yaml and triggers/b.yaml — one name, one file
```

### Migrate to v3

A project created in Kortix starts on `kortix_version: 3`. Projects on `kortix_version: 1` (`kortix.toml`) or `kortix_version: 2` keep running unchanged. Upgrading is opt-in, and it lands as a change request.

**From Kortix.** Select the upgrade alert in the project sidebar. Or open **Customize → Settings → Upgrades** and select **Run** on **Upgrade manifest to v3 (kortix.yaml)**. The alert shows only to people who can write to the project. Or run:

```bash
kortix projects upgrade [<id>]
```

Each one starts a session with the project's default agent. The agent converts the manifest with `kortix migrate`, runs `kortix validate` and opens a change request. It never merges it: a person reviews and merges it. Starting the upgrade needs permission to start sessions in the project.

**By hand.** In a checkout of a v1 or v2 project:

```bash
kortix migrate            # preview: the plan and the new kortix.yaml; nothing changes
kortix migrate --write    # apply it
kortix validate
```

`kortix migrate` does this:

- From v1, each `[[agents]]` entry and each agent `.md` becomes an agent. A grant v1 omitted is written as `all`, because v1 meant all. `env` becomes `secrets`. `default_agent` comes from the old harness config, else the first agent. `kortix.toml` is deleted.
- It moves each agent's `.md` frontmatter into its block. The `.md` keeps the prompt alone and becomes `prompt_file`.
- It copies the project-wide `permission` of the old harness config onto every agent that sets none, and deletes that config file.
- It turns `permission.task` into `subagents`.
- It turns the `skills/<name>/` folders into the `skills` registry.
- It turns the harness folder's command files into the `commands` registry. Each command is enabled on the agent its frontmatter names, else on `default_agent`.
- It turns plugins and `harnesses.pi.packages` into `extensions`, enabled on every agent that loaded them, and `harnesses.pi.codemode` into `codemode`.
- It sets `workspace: { project_repository: true }` on every agent that had the repository, because v3 defaults to `false`.
- It keeps a split manifest split. Each entry stays in its file, and the new registries go to `kortix.yaml`.
