```yaml
    prompt: Write the weekly report from the reports App.
    kortix_permissions: [project.app.read]
    apps: [reports]    # App slugs, all, or none (the default)
```

Set the list in `kortix.yaml`, with `kortix agents scope report-writer --apps reports`
(also `all` and `none`), or in **Customize → Agents →** the agent **→ Apps**.
The App's access dialog lists the agents granted to it. From code, call `apps.access.agents(appId)`. See
[Agents](/docs/guide#agents).

A script, a CI job or a service account calls a non-public App with a personal access
key (`kortix_pat_`) or a service account key (`kortix_sa_`). Send it in
`Authorization: Bearer` or `X-Kortix-App-Authorization: Bearer`. The gate applies the same access decision
as for a signed-in browser, within the token's project binding. A `password` App
never accepts a Kortix credential.

An `openapi` or `http` connector whose base URL is an App of the same project
needs no Kortix credential. The connector gateway adds a signed, 60-second
assertion for the calling session. See [Connectors](/docs/guide#connectors).

### Triggers and App events

An App can start agents, and triggers can call it.

| You want | Use |
|---|---|
| An agent to run when the App sees something | The App posts an App event to `$KORTIX_APP_EVENTS_URL` with `Authorization: Bearer $KORTIX_APP_TOKEN`. A trigger with `on: { event, app }` picks the agent. See [App events](/docs/guide#automations). |
| The App called on a schedule or an event | A trigger with `run: { app, path, method, payload }`. Kortix wakes an on-demand App and sends the request. No session, no model. See [Call an App](/docs/guide#automations). |
| The App to call itself later | `POST /v1/projects/<project_id>/apps/<app_id>/runs` with the App token: `run_after` or `run_at`. See [Call an App](/docs/guide#automations). |

To react to the outside world, try a webhook first, then a schedule. Post App
events only when neither works. An App never starts an agent itself: it posts
events, and a trigger decides. Deploy a watcher App unlisted: it belongs in the
Background list.

App events and runs are REST only. They authenticate with the App token, not
a Kortix token.

### Environment inside an App

Kortix sets these variables in every machine:

| Variable | Value |
|---|---|
| `KORTIX_APP_SLUG` | The App's slug |
| `KORTIX_APP_URL` | The URL of the primary service |
| `KORTIX_SERVICE_URL_<NAME>` | The URL of each other HTTP service (`-` becomes `_`) |
| `KORTIX_USES_URL_<SLUG>` | The URL of each App this App uses (`-` becomes `_`) |
| `KORTIX_MACHINE_ID` | The machine's id, stable across releases |
| `KORTIX_PROCESS_GROUP` | The machine's process group |
| `KORTIX_AUTH_ISSUER`, `KORTIX_AUTH_AUDIENCE`, `KORTIX_AUTH_JWKS` | The values that verify the App's sign-in tokens |
| `KORTIX_APP_VIEWER_SECRET` | The key that verifies the signed viewer header |
| `KORTIX_APP_ACCOUNT_ID` | The account that owns the App |
| `KORTIX_APP_ID` | The App's id |
| `KORTIX_APP_TOKEN` | The App's token for [App events](/docs/guide#automations) and scheduled runs. A secret. |
| `KORTIX_APP_EVENTS_URL` | Where the App posts App events |

Your own values come from `env` and `secrets` in the `kortix.yaml` block:

- At most 128 `env` values and 128 `secrets` mappings. A value is at most
  32 KiB.
- A name is uppercase letters, digits and `_` (`^[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*$`), at most
  128 characters.
- `kortix validate` and the deploy refuse reserved names and say why. Reserved:
  every name that starts with `KORTIX_`; `PORT`, `PATH`, `HOME`, `PWD`, `USER`,
  `LOGNAME`, `SHELL`, `HOSTNAME`, `TERM`, `TMPDIR`, `NODE_ENV`, `NODE_OPTIONS`,
  `LD_PRELOAD`, `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`; and `SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET`,
  `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN`.
- A name cannot be in both `env` and `secrets`.
- `secrets` maps a runtime name to a project secret identifier. The secret must
  have the `environment` exposure. Kortix resolves it when it creates the
  machine and never writes it into the image. See [Security](/docs/guide#security).

### URLs and what visitors see

Kortix assigns the hostname when it creates the App and never changes it. On
Kortix Cloud it is `https://<env>-<slug>-<route-key>.apps.kortix.com`, where
`<route-key>` is 16 hex characters. Another service answers on the same label
plus `--<service>`.

A service URL is `null` when the label would pass 63
characters: reach that service through a binding. A self-hosted instance serves
Apps on its Apps base domain. See [Self-hosting](/docs/developers#app-hosting).

A static App serves files at once:

- HTML revalidates on every load (`no-cache`).
- Hashed build output (`_next/static/`, and hashed files under `assets/` or
  `static/js|css|media/`) is cached for a year. Every other file revalidates.
- With SPA fallback on, a page navigation to an unknown path gets
  `index.html`. A missing file gets your `404.html` with status `404`.
- `/docs` redirects `308` to `/docs/` when `docs/index.html` exists.
- Responses are `private` to shared caches unless the App is `public`.

An authorized request to a stopped server App wakes it, waits for readiness and
then serves that same request. While the App deploys or wakes, a browser gets a
status page with HTTP `202`, `retry-after: 3` and a 3-second refresh. Other
clients get `202` and JSON with a `code` that names the phase, for example
`app_deployment_building` or `app_starting`. Retry after 3 seconds.

| State | HTTP | `code` |
|---|---|---|
| Last deployment failed | 503 | `app_deployment_failed` |
| Last deployment cancelled | 503 | `app_deployment_cancelled` |

Account limits answer `402` or `429`: see
[Run mode, machines and scale](#run-mode-machines-and-scale).

### Releases, rollback and deletion

```bash
kortix apps releases my-site            # vN, status, strategy; marks the live one
kortix apps show my-site                # capabilities, links, run mode, releases
kortix apps logs my-site [deployment]   # runtime logs; --after N --limit N
kortix apps rollback my-site v3         # a new release from v3; waits unless --no-wait
```

- A rollback creates a new release from the target's artifact and config, and
  rolls it out like a deploy. Volumes keep their data. A target that is not
  `ready` answers `409 release_not_ready`.
- An App keeps its live release and the 5 newest other ready releases as
  rollback targets. Kortix retires older ones after each deploy. A self-hosted
  operator can change the number: see [Self-hosting](/docs/developers#app-hosting).
- Runtime log lines carry a `source`: `app` (your process), `appd` (the App
  supervisor) or `caddy` (the access log, which never holds request headers).
- In the web app, open the App, then a release, to read its events and build
  log.

Delete one release, or the whole App:

```bash
kortix apps delete my-site --deployment v1 --yes   # one release and its image
kortix apps delete my-site --yes                   # the App, its machines and images
```

- The live release cannot be deleted (`409 deployment_live`). Roll back first.
- A release that is still building cannot be deleted
  (`409 deployment_in_progress`).
- A build that hits the provider's image limit fails with
  `app_image_quota_exceeded`. Delete releases you do not need.
- Deleting an App needs `project.app.write`. An App with volumes also needs
  `project.app.admin`. Each volume keeps a `final` snapshot for 7 days, then
  Kortix purges it. Export data you need first.

### Template: Convex backend

The `convex` template deploys a self-hosted Convex backend with Kortix sign-in:
a database, server functions, realtime queries, file storage, schedules and
search. It is an ordinary server App with a volume.

```bash
kortix apps launch --template convex   # --no-deploy writes kortix.yaml only
```

Run it in the directory that holds your `kortix.yaml`. Without one, `launch`
creates it. A slug that is already in the file is refused: pick another
`--name`.

`launch` writes two blocks into `kortix.yaml` and the dashboard's two files,
`apps/backend/dashboard/Dockerfile` and `apps/backend/dashboard/start.js` (a file that
already exists is kept). It deploys `backend`, then writes the backend's admin key to
the project secret `BACKEND_ADMIN_KEY` (the slug in capitals, `-` becomes `_`). It
reads the key from the machine and never prints it.

The key belongs to the
backend that this launch created, so every launch writes it again. Then it deploys
`backend-dashboard`.

There are two Apps because Convex ships the backend and the dashboard as two
images, and a Kortix App runs one image. The **Apps** tab shows them as one:
the dashboard is the backend's **Dashboard** tab. The backend runs 24/7 with its
volume; the dashboard sleeps when idle.

| App | What it is |
|---|---|
| `backend` | The Convex backend, image pinned by digest. Service `http` on port 3210 (`access: public`, a `/version` check): the client URL, the sync WebSocket and the admin API. Service `site` on port 3211 (`access: public`): HTTP actions. Volume `data` at `/convex/data`, 10 GB. Always on, 1 vCPU / 1 GB / 10 GB disk. Label `backend`. `display: { preview: none }`. |
| `backend-dashboard` | The Convex dashboard, built from `apps/backend/dashboard/` (`type: dockerfile`). `access: admins`, on demand. It uses `backend` and reaches it through `/_kortix/apps/backend`. `display: { parent: backend }` makes it the **Dashboard** tab of `backend`. `secrets: { NEXT_PUBLIC_ADMIN_KEY: BACKEND_ADMIN_KEY }` signs it in. |

`start.js` signs the dashboard in with the admin key. The dashboard is
`access: admins`, so only people who hold `project.app.admin` reach it.

Both backend services are `public` because Convex checks sign-in in each
function. Keep the backend at 1 machine: a second machine gets its own empty
volume, which makes a second, separate database.

Connect and push your Convex code, for example from `apps/backend/`. A session has
`BACKEND_ADMIN_KEY` in its environment:

```bash
cd apps/backend
npm install convex@1.46.0 @kortix/sdk
export CONVEX_SELF_HOSTED_URL=<backend App URL> CONVEX_SELF_HOSTED_ADMIN_KEY="$BACKEND_ADMIN_KEY"
kortix apps auth-env backend | while IFS='=' read -r k v; do printf '%s' "$v" | npx convex env set "$k"; done
npx convex deploy
```

Use Convex CLI `1.46.0`: it matches the pinned backend.

<Callout type="warning">
The admin key controls the backend's code and data. Grant `BACKEND_ADMIN_KEY` only to
agents that deploy the backend. Never commit the key or ship it in a web App.
</Callout>

Kortix sign-in for the functions:

```ts
// convex/auth.config.ts
export default {
  providers: [{
    type: 'customJwt',
    issuer: process.env.KORTIX_AUTH_ISSUER!,
    applicationID: process.env.KORTIX_AUTH_AUDIENCE!,
    jwks: process.env.KORTIX_AUTH_JWKS!,
    algorithm: 'ES256',
  }],
};
```

```ts
// convex/members.ts
import { query } from './_generated/server';
import { readKortixMember, requireKortixMember } from '@kortix/sdk';

export const me = query({
  args: {},
  handler: async (ctx) => readKortixMember(await ctx.auth.getUserIdentity()),   // null when signed out
});

// In every function that touches non-public data:
// const member = requireKortixMember(await ctx.auth.getUserIdentity(), { roles: ['owner', 'admin'] });
```

In React, read the member with `useQuery(api.members.me)`. Do not call
`useConvexAuth()`: it throws under a plain `ConvexProvider`.

- **Dashboard.** Open **Apps** → `backend` → **Dashboard** (project managers). It opens
  signed in. `kortix apps access-link backend-dashboard` prints a 5-minute link to
  the same page.
- **Before a migration, a bulk import or a backfill,** take
  `kortix apps volumes snapshot backend data`. Restore with
  `kortix apps volumes restore backend data <snapshot>`: the live data returns to the
  snapshot.
- **Keep your own copy** with `npx convex export --path backup.zip --include-file-storage`.
- **From outside**, `kortix apps connect backend` prints how to call the backend:
  its URLs and snippets for a sign-in token, an HTTP call and your own server.
  It prints no secret.

### Build an internal tool

An internal tool is a Convex backend App for data and logic, plus a static App
for the UI. Kortix signs the team in: the UI knows who is looking, and every
function knows who calls it.

```text
apps/backend/convex/        Convex functions: schema.ts, auth.config.ts, members.ts, ...
apps/crm/              Vite + React UI: src/convex.ts wires the binding
apps/backend/dashboard/     the dashboard App: Dockerfile + start.js (the template)
kortix.yaml            apps.backend and apps.backend-dashboard (the template), apps.crm
```

<Steps>
<Step title="Launch the backend">
`kortix apps launch --template convex`.
</Step>
<Step title="Write the functions">
Write the schema and functions in `apps/backend/convex/`, with `auth.config.ts` from the template section. Call `requireKortixMember` first in every public query and mutation. Push with `npx convex deploy`.
</Step>
<Step title="Wire the UI to the backend">
Connect through the binding:

```ts
// apps/crm/src/convex.ts
import { ConvexReactClient } from 'convex/react';
import { kortixBinding } from '@kortix/sdk';

const backend = kortixBinding('backend');
export const convex = new ConvexReactClient(backend.url);
convex.setAuth(backend.token);
```
</Step>
<Step title="Declare the UI App">

```yaml
apps:
  crm:
    path: apps/crm/dist
    type: static
    spa: true
    uses: [backend]
```
</Step>
<Step title="Build, deploy and open it to the project">

```bash
(cd apps/crm && npm run build)
kortix apps deploy apps/crm/dist
kortix apps access crm --mode project
```
</Step>
</Steps>

Never use `public` for internal data. A `public` App has no signed-in member, so
every function that requires one refuses the call.

[Kortix UI](/docs/developers#kortix-ui) (`@kortix/ui`) is the component library for App UIs.
Install it from npm with `npm i @kortix/ui @kortix/sdk`, the same version of
each. Never copy component source into the App.

A Vite App without Tailwind imports
`@kortix/ui/styles.css` once in `src/main.tsx`. A Tailwind v4 App imports
`@import 'tailwindcss'; @import '@kortix/ui/tailwind.css';` in its main CSS.
Compose `Card`, `Table`, `Modal`, `Badge` and `InfoBanner` before you write a
component, and re-theme by CSS variables, never by a copied component. A chat
view of a session is `SessionThread` from `@kortix/ui/session`.

| What broke | Undo |
|---|---|
| The UI | `kortix apps rollback crm <vN>` |
| The backend's image or config | `kortix apps rollback backend <vN>`. The volume keeps its data. |
| Convex code | Check out the last good `apps/backend/convex` from git and run `npx convex deploy` again. |
| Data | `kortix apps volumes restore backend data <snapshot>`. Every later change is lost. |

### Build on Apps from TypeScript

`kortix.project(projectId).apps` in `@kortix/sdk` exposes every App operation.
The CLI uses the same calls.

Branch on an App's `capabilities`, never on how the App was made:

| Capability | The App has it when |
|---|---|
| `deployments`, `rollback`, `preview`, `member_tokens` | Always |
| `static` | The live release is static |
| `sleep`, `machines`, `scale`, `exec` | The live release runs a server |
| `volumes`, `snapshots` | The App has a volume |

A call that needs a capability the App lacks answers `409`:

```json
{ "error": "This App does not support exec.", "code": "app_capability_unsupported", "capability": "exec" }
```

The CLI checks first: it exits `1` with `App <slug> does not support <capability>.`
and sends nothing. The machine and volume routes of an App with no machines
answer `409 app_no_machines`.

```ts
import { createKortix } from '@kortix/sdk';

const kortix = createKortix({ backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1', getToken: async () => process.env.KORTIX_TOKEN! });
const apps = kortix.project(projectId).apps;

const app = await apps.create({ slug: 'docs', name: 'Docs' });
const artifact = await apps.artifacts.uploadArchive(tarGzBytes);
const deployment = await apps.deployments.create(app.app_id, {
  artifact_id: artifact.artifact_id,
  source: { kind: 'static', spa: true },
});

for (;;) {
  const { deployment: current } = await apps.deployments.get(app.app_id, deployment.deployment_id);
  if (current.status === 'ready') break;
  if (current.status === 'failed' || current.status === 'cancelled') throw new Error(current.error ?? current.status);
  await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 2000));
}
```

| Group | Calls |
|---|---|
| App | `list`, `create`, `get`, `update` (also `labels` and `display`), `remove`, `start`, `stop`, `rollback(appId, deploymentId)` |
| Artifacts | `artifacts.register` (`archive` or `oci_image`), `artifacts.uploadArchive`, `artifacts.finalize` |
| Releases | `deployments.create` (`artifact_id`, `source`, `provider?`, `environment?`, `secrets?`, `config?`), `deployments.list`, `deployments.get` (with events and build log), `deployments.logs`, `deployments.remove` |
| Machines | `machines.list`, `machines.start`, `machines.stop`, `machines.restart`, `machines.destroy`, `scale(appId, { worker: 3 })`, `exec(appId, { command: ['ls', '/data'] })` |
| Volumes | `volumes.list`, `volumes.create`, `volumes.update`, `volumes.destroy(appId, volumeId, name)`, `volumes.snapshots`, `volumes.snapshot`, `volumes.restore`, `volumes.fork` |
| Access | `access.get`, `access.update` (`mode`, `member_ids`, `group_ids`, `password`, `viewer_token_scope`), `access.session` (a 5-minute link), `access.agents` |
| Sign-in | `token(appId)`, `authEnv(appId)` |

React hooks in `@kortix/sdk/react`:
`useProjectApps`, `useAppDeployments`, `useAppDeployment`, `useAppAccess`,
`useAppMachines`, `useAppVolumes` and `useAppVolumeSnapshots`. Signatures and
types: [SDK reference](/docs/developers#the-project-handle) and
[React hooks](/docs/developers#react-hooks).

### Permissions and CLI reference

| Permission | Allows | Held by |
|---|---|---|
| `project.app.read` | List and open the Apps you may see. List the agents granted an App. Create an access link. Mint a sign-in token. Read the `auth-env` values. | Member, Manager |
| `project.app.write` | Create an App. Change its name, size, run mode, labels, `display`, `uses` and external URL. Read and change the access policy. Delete a release. Delete an App. | Manager |
| `project.app.deploy` | Upload artifacts, deploy, roll back, start and stop. Read releases, their events and build logs, and runtime logs. List, start, stop, restart and destroy machines. Scale. List and create volumes, set snapshot retention, list and take snapshots, fork a volume. | Manager |
| `project.app.admin` | `exec`. Restore or destroy a volume. Open an `access: admins` service. Delete an App that has volumes. Read the App's event credentials (`GET …/apps/<app_id>/events/credentials`). | Manager |

`project.app.write` and `project.app.deploy` include `project.app.read`.
`project.app.admin` includes `project.app.write`, not `project.app.deploy`.
Account owners and admins act as Managers on every project. See
[Teams](/docs/guide#teams) for roles and custom roles.

`<app>` is an App id or slug. Every subcommand takes `--project <id>`,
`--host <name>` and `--json`.

| Command | What it does |
|---|---|
| `kortix apps list` (`ls`) | The Apps you may open: slug, state, URL |
| `kortix apps create <slug>` (`new`) | Create an App without a deploy. `--name`, `--cpu`, `--memory`, `--disk`, `--idle-timeout`, `--always-on` or `--on-demand`, `--uses`. `--url <https-url>` creates an external App |
| `kortix apps launch --template convex` | Write a template's blocks to `kortix.yaml` in the current directory and deploy them. `--name <slug>`, `--no-deploy` |
| `kortix apps deploy [path]` | Deploy a directory, a `.tar.gz` or `--image`. Flags: `--app`, `--slug`, `--name`, `--manifest-app`, `--type static\|bundle\|dockerfile`, `--command`, `--port`, `--dockerfile`, `--root`, `--output-dir`, `--install-command`, `--build-command`, `--readiness-path`, `--spa` or `--no-spa`, `--include-node-modules`, `--access`, `--password`, `--members`, `--groups`, `--always-on` or `--on-demand`, `--listed` or `--unlisted`, `--no-wait`, `--wait-seconds` |
| `kortix apps set <app>` (`update`) | Change `--name`, `--url` (an external App), `--cpu`, `--memory-gb`, `--disk-gb`, `--idle-timeout`, `--always-on` or `--on-demand`, `--uses`, `--listed` or `--unlisted` |
| `kortix apps link <app> --uses <slugs>` | Add used Apps. `unlink` removes them. |
| `kortix apps show <app>` (`get`) | Capabilities, links, run mode and releases |
| `kortix apps releases <app>` | Releases, marking the live one |
| `kortix apps rollback <app> <vN>` | A new release from `vN`. `--no-wait` |
| `kortix apps logs <app> [deployment]` | Runtime logs. `--after N`, `--limit N` |
| `kortix apps start <app>`, `stop <app>` | Wake a server App, or suspend it now |
| `kortix apps machines [ls\|start\|stop\|restart\|destroy] <app> [machine]` (`machine`) | List machines or act on one |
| `kortix apps scale count <app> <n>` | Machines for a group (0–20). `--process <group>` (default `app`) |
| `kortix apps scale show <app>` | Machines per group |
| `kortix apps exec <app> -- <command…>` | Run one command. `--machine <id>`, `--timeout <s>` |
| `kortix apps volumes [ls\|create\|set\|snapshots\|snapshot\|restore\|fork\|destroy] <app> …` (`volume`) | Manage volumes and snapshots. `set --retention <days>` keeps daily snapshots that many days. |
| `kortix apps labels <app> [a,b]` | Show or replace labels. `--clear` |
| `kortix apps access <app>` | Read or set access. `--mode`, `--password`, `--members`, `--groups`, `--viewer off\|identity\|api` |
| `kortix apps access-link <app>` | A 5-minute sign-in link |
| `kortix apps connect <app>` | How to reach the App from code. No secret. |
| `kortix apps token <app>` | A 15-minute sign-in token that names you |
| `kortix apps auth-env <app>` | The `KORTIX_AUTH_*` values as dotenv lines |
| `kortix apps delete <app> --yes` (`rm`, `remove`) | Delete the App. `--deployment <vN>` deletes one release. |

The whole CLI: [CLI](/docs/developers#cli). Every route: the REST reference at
`https://api.kortix.com/v1/docs`.

## Self-hosting

A self-hosted instance is the full Kortix platform on a server you control: one Docker Compose stack that the `kortix self-host` CLI installs, configures and updates. This page is for the operator who runs that server. Sessions run on Platinum sandboxes, not on the server. The instance must be reachable from the internet so those sandboxes can call back.

<Steps>
<Step title="Point DNS at the server">
Create A or AAAA records for `kortix.example.com` and `api.kortix.example.com`, plus a wildcard `*.p.kortix.example.com` for previews. Open ports 80 and 443.
</Step>
<Step title="Run the bootstrap on the server">
It installs Docker and the CLI, then starts the stack with a TLS certificate and your Platinum API key.

```sh
curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kortix-ai/suna/dev/scripts/kortix-selfhost-up.sh \
  | bash -s -- --domain kortix.example.com --email ops@example.com --platinum-key <platinum_api_key>
```
</Step>
<Step title="Answer the setup questions">
If the bootstrap added you to the `docker` group, open a new shell first. Run `kortix self-host configure`. Give your admin email and the preview domain `p.kortix.example.com`.

Enter keeps the Platinum API key the bootstrap set. Then run `kortix self-host start` to apply the answers.
</Step>
<Step title="Sign up as the admin">
Open `https://kortix.example.com` and sign up with the admin email. Connect GitHub at `/admin/git`, create the team account and a project, then add a model key. See [First sign-in and setup](#first-sign-in-and-setup).

Do this as soon as the stack starts. Until you set `EMAIL_URL` or `DISABLE_SIGNUP=true`, anyone who reaches the instance can create a confirmed account for any address, the admin address included.
</Step>
<Step title="Check the instance">
Run `kortix self-host doctor` and fix every failing check. See [Secure the instance](#secure-the-instance).
</Step>
</Steps>

### What runs where

`kortix self-host init` writes `docker-compose.yml`, `.env`, a `Caddyfile` and `updater.sh` into `~/.config/kortix/self-host/<instance>/`. `kortix self-host start` runs `docker compose up`. One Compose project, `kortix-<instance>`, holds every service.

| Service | What it does |
|---|---|
| `frontend` | The web app. |
| `kortix-api` | The REST API at `/v1`. It relays any `/v1/llm` request that reaches it to `llm-gateway`. |
| `llm-gateway` | The standalone LLM gateway. Caddy sends `api.<domain>/v1/llm*` here. |
| `kortix-migrate` | A one-shot job. It migrates the database before `kortix-api` starts. |
| `kortix-updater` | Pulls new images once a day, migrates, and rolls the app services. It mounts the Docker socket. |
| `capture-store` | The object store for [Kortix Capture](/docs/capture) recordings (MinIO). It publishes no port: devices reach it through the API. Its data is in the `kortix-capture-data` volume and grows with every enrolled device. |
| `supabase-*` | The Supabase distribution, every image pinned by digest: Postgres (`supabase-db`), auth, REST, realtime, storage, imgproxy, meta, functions, Kong, the Supavisor pooler, Studio, analytics and vector. |
| `caddy` | Domain mode only. Terminates TLS on ports 80 and 443 with ACME HTTP-01 and routes traffic. |
| `cloudflared` | Tunnel mode only. Publishes `kortix-api` through a Cloudflare tunnel. |

Off the box:

- **Sandboxes.** Every session runs in its own Platinum sandbox. `kortix-api` creates it with `PLATINUM_API_KEY`. The sandbox calls back to the instance's public API URL, `KORTIX_URL`.
- **What a session keeps.** `/workspace` holds this session's files, kept for the life of the session. `/drives` (Drive) holds your files and the company's files, shared across sessions and kept until deleted. Anything else (home directory, `/tmp`, installed packages) can be gone when the session reopens. Put tools in a [sandbox template](/docs/developers#sandbox-templates).
- **Drive and App volumes.** Each Drive and each App volume is a Platinum volume. Its data is at Platinum, not on the server.
- **Images.** `kortix/kortix-frontend`, `kortix/kortix-api` and `kortix/kortix-gateway` on Docker Hub. Pulls need no credentials.
- **Model providers.** The gateway calls the providers your projects connect.

#### Reachability modes

| Mode | How you set it | Public URLs | App replicas | Sessions run |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| domain | `--domain <domain>` | `https://<domain>` and `https://api.<domain>` | 2 each of `kortix-api`, `llm-gateway`, `frontend` behind Caddy | yes |
| tunnel | `--tunnel cloudflare` | The web app stays on `http://localhost:13737`. Only the API is public, at the tunnel URL. | 1 each | yes |
| local | neither flag | none: every port binds `127.0.0.1` | 1 each | no, the sandbox cannot reach the API |

Domain mode is the production setup. Tunnel mode is for evaluation on your own machine. Without a domain, the services publish loopback ports: frontend `13737`, API `13738`, Supabase `13740`. When the stack is stopped, `start` moves a port that is in use to a free one.

### Requirements

- **A Linux server** with a public IP. The bootstrap installs Docker Engine and the Compose plugin. On a server that has them, install only the CLI. The CLI runs on Linux and macOS, on x86_64 and arm64.
- **DNS.** A or AAAA records for `<domain>` and `api.<domain>`, both pointed at the server, before the first start. Caddy needs them to get the certificate.
- **Ports 80 and 443** open inbound.
- **Wildcard records** for the optional origins:
  - `*.p.<domain>` for sandbox previews. Use it on every public instance: without it, browser previews fall back to a path proxy that breaks root-absolute links, and `doctor` fails.
  - `*.<apps base domain>`, for example `*.apps.<domain>`, to host [Apps](/docs/developers#apps-reference).
- **A Platinum API key** (`PLATINUM_API_KEY`). Without it, sessions do not start and projects have no Drive.
- **A model provider key.** You add it in the web app after the first sign-in.

### Install

#### Bootstrap a bare server

The bootstrap script runs on Linux only. It installs Docker and the Compose plugin when they are missing, installs the CLI, runs `kortix self-host init`, sets the domain, and runs `kortix self-host start`. Re-running it is safe.

| Flag | Default | Sets |
|---|---|---|
| `--domain <domain>` | none | `KORTIX_DOMAIN`. Turns on Caddy and TLS. |
| `--api-domain <domain>` | `api.<domain>` | `KORTIX_API_DOMAIN` |
| `--email <email>` | `admin@<domain>` | `KORTIX_ACME_EMAIL`, the certificate contact |
| `--channel <stable\|latest>` | `stable` | The image channel |
| `--auto-update <on\|off>` | `on` | The daily updater |
| `--instance <name>` | `default` | The instance name |
| `--platinum-key <key>` | `PLATINUM_API_KEY` from the environment | `PLATINUM_API_KEY` |

The bootstrap runs `init` non-interactively. It sets no admin email and no preview domain, so run `kortix self-host configure` and then `kortix self-host start` after it. When the script adds your user to the `docker` group, open a new shell before the next `kortix self-host` command.

#### Install by hand

Use this path on a server that already has Docker and the Compose plugin.

```sh
curl -fsSL https://kortix.com/install | bash
kortix self-host init --domain kortix.example.com --admin-email you@example.com
kortix self-host start
```

A first `init` in a terminal asks every setup question. `--yes`, or a shell with no terminal, skips the questions and keeps the defaults. `start` runs `init` first when the instance has no configuration. When the Platinum API key is missing, `init` and `start` print a warning and continue.

#### Evaluate with a Cloudflare tunnel

To try Kortix on your own machine with no domain:

```sh
kortix self-host init --tunnel cloudflare
kortix self-host start
```

The web app opens at `http://localhost:13737`. `cloudflared` publishes the API at an `https://<random>.trycloudflare.com` URL. That URL changes each time `cloudflared` restarts, and `start` and `update` capture the new one. For a stable URL, create a named tunnel in Cloudflare Zero Trust, point its public hostname at `http://kortix-api:8008`, then set both values:

```sh
kortix self-host env set CLOUDFLARE_TUNNEL_TOKEN=<token> CLOUDFLARE_TUNNEL_HOSTNAME=kortix-api.example.com
kortix self-host start
```

### Configure

`kortix self-host configure` asks the setup questions again, in this order. Each answer is pre-filled with the current value, so Enter keeps it. `configure` writes the files only. Run `kortix self-host start` to apply them.

| Step | What it sets |
|---|---|
| Reachability | `domain` (domain, API subdomain, ACME email) or `tunnel` (quick or named tunnel). |
| Admin email | `KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS`, comma-separated. These people are platform admins. Also `--admin-email <email>`. |
| Enterprise license | `ENTERPRISE_LICENSE_AVAILABLE`. With a license, it unlocks SSO, SCIM, role-based access control and audit. Also `--enterprise-license`. |
| Restrict account creation | On by default. Only a platform admin can create a new account. Others get `403` `account_creation_restricted`. Sign-ups, invites, SSO and each person's personal account still work. Also `--no-restrict-account-creation` and `--restrict-account-creation`. |
| Platinum | `PLATINUM_API_KEY`, `PLATINUM_API_URL` (default `https://api.platinum.dev`) and `PLATINUM_TEMPLATE` (optional). |
| Apps hosting | Optional. `KORTIX_APPS_BASE_DOMAIN`, which needs a wildcard DNS record. |
| Preview origins | `KORTIX_PREVIEW_BASE_DOMAIN`, suggested `p.<domain>`, which needs a `*.p.<domain>` record. In domain mode the default answer is configure. |
| Composio | Optional. `COMPOSIO_API_KEY`, the app catalog for [connectors](/docs/guide#connectors). |
| Update policy | Auto-update on or off, channel, daily time and timezone. |

SAML sign-in is on in every instance, and `init` generates `SAML_PRIVATE_KEY` once. Do not change that key after you register an identity provider: it is the instance's SAML identity. To set up SSO, see [Single sign-on and directory sync](/docs/guide#single-sign-on-and-directory-sync).

### First sign-in and setup

1. **Sign up as the admin.** Open `https://<domain>` and sign up with an address in `KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS`. A password, email-code or social sign-in matches that list. A SAML sign-in never does. To change the admins later, set the full comma-separated list: `kortix self-host env set KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS=you@example.com,ops@example.com`. The value replaces the list.
2. **Connect managed git.** Open `/admin/git`. Kortix creates and pushes every new project repository through this one GitHub identity. Pick **Create a GitHub App** (recommended), **Paste an existing App**, or **Use an access token**. Until it is set, Kortix cannot create project repositories. You can still connect an existing repository. See [Git](/docs/developers#git).
3. **Create the team account.** Your sign-up account is personal. It cannot hold SAML SSO (`403` `sso_personal_account`), and a project cannot move to another account. The menu that creates accounts lives inside a project, so first create one project at `/new`. In it, open the project menu → **Switch Project** → **Create an account…**. Only platform admins can create accounts by default. Invite your team and set up SSO in this account.
4. **Create a project** in the team account. After **Create an account…**, Kortix opens `/new` with the new account selected.
5. **Add a model key** in the project: **Customize → Models → Providers**, or `kortix providers set <provider>`. Each project needs its own key. A self-hosted instance has no Kortix-managed models. A project can connect **Platinum** as its provider with a Platinum API key. See [Connect your own provider](/docs/guide#models).
6. **Turn on email.** Set one connection string:

   ```sh
   kortix self-host env set EMAIL_URL=smtp://user:password@smtp.example.com:587
   ```

   Kortix then sends sign-in and invitation mail through it, requires email confirmation for new accounts, and adds magic-link sign-in. `EMAIL_FROM` defaults to `Kortix <noreply@<domain>>`. Other forms: `smtps://user:password@host:465`, `resend://<api-key>`, `ses://<key-id>:<secret>@<region>`, `ses://<region>`, `mailtrap://<api-token>`. Separate several with commas for a fallback chain, tried left to right.
7. **Close sign-up.** When your team has signed up, run `kortix self-host env set DISABLE_SIGNUP=true`, then `kortix self-host start` so the auth service reads it.

A new instance starts with these defaults:

| Setting | Default |
|---|---|
| Sign-in methods | Password only. Email sign-ups confirm without an email, so a sign-up's address is unproven. See [Unconfirmed email addresses](#unconfirmed-email-addresses). |
| Social sign-in | None |
| Billing | Off |
| Marketing pages | Off. Every marketing route opens the web app. Turn on with `KORTIX_PUBLIC_DISABLE_LANDING_PAGE=false`. |
| Connector app catalog | Hidden until `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` is set |
| Account creation | Platform admins only |

### Secure the instance

`kortix self-host doctor` runs these checks. It exits `0` when all pass and `1` when one fails. `--json` prints the result as JSON.

| Check | Fails when | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `docker` | `docker --version` fails | Install Docker. |
| `docker-compose` | The Compose plugin is missing | Install the Compose plugin. |
| `compose-config` | The rendered `docker-compose.yml` is not valid | Correct the value the error names with `env set`. |
| `capture-store` | The Compose file has no `capture-store` service, so Capture devices cannot sync | Run `kortix self-host update`. |
| `instance-dir` | The instance directory moved since the last write | Run `kortix self-host configure`, or any of `init`, `start`, `update`, `env set`. |
| `email-url` | `EMAIL_URL` is set but does not parse | Correct the value. |
| `preview-origins` | Domain mode and `KORTIX_PREVIEW_BASE_DOMAIN` is empty | Point `*.p.<domain>` at the server, then `kortix self-host env set KORTIX_PREVIEW_BASE_DOMAIN=p.<domain> KORTIX_PREVIEW_ALLOW_DIRECT_EDGE=true`. |
| `open-sign-up` | A domain or tunnel instance has sign-up open and email confirmation off | Set `EMAIL_URL`, or set `DISABLE_SIGNUP=true` once your team is in. |

<Callout type="warning">
A new instance confirms every sign-up without an email. Until you fix `open-sign-up`, anyone who reaches the instance can create a confirmed account for any address, an admin address included. Sign up as the admin first, then set `EMAIL_URL` or `DISABLE_SIGNUP=true`. `kortix self-host init` prints the same warning on a domain or tunnel instance.

`init --disable-signup` turns sign-up off from the first start, for an install that creates its accounts another way. While it is off, nobody can sign up, the admin included.
</Callout>

What the stack does by itself:

- Only Caddy publishes public ports, 80 and 443. Every other published port binds `127.0.0.1`.
- `.env` and `docker-compose.yml` are written with mode `0600`.
- [Connectors](/docs/guide#connectors) refuse private and internal addresses with `connector_egress_blocked`. List the internal hosts they may reach in `KORTIX_CONNECTOR_EGRESS_ALLOW_HOSTS`.
- `kortix-updater` holds the Docker socket, so its image is pinned by digest.

Keep `.env` safe: it holds every secret, and `API_KEY_SECRET` in it encrypts every stored secret, connector credential and token. A lost `API_KEY_SECRET` makes them unreadable. The Secrets page marks each such project secret **Undecryptable**, and **Customize → Models → Providers** does the same for each account key and ChatGPT login.

A connector credential shows no mark: a call through it returns `credential_undecryptable`. A session start names the first value it needs, and any other request that needs one answers `409 SECRET_UNDECRYPTABLE`. Enter each value again to fix it, and save each OAuth application again before you reconnect its account.

#### Unconfirmed email addresses

`ENABLE_EMAIL_AUTOCONFIRM=true` is the default until you set `EMAIL_URL`. Auth then confirms each sign-up without an email, and the API reads the same value from `.env`. While it is `true`, the API treats the email of a password, email-code or social login as unproven. Whoever signed up first holds the address:

| Path | With `ENABLE_EMAIL_AUTOCONFIRM=true` |
|---|---|
| Sign-in with a pending invite | Sign-in does not accept the invite. |
| SCIM `POST /Users` for that address | Creates a pending invite (`201`). No membership. A password login does not see that invite, and accepting or declining it returns `403` `email_unconfirmed`. The invite page then shows **Continue with single sign-on**. It signs in through the account's SAML provider, also for an address outside the provider's domain, and the person joins. An admin can instead click **Resend** on the pending invite: it is then an invite a person sent. |
| **Members** → **Invite** for that address | Creates a pending invite instead of adding the login. |
| An invite a person sent or resent (**Members** → **Invite** or **Resend**, a project invite) | The login that holds the address sees it in the project chooser and the **Switch Project** menu, and can accept it. That login is whoever signed up first with the address, so it may not be the person you invited. |
| `KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS` | Matches a password login with the address. Whoever signs up first with an allowlisted address is platform admin. |

A login that is already a member of the account, and a login through the account's own SAML provider, still match inside that account. `KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS` never matches a SAML login. Setting `EMAIL_URL` turns `ENABLE_EMAIL_AUTOCONFIRM` off and restores the matching. A login created while it was on is not confirmed again: after you add email, its address matches invites and SCIM like a confirmed one.

Set `EMAIL_URL` before you invite people. Without it, invite a person only after that person has signed up: an address has one password login, so nobody else can take it then. A cloud instance never sets the value.

### The platform admin console

Platform admins open `/admin`. On a self-hosted instance, a platform admin is a person listed in `KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS`.

| Page | Path | What it holds |
|---|---|---|
| Overview | `/admin` | Live instance numbers, refreshed every 15 seconds. |
| Accounts | `/admin/accounts` | Every account. Filter, sort and inspect one. |
| Projects | `/admin/projects` | Every project across every account, most active first, with its operator-managed feature flags. |
| Analytics | `/admin/analytics` | Daily platform activity, by UTC day. |
| Sandboxes | `/admin/sandboxes` | Where new sandboxes are placed, and the live fleet. |
| Git | `/admin/git` | The instance's managed GitHub identity. When `MANAGED_GIT_*` environment variables set it, the page is read-only. |
| Maintenance | `/admin/utils` | System-wide notices and the full-lockdown switch. |

### Connect your clients

`kortix self-host start` registers the CLI host `selfhost` on the server. To point the web, CLI, TUI, desktop and mobile clients at the instance, see [Connect to a self-hosted instance](/docs/guide#everywhere).

### Integrations

Set these with `kortix self-host env set`. Each row says what the variable turns on or off.

| Variables | Effect |
|---|---|
| `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` | The connector app catalog. Without it, Easy connect, the `/connections` page and the onboarding apps step are hidden. Custom connectors work without it. |
| `KORTIX_OAUTH_CLIENTS` | Your own OAuth clients for the [app catalog](/docs/guide#connectors) apps whose servers have no dynamic client registration. JSON, one entry per vendor: `google`, `github`, `slack`, `hubspot`, `asana`, `box`. Without an entry, those apps are not listed. See [Kortix OAuth clients](#kortix-oauth-clients). |
| `SLACK_CLIENT_ID`, `SLACK_CLIENT_SECRET`, `SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET` | One-click **Add to Slack** with your own Slack app. Without all three, each project connects its own Slack app. See [Channels](/docs/guide#channels). |
| `MICROSOFT_APP_ID`, `MICROSOFT_APP_PASSWORD`, `MICROSOFT_APP_TENANT` (default `botframework.com`) | One shared Microsoft Teams bot. Without them, each project brings its own bot app. |
| `RESEND_API_KEY` | Transactional email through Resend. |
| `TUNNEL_ENABLED` | Default `true`. `false` turns off [computers](/docs/guide#computers): `/v1/tunnel/*` answers `503` `tunnel_disabled`. |
| `PLATINUM_API_KEY` | Required. Sessions, App machines and volumes, and Drive (`/drives`). |

OAuth callbacks, such as `<KORTIX_URL>/v1/connectors/oauth2/callback` and the Slack and Teams redirects, use `KORTIX_URL`. Domain mode and a named tunnel keep it stable. A quick tunnel changes it at every restart and breaks every callback you registered.

#### Kortix OAuth clients

Some MCP servers in the catalog do not let Kortix register itself. Kortix signs in to them with an OAuth client that you create at the vendor once per instance.

1. At the vendor, create an OAuth app with the redirect URI `<KORTIX_URL>/v1/connectors/oauth2/callback`.
   - Google: a **Web application** client. Select **Use this client for an AI-powered agent**. Enable the Gmail, Calendar, Drive, Docs and Sheets APIs and their MCP services. Add the scopes from Google's [MCP server guide](https://developers.google.com/workspace/guides/configure-mcp-servers). The Workspace MCP servers are a Google developer preview.
   - GitHub: an OAuth app (not a GitHub App).
2. Store the clients as one JSON value:

   ```sh
   kortix self-host env set KORTIX_OAUTH_CLIENTS='{"google":{"client_id":"<id>","client_secret":"<secret>"},"github":{"client_id":"<id>","client_secret":"<secret>"}}'
   ```

3. Restart the API. The apps of each configured vendor appear in the catalog.

#### Composio toolkits that need your own OAuth app

Some toolkits have no Composio-managed sign-in. Syncing one fails with `422` `composio_auth_config_required`. In the Composio dashboard, open **Auth Configs** and create one with your own OAuth app.

Then sync again. No restart is needed.

### Updates and versions

`kortix-updater` checks once a day at `KORTIX_UPDATE_TIME` (default `02:00`) in `KORTIX_UPDATE_TZ` (default `America/New_York`). Auto-update is on by default.

| Track | Images |
|---|---|
| `stable` (default) | A release that Kortix promotes to self-hosted instances after it runs in production. |
| `latest` | Every Kortix release. |
| `--tag <version>` | One exact version. A pin wins over the channel. |

Each run:

1. Checks free disk space. It stops below `KORTIX_MIN_FREE_DISK_MB` (default `2048`).
2. Pulls the configured tags. When no image changed, it stops.
3. Runs `kortix-migrate`. When a migration fails, nothing is swapped and the previous version keeps serving.
4. Rolls `kortix-api`, `llm-gateway` and `frontend`. With 2 replicas, new containers must pass their health check within `KORTIX_ROLLOUT_TIMEOUT` (default `300` seconds) before the old ones stop, so the instance stays up. A swap that fails keeps the previous version. With 1 replica, each service restarts in place with a short interruption.
5. Removes superseded images after a fully successful run. It keeps the current and the previous version for a fast rollback.

`kortix self-host status` shows the last outcome (`ok`, `degraded`, `failed`, `skipped` or `never-run`) and the version change. It also shows a drift check of running images against the configuration, and the schedule. Set `KORTIX_UPDATE_WEBHOOK_URL` to receive each run's status as a JSON `POST`.

```sh
kortix self-host update                          # apply the channel now
kortix self-host update --channel latest         # switch channel
kortix self-host update --tag <version>          # pin one version
kortix self-host rollback --release <version>    # go back to a version
kortix self-host version                         # running, latest and channel
```

A manual `update` uses the same rollout as the daily run. When another update holds the lock, it exits `75` and skips the rollout. A pin holds through every daily run.

`init` or `update` without `--tag` moves the instance back to its channel. To change auto-update, the time or the timezone, run `kortix self-host configure`, then `kortix self-host start`.

#### Sessions saved by an older version

Each session's history is its session log. An instance that ran a version without the session log also holds history in the older v1 store. The API converts it by itself:

- A background pass (`SESSION_LOG_BACKFILL`, default `write`) converts every old session, oldest first, on the leader API container. It logs `[session-log] backfill tick` with counts.
- Opening an old session before the pass reaches it converts that session first.
- A session over the conversion size cap (24 MiB, `SESSION_LOG_RESTORE_MAX_BYTES`) is converted only by the background pass. Until then, its log read answers `503` `session_log_converting` with `Retry-After: 60`.

`SESSION_LOG_BACKFILL=off` stops the pass. Keep it on until the API logs `the session logs hold every v1 mirror message`: until then, the v1 store is the only copy of some history.

### Operate and tune

#### Settings and secrets

Every setting lives in the instance `.env`.

- `kortix self-host env ls` lists every value by group and masks secrets. `--show` reveals them.
- `kortix self-host env set KEY=VALUE ...` writes values and restarts the services that read them. A bare `KEY` prompts for the value. A new `API_KEY_SECRET` makes every stored credential undecryptable, so `env set` warns first. At a terminal it asks you to confirm. A script must pass `--yes`: without it the command exits `2` and writes nothing.
- `kortix self-host env rotate <KEY>` or `--all-generated` regenerates a generated secret. Rotating `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET` also regenerates the anon and service-role keys. The CLI refuses `API_KEY_SECRET`, `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` and `SAML_PRIVATE_KEY`, because a new value breaks stored data or connections.

`env set` refuses the image and version keys (`KORTIX_VERSION`, `API_IMAGE`, `FRONTEND_IMAGE`, `GATEWAY_IMAGE`). Use `--tag` or `--channel` instead. When a change does not take effect, run `kortix self-host start`: it recreates every service whose configuration changed.

#### Memory limits

| Variable | Default | Container |
|---|---|---|
| `KORTIX_API_MEMORY_LIMIT` | `2048m` | `kortix-api`, which relays `/v1/llm` request bodies when they reach it |
| `KORTIX_GATEWAY_MEMORY_LIMIT` | `2048m` | `llm-gateway` |
| `KORTIX_FRONTEND_MEMORY_LIMIT` | `512m` | `frontend` |

Postgres has a fixed limit of `1280m`. `KORTIX_GATEWAY_MAX_REQUEST_BYTES` raises the gateway's request-body cap for image-heavy turns. Raise the gateway memory limit with it.

#### Session and sandbox lifecycle

| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_AUTOSTOP_MINUTES` | `15` | A sandbox stops this many idle minutes after its last turn ends. An open session page never holds the sandbox past this window. |
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_TURN_GRANT_MINUTES` | `240` | How long a running turn holds its sandbox. Model calls and an observed running turn renew it. |
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_TURN_WAITING_MINUTES` | `120` | How long a turn that waits on a person holds its sandbox. At most half the turn grant. Unattended runs get 30. |
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_PREVIEW_GRANT_MINUTES` | `30` | How long preview traffic keeps a sandbox running. |
| `KORTIX_SANDBOX_PROVIDER_AUTOSTOP_MINUTES` | `720` | Platinum's own idle stop, a backstop for sandboxes the API cannot reach. |
| `KORTIX_DEFAULT_SANDBOX_CPU`, `_MEMORY_GB`, `_DISK_GB` | `2`, `4`, `20` | The sandbox size when a template sets none. |

People see the effect of these values in [Computers](/docs/guide#computers).

#### App hosting

| Variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `KORTIX_APPS_BASE_DOMAIN` | none | The domain [Apps](/docs/developers#apps-reference) are served on, for example `apps.kortix.example.com`. It needs a `*.apps.kortix.example.com` DNS record. `kortix self-host configure` sets it. Caddy serves Apps only when it is set. |
| `KORTIX_APPS_RETAINED_DEPLOYMENTS` | `5` | The ready releases each App keeps as rollback targets, beside the live one. At least `1`. |

#### Logs, containers and the database console

- `kortix self-host logs [service]` follows the logs, for example `kortix self-host logs kortix-api`. Each container keeps 3 log files of 10 MB.
- `kortix self-host ps` lists the containers. `status` adds the update report.
- Supabase Studio answers on `http://127.0.0.1:13740` (`SUPABASE_PORT`) on the server. Reach it through an SSH tunnel and sign in as `kortix` with `DASHBOARD_PASSWORD` from `env ls --show`.

#### Several instances on one server

Pass `--instance <name>` to every command. A name starts with a letter and holds letters, digits, dots, underscores and dashes. Each instance has its own directory and Compose project.

Only one instance per server can run in domain mode, because Caddy binds ports 80 and 443. `KORTIX_SELF_HOST_CONFIG_DIR` moves the root directory from `~/.config/kortix/self-host`.

#### How Caddy routes

| Host | Path | Goes to |
|---|---|---|
| `api.<domain>` | `/v1/llm*` | `llm-gateway:8090` |
| `api.<domain>` | everything else | `kortix-api:8008` |
| `<domain>` | `/rest/v1`, `/auth/v1`, `/storage/v1`, `/realtime/v1`, `/functions/v1`, `/graphql/v1` | `supabase-kong:8000` |
| `<domain>` | everything else | `frontend:3000` |
| `*.<preview base domain>` | all | `kortix-api`, one origin per sandbox port |
| `*.<apps base domain>` | all | `kortix-api`, one hostname per App |

Caddy health-checks each replica and retries a failed connection on the other one. It sends `Strict-Transport-Security` with a 30-day max-age. The two wildcard blocks exist only when their base domain is set.

Caddy gets one certificate per hostname on its first request. The API approves each hostname before Caddy asks for the certificate. You need a wildcard DNS record, not a wildcard certificate.

### Back up and uninstall

The server holds its data in two places: the instance directory, `~/.config/kortix/self-host/<instance>/`, and named Docker volumes. Each Docker volume is named `kortix-<instance>_<volume>`, for example `kortix-default_kortix-capture-data`.

| Data | Where | Server backup |
|---|---|---|
| Every secret and signing key | `.env` in the instance directory | Yes |
| The Postgres database | `volumes/db/data` in the instance directory | Yes |
| Stored files | `volumes/storage` in the instance directory | Yes |
| Postgres settings and its pgsodium key | Docker volume `db-config` | Yes |
| Kortix Capture recordings | Docker volume `kortix-capture-data` | Yes |
| TLS certificates | Docker volume `kortix-caddy-data` | Yes |
| Updater status, the deployed version and image history | Docker volume `kortix-updater-state` | Yes |
| Drive folders and App volumes | Platinum volumes, at Platinum | No |
| Each session's `/workspace` | The session's Platinum sandbox | No |

Stop the stack so the files are consistent. Copy the instance directory and each Docker volume, then start again:

```sh
kortix self-host stop
cd ~
sudo tar -czf kortix-backup.tgz -C ~/.config/kortix/self-host default
for v in db-config kortix-capture-data kortix-caddy-data kortix-updater-state; do
  docker run --rm -v "kortix-default_$v":/data -v "$PWD":/backup alpine:3.20 \
    tar -czf "/backup/$v.tgz" -C /data .
done
kortix self-host start
```

Replace `default` with your instance name. App volumes keep their own daily snapshots: see [Volumes and snapshots](/docs/developers#volumes-and-snapshots).

To restore on a server with Docker and the CLI, put the files from the backup in your home directory and run:

```sh
cd ~
mkdir -p ~/.config/kortix/self-host
sudo tar -xzf kortix-backup.tgz -C ~/.config/kortix/self-host
for v in db-config kortix-capture-data kortix-caddy-data kortix-updater-state; do
  docker run --rm -v "kortix-default_$v":/data -v "$PWD":/backup alpine:3.20 \
    tar -xzf "/backup/$v.tgz" -C /data
done
kortix self-host start
```

`start` warns that each volume `already exists but was not created by Docker Compose`. The warning is harmless: the stack uses the restored volumes.

<Callout type="warning">
`kortix self-host uninstall` runs `docker compose down --volumes --remove-orphans` and deletes the instance directory. It deletes the database, the stored files, the Capture recordings and the certificates. It cannot be undone.

In a terminal you type the instance name to confirm. Without a terminal it needs `--yes`.
</Callout>

### Commands

Every subcommand takes `--instance <name>` (default `default`).

| Command | What it does |
|---|---|
| `init` | Writes or refreshes the Compose file and `.env`. A first run in a terminal asks the setup questions. Alias `setup`. |
| `start` | Pulls the images and starts the stack. Aliases `up`, `deploy`. |
| `stop` | Stops the stack. Data stays. Alias `down`. |
| `restart` | `stop`, then `start`. |
| `configure` | Asks the setup questions again. Alias `config`. |
| `status` | Container state, the last update, drift and the schedule. `--json` |
| `ps` | `docker compose ps` only. |
| `doctor` | Runs the checks in [Secure the instance](#secure-the-instance). `--json` |
| `plan` | Validates the Compose file. Applies nothing. `--json` |
| `logs [service]` | Follows the logs. |
| `open` | Opens the web app in a browser. |
| `version` | The running version, the newest release and the channel. |
| `update` | Applies the channel, or `--tag <version>`, now. Aliases `upgrade`, `reconcile`. |
| `rollback --release <version>` | Updates to that version. `--tag` also works. |
| `env ls [--show]` | Lists every setting. `--json` |
| `env set KEY=VALUE ...` | Sets values and restarts the affected services. |
| `env rotate KEY` or `--all-generated` | Regenerates a generated secret. |
| `uninstall` | Deletes the stack, its data and its directory. |
| `connect-github` | Deprecated. Prints where to connect GitHub. Use `/admin/git`. |

| Flag | Used with | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `--domain <domain>` | `init`, `configure`, `update` | Domain mode. `--domain ""` clears the domain. |
| `--tunnel cloudflare` | `init`, `configure`, `update` | Tunnel mode. |
| `--tag <version>` | `init`, `update`, `rollback` | Pin a version. Aliases `--version`, `--release`. |
| `--channel <stable\|latest>` | `init`, `update`, `configure` | The channel to track. |
| `--auto-update <on\|off>` | `init`, `update`, `configure` | The daily updater. Takes effect at the next `start`. |
| `--update-time <HH:MM>`, `--update-tz <zone>` | `init`, `update`, `configure` | The daily window, as 24-hour time and an IANA zone. Takes effect at the next `start`. |
| `--admin-email <email>` | `init`, `configure` | Sets `KORTIX_PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS`. |
| `--enterprise-license` | `init`, `configure`, `update` | Sets `ENTERPRISE_LICENSE_AVAILABLE=true`. |
| `--restrict-account-creation`, `--no-restrict-account-creation` | `init`, `configure`, `update` | Account creation for admins only, or for everyone. |
| `--disable-signup` | `init`, `configure`, `update` | Sets `DISABLE_SIGNUP=true`. Nobody can sign up, the admin included. `env set DISABLE_SIGNUP=false` opens it again. |
| `--yes` | any | Accept defaults. Required for `uninstall` without a terminal. |
| `--json` | `status`, `doctor`, `plan`, `env ls` | Machine-readable output. |

## SDK

`@kortix/sdk` is the TypeScript client for the Kortix API. It creates projects and sessions, sends prompts, streams the agent's reply and reads the result. One package holds layers for servers, browsers, React and Generative UI, and `@kortix/ui` adds prebuilt components on top.

### What the SDK is

| Entry point | Runs in | Depends on | Use it when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [`@kortix/sdk`](/docs/developers#sessions-from-code) | Both | A global `fetch` | You call the API from any runtime: Node 18 or later, Bun, a browser, an edge worker. It holds the client and every framework-free module. |
| [`@kortix/sdk/server`](/docs/developers#sdk-authentication) | Server | `node:async_hooks` | One server acts for many users, signs people in with Kortix, or reads the viewer of a Kortix App. Never bundle it for a browser. |
| [`@kortix/sdk/react`](/docs/developers#react) | Browser | `react` 18 or later, `@tanstack/react-query` 5.75.2 or a later 5.x | You run a session in a React or React Native app. `useSession` owns the whole session. |
| [`@kortix/sdk/genui/fence`](/docs/developers#generative-ui) | Both | Nothing | You only detect and split `openui` blocks in agent text. It loads no parser. |
| [`@kortix/sdk/genui`](/docs/developers#generative-ui) | Both | `zod`, `@openuidev/lang-core` 0.3.1 | You parse blocks, or turn them into Markdown for a CLI, email or bot. |
| [`@kortix/sdk/genui/react`](/docs/developers#generative-ui) | Browser | `react`, `zod`, `@openuidev/lang-core` 0.3.1 | You render blocks with your own React components. |
| [`@kortix/ui`](/docs/developers#kortix-ui) | Browser | `@kortix/sdk`, `react` 19 or later, `react-dom` 19 or later, `@tanstack/react-query` | You want the Kortix session chat and components without building them. |

Every task guide is in a later H2: [SDK authentication](/docs/developers#sdk-authentication), [Sessions from code](/docs/developers#sessions-from-code), [Projects from code](/docs/developers#projects-from-code), [React](/docs/developers#react), [Generative UI](/docs/developers#generative-ui), [Kortix UI](/docs/developers#kortix-ui). Exact signatures are in the [SDK reference](/docs/developers#sdk-reference).

### Install

```bash
npm install @kortix/sdk
```

The package is ESM only. Add the optional peers of the layers you use:

| Layer | Install |
| --- | --- |
| `@kortix/sdk/react` | `npm install react @tanstack/react-query` |
| `@kortix/sdk/genui`, `@kortix/sdk/genui/react` | `npm install zod @openuidev/lang-core@0.3.1` |
| `@kortix/ui` | `npm install @kortix/ui`, at the same version as `@kortix/sdk`. See [Kortix UI](/docs/developers#kortix-ui). |

A page with no build step loads the browser bundle. It defines the global `Kortix` with the same names as the root entry point, for example `Kortix.createKortix` and `Kortix.narrowChatEvent`:

```html
<script src="https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@kortix/sdk@<version>/dist/kortix.global.js"></script>
```

Replace `<version>` with a published version. The package also ships an ES module bundle at `dist/kortix.esm.min.js`. Load the global bundle or the ESM build on one page, never both. With both, the page has two `ApiError` classes and `instanceof ApiError` fails.

A browser calls the Kortix API directly only from an origin the API allows. Kortix Cloud allows its own origins. From your own site, send browser calls through your server, for example the same-origin proxy of [Sign in with Kortix](/docs/developers#sign-in-with-kortix). A self-hosted instance adds origins with the API's `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` setting.

### Create the client

Call `createKortix` once per process, with the API base URL and a function that returns a token.

```ts
import { createKortix } from '@kortix/sdk';

export const kortix = createKortix({
  backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
  getToken: async () => process.env.KORTIX_API_KEY ?? null,
});
```

| Option | Type | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `backendUrl` | `string`, required | The API base URL, including `/v1`. Kortix Cloud is `https://api.kortix.com/v1`. A self-hosted instance is its API origin plus `/v1`. |
| `getToken` | `() => Promise<string \| null>`, required | Returns the credential, or `null` when no one is signed in. The SDK calls it on every request and stores nothing. Your host owns storage and refresh. A getter that caches can carry `invalidate(rejectedToken)`: the SDK calls it on a `401` before its one replay. See [SDK authentication](/docs/developers#sdk-authentication). |
| `credentialHeader` | `string` | Sends the token raw in this header instead of `Authorization: Bearer`. For a proxy that reads a custom header. |
| `clientVersion` | `string` | Your surface and version as `<surface>/<version>`, for example `my-app/1.2.0`. The SDK sends it as `X-Kortix-Client-Version`. The API writes it to the request log only. It grants nothing. |
| `fetch` | fetch-shaped function | A fetch implementation for tests, edge runtimes and adapters. Default: the global `fetch`. |
| `eventStreamTransport` | `RuntimeEventTransport` | Delivers the event stream's bytes when your `fetch` cannot stream a response body, for example React Native without `expo/fetch`. The SDK keeps reconnect, resume and heartbeat. Default: the configured `fetch`, read as a stream. |
| `getUserId` | `() => Promise<string \| null>` | Returns the current user id. The SDK scopes the offline message cache by it. |
| `billingEnabled` | `boolean` | Turns on the billing gates of `@kortix/ui`: upgrade and balance prompts. Default: off. |
| `sandboxId` | `string \| null` | The default sandbox id for a host with a single local sandbox. |
| `featureFlags` | `KortixFeatureFlagOverrides` | Per-flag overrides for a host without `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` build-time variables, such as React Native or a CLI. |
| `onError` | `(error, context?) => void` | An error sink for a toast or a log. Default: no-op. |
| `onAccessRemedy` | `(code: 'sso_required' \| 'account_mfa_required') => void` | Runs when the API refuses a request until the person signs in through single sign-on or completes multi-factor authentication. It runs for REST responses, not for the session runtime or its event stream. A browser also gets the `kortix:sso-required` and `kortix:mfa-required` window events. Default: no-op. |
| `onToast` | `(level, message, options?) => void` | A toast sink. `level` is `'info'`, `'success'`, `'error'` or `'warning'`. Your host renders it. |
| `onNotify` | `(event: { kind, sessionId, ... }) => void` | An OS or web notification sink. Your host renders it. The React layer sends turn-end notices here. |

<Callout type="warning">
The client holds one configuration for the whole process. A second `createKortix()` call with a different `backendUrl` or `getToken` re-points every earlier client and logs one console warning. A server that acts for many users at the same time creates one client per request with `createScopedKortix()` from `@kortix/sdk/server`. See [Serve many users from one backend](/docs/developers#serve-many-users-from-one-backend).
</Callout>

The client's handles (`kortix.projects`, `kortix.project(projectId)`, `kortix.session(projectId, sessionId)`, `kortix.auth`, `kortix.accounts` and more) are listed in the [SDK reference](/docs/developers#sdk-reference). `kortix.validateToken()` checks the token `getToken` returns now and resolves `{ valid, identity?, error? }`. It never throws.

### Get an API key

A script, a CI job or a server authenticates with a personal access key. The key starts with `kortix_pat_`. Create one in the web app under **Settings → Personal access keys**, or with the CLI:

```bash
kortix tokens new my-script --expires 90d
```

Kortix shows the key once. Store it as a secret and return it from `getToken`. Unattended automation that acts for no single person uses a service account key (`kortix_sa_`, **Account settings → API keys**).

<Callout type="warning">
A personal access key acts as you. Keep it on a server. Never put it in a browser bundle, a mobile app or a public repository. A browser app signs people in with [Sign in with Kortix](/docs/developers#sign-in-with-kortix) instead. Every credential and where it runs: [Choose a credential](/docs/developers#choose-a-credential).
</Callout>

### Your first session

This script creates a session in your first project, waits until it is ready, sends one prompt, streams the events until the turn ends, and prints the agent's final text.

```ts title="first-session.mts"
import { createKortix, narrowChatEvent } from '@kortix/sdk';

const kortix = createKortix({
  backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
  getToken: async () => process.env.KORTIX_API_KEY ?? null,
});

const [project] = await kortix.projects.list();
if (!project) throw new Error('No project. Create one with kortix.projects.provision({ name }).');
const { session_id } = await kortix.project(project.project_id).sessions.create({ name: 'First session' });
const session = kortix.session(project.project_id, session_id);
const { runtimeSessionId } = await session.ensureReady();

const replyIds = new Set<string>();
const textParts = new Map<string, { messageID: string; text: string }>();
let finish!: (error?: unknown) => void;
const turnEnded = new Promise<unknown>((resolve) => (finish = resolve));

const stream = await session.stream({
  onEvent: (raw) => {
    const e = narrowChatEvent(raw);
    if (!e || ('sessionID' in e && e.sessionID && e.sessionID !== runtimeSessionId)) return;
    if (e.type === 'message.updated' && e.message.role === 'assistant') replyIds.add(e.message.id);
    if (e.type === 'message.part.updated' && e.part.type === 'text') textParts.set(e.part.id, e.part);
    if (e.type === 'session.idle' && replyIds.size > 0) finish();
    if (e.type === 'session.error') finish(e.error ?? 'session.error');
  },
});

await session.send('List the top-level files in this repository.');
const error = await turnEnded;
stream.close();
if (error) throw error;
for (const part of textParts.values()) if (replyIds.has(part.messageID)) console.log(part.text);
```

Run it with Bun or `tsx`:

```bash
KORTIX_API_KEY=kortix_pat_... bun first-session.mts
KORTIX_API_KEY=kortix_pat_... npx tsx first-session.mts
```

What each step does:

1. `sessions.create()` returns the session row with its `session_id`. The sandbox starts to provision in the background.
2. `ensureReady()` resolves when the runtime accepts work. It returns `runtimeSessionId`, the id of the root conversation. It waits up to 180 seconds by default.
3. `stream()` opens before `send()`, so the script misses no early event.
4. `send()` resolves when the prompt is stored, not when the turn ends.
5. `narrowChatEvent()` reduces raw runtime events to the chat events. Each `message.part.updated` event carries the whole part, so the map keeps the latest text of each part.
6. The turn ends on `session.idle` for `runtimeSessionId`, after an assistant message exists. `session.error` ends it with an error.

Each run creates a new session, and the session appears in the web app with the same transcript. Answers to questions and permission requests, files, errors and retries are in [Sessions from code](/docs/developers#sessions-from-code).

### Versions and stability

- `@kortix/sdk` publishes to npm with each Kortix platform release and carries the release version.
- These entry points follow semver: `@kortix/sdk`, `/react`, `/server`, `/genui`, `/genui/fence`, `/genui/react`, `/wire-message-id`, `/workspace-search` and `/capture-rules`.
- Exported names, types included, are the public contract. A renamed export keeps a `@deprecated` alias until the next major version.
- `@kortix/sdk/internal/*` serves the Kortix web app and has no semver. It can change in any release.
- Other subpaths, such as `@kortix/sdk/turns` and `@kortix/sdk/projects-client`, are deprecated aliases of root names. They resolve until the next major version. The list is in the [SDK reference](/docs/developers#sdk-reference).
- A runtime does not serve every session control. Check a control with `runtimeSupports` before you show it: see [Gate on runtime capabilities](/docs/developers#gate-on-runtime-capabilities).
- Changes per release are in the [CHANGELOG](https://github.com/kortix-ai/suna/blob/dev/packages/sdk/CHANGELOG.md). Runnable examples are in [packages/sdk/examples](https://github.com/kortix-ai/suna/tree/dev/packages/sdk/examples) on GitHub. The npm package does not include them.

## SDK authentication

Every SDK request carries one bearer credential. `createKortix` gets it from your
`getToken` function. `backendUrl` names the API that receives it. This section
lists every source of those two values. Pick a row in the matrix, then read its
subsection. The client options themselves are in
[Create the client](/docs/developers#sdk).

On Kortix Cloud, `backendUrl` is `https://api.kortix.com/v1`. On a self-hosted
instance, it is `<your API origin>/v1`.

### Choose a credential

A credential holds the roles of the principal it acts as, and nothing more.

| Caller | Credential | Runs in | `getToken` returns | Read |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A script, a CI job, your own backend | Personal access key, `kortix_pat_` | Server only | The key from an environment variable | [getToken and 401 recovery](#gettoken-and-401-recovery) |
| A web app that signs people in through the instance's Supabase | Supabase JWT | Browser or server | `(await supabase.auth.getSession()).data.session?.access_token ?? null` | [Sign users in headlessly](#sign-users-in-headlessly) for new code |
| Your own web app with a "Sign in with Kortix" button | OAuth access token, `kortix_oat_` (1 hour; refresh token `kortix_ort_`, 30 days) | Server holds it; the browser sends a placeholder | `auth.kortix(request)` on the server; `'kortix-session'` in the browser | [Sign in with Kortix](#sign-in-with-kortix) |
| Code inside a Kortix App | App viewer token | Browser of the App, or the App's server | `kortixAppViewerToken()` in the browser; `createAppViewerKortix(request, …)` on the server | [Know the viewer in a Kortix App](#know-the-viewer-in-a-kortix-app) |
| One server process that acts for many users at the same time | Any of the above, per request | Server only | A function of the current request | [Serve many users from one backend](#serve-many-users-from-one-backend) |

Two more credentials exist. A service account key, `kortix_sa_`, acts as a
service account for unattended automation. It has no access until you assign
it a role. Create one under **Account settings → API keys**, or with
`kortix tokens service-accounts new`. Kortix sets `KORTIX_TOKEN` and
`KORTIX_API_URL` inside a session sandbox. They act as the session's agent,
limited to that project and session. Their permissions are the agent's
`kortix_permissions`, capped by its ceiling role (see
[What an agent may do](/docs/guide#agents)).
Creation, expiry, project binding and key rules for every credential are in
[Security](/docs/guide#security). A valid credential without the needed permission
gets `403`. Grant the role in [Teams](/docs/guide#teams).

<Callout type="warning">
  A `kortix_pat_` or `kortix_sa_` key never ships to a browser. Keep it on a
  server and give browser code a proxy:
  [Sign in with Kortix](#sign-in-with-kortix) for people,
  [`forwardKortixRequest`](#serve-many-users-from-one-backend) for a key your
  server holds.
</Callout>

### getToken and 401 recovery

The SDK calls `getToken` before every request. It caches nothing, so your code
owns token storage and refresh.

- `getToken` returns `null`: the SDK sends no request. A typed call such as
  `kortix.projects.list()` throws `AuthError` with `code: 'NO_SESSION'`.
  `kortix.validateToken()` resolves `{ valid: false, error }` instead.
- The SDK sends the token as `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. With
  `credentialHeader` set (for example `'x-app-reader-token'`, for a proxy that
  reads a custom header), it sends the raw token in that header and sends no
  `Authorization`. The `401` replay still calls `getToken` again. A header of
  the same name that a call sets itself wins.
- The API answers `401`: the SDK calls `getToken.invalidate(rejectedToken)`
  when it exists, calls `getToken` again, and replays the request once. A
  second `401` goes to the caller.
- Parallel `401`s on one token share one `invalidate` call and one `getToken`
  call. A host that rotates its refresh token on each call does not race
  itself.
- The SDK does not replay when the new token equals the rejected one, when the
  request body is a `ReadableStream`, or when the call set its own
  `Authorization` header.

A `getToken` that caches must attach `invalidate`. Without it, the second call
returns the same dead token and the SDK does not replay.

```ts
import { createKortix } from '@kortix/sdk';

declare function fetchTokenFromMyServer(): Promise<string | null>;

let cached: string | null = null;

const getToken = Object.assign(
  async () => (cached ??= await fetchTokenFromMyServer()),
  {
    invalidate: (rejected: string) => {
      if (cached === rejected) cached = null;
    },
  },
);

const kortix = createKortix({ backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1', getToken });
```

To branch on an auth failure, call `isAuthFailure(error)`. It returns `true`
for `AuthError` and for any error with `status` `401` or `403`. It reads the
status, never the message text.

```ts
import { createKortix, isAuthFailure } from '@kortix/sdk';

declare function redirectToSignIn(): void;

const kortix = createKortix({
  backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
  getToken: async () => localStorage.getItem('kortix-token'),
});

try {
  await kortix.projects.list();
} catch (error) {
  if (isAuthFailure(error)) redirectToSignIn();
  else throw error;
}
```

To check a credential without a try block, call `kortix.validateToken()`. It
reads `GET /accounts/me` and never throws. It resolves to
`{ valid: true, identity }` or `{ valid: false, error }`.

`clientVersion` (for example `'my-app/1.4.0'`) goes out as
`X-Kortix-Client-Version`. Kortix writes it to request logs only. It grants
nothing. The audit log records the credential that `getToken` returned.

### Sign users in headlessly

`kortix.auth.*` signs people up and in through the Kortix API at `/v1/auth/*`.
It needs no Supabase URL or key. It works the same on Kortix Cloud and on a
self-hosted instance. Use it for a CLI, a native app, or your own sign-in
screen.

Create one session store and one client. The store keeps the tokens, refreshes
them 60 s before they expire, and feeds `getToken`.

```ts
import { createKortix, createKortixSession } from '@kortix/sdk';

const session = createKortixSession({
  storage: {
    get: () => localStorage.getItem('kortix'),
    set: (value) => localStorage.setItem('kortix', value),
    remove: () => localStorage.removeItem('kortix'),
  },
});

const kortix = createKortix({
  backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
  getToken: session.getToken,
});

const { session: tokens, user } = await kortix.auth.signInWithPassword({
  email: 'ada@example.test',
  password: 'correct-horse-battery',
});
await session.set(tokens, user);

await kortix.projects.list(); // runs as that person
```

Create the store with `createKortixSession`. Do not create a second
`createKortix` client to reach `kortix.auth.session`: every `createKortix` call
re-points the one process-wide config.

#### Sign-in flows

| Flow | Calls |
|---|---|
| Sign up | `auth.signUp({ email, password, data?, redirect_to? })` returns `{ user, session, requires_email_confirmation }`. When `requires_email_confirmation` is `true`, `session` is `null` until the person confirms with the emailed link or with `verifyOtp({ type: 'signup' })`. |
| Password | `auth.signInWithPassword({ email, password })` |
| Magic link or code | `auth.sendMagicLink({ email, create_user?, redirect_to?, data? })`, then `auth.verifyOtp({ email, token, type: 'magiclink' })` with the code from the email |
| Social login (PKCE) | `auth.signInWithProvider({ provider, redirect_to, scopes? })` returns `{ url, code_verifier }`. Keep `code_verifier`. Send the browser to `url`. Then call `auth.exchangeCode({ code, code_verifier })` with the `code` from the redirect. `provider` is one the instance has configured, for example `google` or `github`. |
| Enterprise SSO | `auth.signInWithSso({ domain, redirect_to? })` returns `{ url }`. A `404` means the domain has no identity provider. See [Security](/docs/guide#security). |
| Password reset | `auth.resetPassword({ email, redirect_to? })`, then `auth.verifyOtp({ email, token, type: 'recovery' })`, then `auth.updatePassword({ password }, accessToken)` with the access token from that session. The password is 8 to 256 characters. |

Every flow that returns a session returns `{ session, user }`. Pass both to
`session.set(...)`. `redirect_to` is an absolute `http` or `https` URL that the
instance allows.

#### The signed-in person

These calls take the access token as an argument, so they work before
`getToken` is wired. `auth.refresh` takes the refresh token instead.
`auth.user`, `auth.updateUserMetadata`, `auth.updatePassword` and the
`auth.mfa.*` calls refuse any other credential, such as a personal access key,
with `401 invalid_token`. `auth.signOut` always answers `200`.

| Call | Does |
|---|---|
| `auth.user(accessToken)` | Returns `{ user }` |
| `auth.updateUserMetadata({ data }, accessToken)` | Writes profile metadata: display name, avatar, language. It refuses a password or email change. |
| `auth.updatePassword({ password }, accessToken)` | Sets a new password |
| `auth.mfa.enroll({ factor_type, friendly_name?, phone?, issuer? }, accessToken)` | Enrolls a `totp` or `phone` factor |
| `auth.mfa.challenge(factorId, { channel? }, accessToken)` | Starts a challenge |
| `auth.mfa.verify(factorId, { challenge_id, code }, accessToken)` | Verifies the code. Returns the new token pair (`access_token`, `refresh_token`, `expires_at`) at the top level, not as `{ session, user }`. Store it with `session.set(result as unknown as AuthSession)`. |
| `auth.mfa.unenroll(factorId, accessToken)` | Removes a factor |
| `auth.refresh({ refresh_token })` | Rotates the token pair. The session store calls it for you. |
| `auth.signOut(accessToken, { scope? })` | Revokes the session at Kortix. `scope`: `global` (default, every device), `local` (this session), `others` (every other session). |
