To sign out, revoke the session, then clear the store:

```ts
const token = await session.getToken();
if (token) await kortix.auth.signOut(token);
await session.clear();
```

#### The session store

`kortix.auth.session(options?)` is the same function as
`createKortixSession(options?)`. Each call creates a new store. Create one and
keep it.

| Option | Default | Does |
|---|---|---|
| `storage` | none (memory only) | `{ get, set, remove }`, sync or async: `localStorage`, a file, a cookie jar, a database row |
| `onChange` | none | Runs after every sign-in, refresh and sign-out |
| `refreshSkewSeconds` | `60` | Refreshes this many seconds before expiry |
| `request` | the client's `backendUrl` | `{ backendUrl?, fetch?, signal? }` for refresh calls |

| Method | Does |
|---|---|
| `getToken()` | A valid access token, refreshed first when needed. `null` when signed out. |
| `set(session, user?)` | Stores a session that a `kortix.auth.*` call returned |
| `current()`, `user()` | The stored session and user. No refresh. |
| `refresh()` | Rotates now. Concurrent callers share one refresh. |
| `subscribe(listener)` | Calls `listener` on every change. Returns an unsubscribe function. |
| `load()` | Reads storage now instead of on first use |
| `clear()` | Forgets the session locally. Call `auth.signOut` first to revoke it. |

`session.getToken` has no `invalidate`. A `401` on a token that has not expired
goes to the caller. Handle it with `isAuthFailure`. A refresh that Kortix
rejects with a `4xx` signs the person out: the store clears itself and
`getToken()` returns `null`. A network failure keeps the stored session for the
next call.

#### Errors and limits

Every `kortix.auth.*` call throws `HeadlessAuthError` with `code`, `message` and
`status`.

| `code` | `status` | Cause |
|---|---|---|
| `sso_required` | `403` | The email domain must sign in with SSO. Sign-up, password, magic link and `verifyOtp` refuse it. Use `signInWithSso`. |
| `signup_closed` | `403` | The instance does not accept sign-ups from this address |
| `over_request_rate_limit` | `429` | Too many attempts. Wait for the `retry-after` header. |
| `invalid_token` | `401` | A call that needs the person's access token got another credential |
| `not_configured` | `0` | No `backendUrl`. Create the client first. |

Every `/v1/auth/*` route that takes no access token shares one limit: 30
attempts per minute per client IP, counted on each API replica. Other codes come unchanged from the
identity service.

### Sign in with Kortix

Sign in with Kortix makes Kortix the identity provider for a web app you run.
People sign in with their Kortix account. Your server knows who they are. Your
app calls Kortix as that person, with that person's roles. The protocol is
OAuth 2.1 with PKCE. The browser never holds a Kortix token.

Register the app first. The fields are in
[OAuth client registration](#oauth-client-registration).

<Steps>
  <Step title="Create the auth kit on your server">
    ```ts
    // lib/kortix-auth.ts (server only)
    import { createKortixAuth } from '@kortix/sdk/server';

    export const auth = createKortixAuth({
      backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
      clientId: process.env.KORTIX_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID!,
      clientSecret: process.env.KORTIX_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET, // omit for a public client
      redirectUri: 'https://dashboard.example.test/api/kortix/auth/callback',
      cookieSecret: process.env.KORTIX_AUTH_COOKIE_SECRET!, // 32 characters or more
    });
    ```
  </Step>
  <Step title="Mount one catch-all route">
    ```ts
    // app/api/kortix/auth/[...kortix]/route.ts (Next.js App Router)
    import { auth } from '@/lib/kortix-auth';

    const handle = (request: Request) => auth.handler(request);
    export { handle as GET, handle as POST };
    ```

    Any framework works. Pass a Web `Request` to `auth.handler` and return its
    `Response`.
  </Step>
  <Step title="Gate pages and call Kortix as the person">
    ```ts
    import { auth } from '@/lib/kortix-auth';

    export async function GET(request: Request) {
      const gate = await auth.requireViewer(request);
      if (gate.response) return gate.response; // 302 to /refresh or /signin

      const kortix = await auth.kortix(request); // acts as the signed-in person
      return Response.json(await kortix.projects.list());
    }
    ```

    `requireViewer` sends a person whose session cookie holds an expired
    access token to `/refresh`. A person with no session cookie goes to
    `/signin`.
  </Step>
  <Step title="Call Kortix from the browser">
    ```tsx
    import { createKortix } from '@kortix/sdk';
    import { SignInWithKortix, useKortixViewer } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

    // The same values auth.clientConfig() returns on the server.
    createKortix({
      backendUrl: window.location.origin + '/api/kortix/auth/proxy',
      getToken: async () => 'kortix-session',
    });

    export function Header() {
      const { status, viewer } = useKortixViewer();
      if (status === 'signed-in') return <span>{viewer.email}</span>;
      return <SignInWithKortix className="button" />;
    }
    ```

    The browser sends the placeholder bearer `kortix-session`
    (`KORTIX_SESSION_SENTINEL`). The `/proxy` route replaces it with the
    person's token on your server. Every SDK call works through it, including
    [`useSession`](/docs/developers#react).
  </Step>
</Steps>

<Callout type="warning">
  Never import `@kortix/sdk/server`, or a module that imports it such as
  `lib/kortix-auth.ts`, into browser code. It pulls in `node:async_hooks`.
  `auth.clientConfig(origin?)` returns
  `{ backendUrl: '<origin><basePath>/proxy', getToken }` on the server. In the
  browser, write the same two values literally, as in step 4.
</Callout>

#### createKortixAuth options

| Option | Default | Does |
|---|---|---|
| `backendUrl` | required | Kortix API base, including `/v1` |
| `clientId` | required | The registered client id |
| `clientSecret` | none | The client secret. Omit it for a public client. |
| `redirectUri` | required | Absolute callback URL, byte-identical to a registered one |
| `cookieSecret` | required | 32 characters or more. Encrypts the cookies with AES-256-GCM. |
| `scopes` | `['profile', 'email', 'kortix']` | Scopes to request |
| `basePath` | the `redirectUri` path without `/callback` | Where `handler` is mounted |
| `sessionTtlSeconds` | `2592000` (30 days) | How long a session lives without a new sign-in |
| `cookieSameSite` | `'lax'` | `'none'` only for an app that signs in while embedded in another origin. It needs `https`. Over `http` the cookie stays `Lax`. |
| `fetch` | global `fetch` | Custom fetch for tests or edge runtimes |

The kit treats an access token as expired 30 s before Kortix does, so a call
in flight never lands on a dead token.

`createKortixAuth` throws `KortixAuthError` with code `invalid_options` when
`backendUrl` or `clientId` is empty, `redirectUri` is not an absolute `http` or
`https` URL, or `cookieSecret` is shorter than 32 characters. `auth.kortix`
throws `KortixAuthError` with code `unauthenticated` when nobody is signed in.
The handler throws nothing. It reports `state_mismatch` and
`token_exchange_failed` as the `error` field of a JSON response.

| Scope | Grants your app |
|---|---|
| `profile` | The person's id, email and account memberships |
| `email` | The email address |
| `kortix` | Acting as the person on the whole Kortix API. Without it, the token reaches only the identity routes. |

#### Routes the handler serves

All paths are under `basePath`, `/api/kortix/auth` in the example.

| Path | Does |
|---|---|
| `/signin?return_to=/path` | Starts sign-in with PKCE and a 10-minute transaction cookie, then redirects to Kortix |
| `/callback` | Exchanges the code, sets the encrypted `HttpOnly` session cookie, redirects to `return_to` |
| `/refresh?return_to=/path` | Rotates the token pair and redirects. `requireViewer` sends people here. |
| `/signout?return_to=/path` | Revokes the refresh token at Kortix and clears the cookie |
| `/me` | The person as JSON, or `401`. Refreshes an expired access token. |
| `/proxy/*` | Forwards the request to the Kortix API as the person |

`return_to` is always reduced to a same-origin path (`safeReturnTo`). When the
person declines consent, the callback redirects to `return_to` with
`?kortix_auth_error=<code>`. A missing or expired transaction answers `400`
with `error: 'state_mismatch'`. A failed code exchange answers with
`error: 'token_exchange_failed'`.

#### Server and browser API

| From | API | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| `@kortix/sdk/server` | `auth.viewer(request)` | `{ userId, email, accounts, scopes, token, expiresAt }` or `null`. Read-only: it never rotates the refresh token. |
| `@kortix/sdk/server` | `auth.requireViewer(request)` | `{ viewer }`, or `{ response }` with a redirect to `/refresh` or `/signin` |
| `@kortix/sdk/server` | `auth.kortix(request)` | A request-scoped client that acts as the person. Throws `KortixAuthError` `unauthenticated` when signed out. |
| `@kortix/sdk/server` | `auth.signInUrl(returnTo?)`, `auth.signOutUrl(returnTo?)` | Link targets |
| `@kortix/sdk/server` | `auth.clientConfig(origin?)` | The browser `createKortix` config: `backendUrl` is `/proxy`, `getToken` yields `kortix-session` |
| `@kortix/sdk/react` | `useKortixViewer({ basePath? })` | `{ status, viewer, reload }`. `status` is `loading`, `signed-in`, `signed-out` or `error`. |
| `@kortix/sdk/react` | `<SignInWithKortix basePath? returnTo? />` | A link that starts sign-in and returns to the current page |
| `@kortix/sdk/react` | `kortixSignOutHref(basePath?, returnTo?)` | The sign-out link target |

The React helpers default `basePath` to `/api/kortix/auth`. Pass `basePath`
when you mount `handler` elsewhere.

#### Consent and revocation

The first sign-in shows a Kortix consent screen with your app's name and
scopes. Kortix remembers the approval per person and app. Later sign-ins
return straight to your app. A person revokes an app with
`kortix.accounts.connectedApps.list()` and `.revoke(clientId)`, or on the web
and in the CLI as [Security](/docs/guide#security) describes. After a revoke, the
app's tokens stop working and the app must ask again.

#### Discovery for other OAuth clients

The SDK derives every endpoint from `backendUrl`. A generic OAuth client reads
`GET /.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` on the API origin. The same
document is at `/v1/oauth/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server`. Dynamic
client registration is at `POST /v1/oauth/register`. Kortix deletes a
self-registered client after 7 days when it has no approval and no token.

#### OAuth client registration

Register in **Account → Tokens → OAuth apps**, or with
`kortix.iam.oauthClients.create(accountId, input)`. Registration needs
`token.create` on the account. `kortix.iam.oauthClients` also has `list`,
`get`, `update`, `rotateSecret` and `remove`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `name` | Shown on the consent screen |
| `client_type` | `'confidential'` (default) for a server that keeps a secret. `'public'` for a browser or native app: PKCE, no secret. |
| `redirect_uris` | Compared byte for byte. `https` only, except on a loopback host (`localhost`, `*.localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`). |
| `scopes` | Any of `profile`, `email`, `kortix`. Default: all three. |
| Result | `client_id`. `client_secret` is shown once. |

### Know the viewer in a Kortix App

An App that Kortix hosts needs no sign-in of its own. The Apps gate signs the
visitor in before the App serves its first byte. The App asks the gate who the
visitor is. The App's viewer setting decides what it gets:

| `kortix apps access <app> --viewer` | The App gets |
|---|---|
| `off` | No viewer |
| `identity` | Who the viewer is: user id, email, name, groups, role |
| `api` | Identity, plus a token that acts as the viewer on the Kortix API, capped by their role |

The viewer token is not the person's Kortix session. It expires in 1 hour. It
carries `profile email`, plus `kortix` on an `api` App. It dies with the App.
An App on its own domain has no gate: use
[Sign in with Kortix](#sign-in-with-kortix) there.

#### In the browser

`/_kortix/api/v1/*` on the App's own origin forwards each call to the Kortix
API as the viewer. The browser makes no cross-origin request.

```tsx
import { createKortix, kortixAppViewerToken } from '@kortix/sdk';
import { useKortixAppViewer } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

createKortix({
  backendUrl: '/_kortix/api/v1',
  getToken: kortixAppViewerToken(),
});

export function Greeting() {
  const { status, viewer } = useKortixAppViewer();
  if (status === 'loading') return null;
  if (status === 'anonymous') return <span>Not signed in</span>;
  return <span>{viewer.name ?? viewer.email}</span>;
}
```

| API | Does |
|---|---|
| `kortixAppViewerToken({ path?, fetch? })` | A `getToken` with `invalidate`. Yields the viewer token, or `null` when nobody is signed in or the App is `identity`. Refetches 60 s before expiry. |
| `fetchKortixAppViewer({ path?, fetch? })` | `GET /_kortix/viewer`. Returns `{ user_id, email, name, picture, group_ids, groups, role, account_id, project_id, app_id, access_mode, scopes, access_token, expires_at }` or `null`. Concurrent callers share one request. |
| `useKortixAppViewer({ path? })` | `{ status: 'loading' \| 'anonymous' \| 'viewer', viewer }` |
| `clearKortixAppViewerCache()` | Drops the cached viewer and tokens, for example after sign-out |

`fetchKortixAppViewer` never throws. Any non-`2xx` gate answer yields `null`.
The gate sends these two:

| Status | `error` | Cause |
|---|---|---|
| `401` | `no_viewer_identity` | Nobody is signed in, or the viewer has no access to the App |
| `404` | `viewer_disabled` | The App's viewer setting is `off` |

An agent session gets its identity with `access_token: null`. The gate never
gives an agent a token that acts as the person who started it.

#### On the App's server

The gate signs the viewer into every proxied request as the
`x-kortix-app-viewer` header (`APP_VIEWER_HEADER`). An `api` App also gets the
token in `x-kortix-app-viewer-token` (`APP_VIEWER_TOKEN_HEADER`). The gate
deletes a client-supplied copy. The App verifies the header with
`KORTIX_APP_VIEWER_SECRET` (`APP_VIEWER_SECRET_ENV`), which Kortix sets at
deploy. Without the secret, every function below finds no viewer.

```ts
import { createAppViewerKortix, createKortixAppGuard } from '@kortix/sdk/server';

const guard = createKortixAppGuard();

export async function GET(request: Request) {
  const gate = await guard.requireGroup(request, ['Finance']);
  if (gate.response) return gate.response; // 404 { error: 'not_found' }

  const kortix = await createAppViewerKortix(request, {
    backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
  });
  return Response.json({
    viewer: gate.viewer.userId,
    projects: await kortix.projects.list(),
  });
}
```

| API (`@kortix/sdk/server`) | Does |
|---|---|
| `readAppViewer(request, { secret? })` | The verified viewer `{ userId, email, name, picture, groupIds, groups, role, projectId, accountId, appId, accessMode, token, expiresAt }`, or `null`. One HMAC check, no network call. |
| `createAppViewerKortix(request, { backendUrl, secret? })` | A request-scoped client that acts as the viewer. Throws `AppViewerUnavailableError` with code `no_viewer` (no verified viewer) or `identity_only` (the App is not `api`). |
| `createKortixAppGuard(options?)` | One resolver for the gate header and, with `auth` from `createKortixAuth`, the App's own sign-in. Returns `{ viewer, requireViewer, requireGroup, identityConfigured }`. |
| `guard.requireViewer(request)` | `{ viewer }`, or `{ response }`: a `302` to sign-in when `auth` is set, else `404`. |
| `guard.requireGroup(request, groups)` | `{ viewer }` when the viewer is in any of `groups` (ids or names), else `{ response }` `404`. An empty list refuses everyone. |

`createKortixAppGuard` options: `secret`, `backendUrl` (for the group lookup on
the sign-in path), `auth`, `accountId` (default `KORTIX_APP_ACCOUNT_ID`),
`groupCacheTtlMs` (default `60000`), `fetch`. A failed group lookup yields no
groups, so a group gate denies.

#### Member tokens for App-to-App calls

A member token is a 15-minute ES256 token that names the viewer for one App.
Its `aud` is the App id. One App uses it to call another App that it lists in
`uses`. The functions come from `@kortix/sdk` and run in a browser, Node 18+,
Bun, Deno and a Worker.

```ts
// Browser of the calling App
import { kortixBinding } from '@kortix/sdk';

const api = kortixBinding('api');
const res = await fetch(`${api.url}/orders`, {
  headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${await api.token()}` },
});
```

```ts
// Server of the called App
import { KortixMemberError, requireKortixMember, verifyKortixToken } from '@kortix/sdk';

export async function GET(request: Request) {
  const bearer = request.headers.get('authorization')?.replace(/^Bearer /, '') ?? '';
  try {
    const member = requireKortixMember(await verifyKortixToken(bearer), { groups: ['Finance'] });
    return Response.json({ userId: member.userId });
  } catch (error) {
    if (error instanceof KortixMemberError) {
      return new Response(null, { status: error.code === 'forbidden' ? 403 : 401 });
    }
    throw error;
  }
}
```

| API (`@kortix/sdk`) | Does |
|---|---|
| `kortixToken({ audience?, path?, fetch? })` | A fetcher for `GET /_kortix/token`. `audience` is this App (default) or a slug or id from `uses`. Yields `null` with no viewer. `{ forceRefreshToken: true }` asks the gate again. |
| `kortixBinding(slug, { origin?, fetch? })` | `{ url, token }`. `url` is `<origin>/_kortix/apps/<slug>` for HTTP and WebSocket. Outside a browser, pass `origin`. |
| `verifyKortixToken(token, { jwks?, issuer?, audience?, fetch? })` | Checks signature, `exp` (60 s skew), issuer and audience. Defaults come from `KORTIX_AUTH_JWKS`, `KORTIX_AUTH_ISSUER`, `KORTIX_AUTH_AUDIENCE`. With no audience it refuses; `audience: false` accepts any App of the project. Returns a `KortixMember`. Throws `KortixMemberError` with code `unauthenticated` when any check fails. |
| `readKortixMember(claims)` | A `KortixMember` from claims something already verified, or `null`. It never checks a signature. |
| `requireKortixMember(claims, { groups?, roles? })` | The member, or throws `KortixMemberError`. An empty list refuses. |
| `KortixMemberError` | `code` is `unauthenticated` (map to `401`) or `forbidden` (map to `403`) |

A `KortixMember` is `{ userId, email, name, picture, groups, groupIds, role, accountId, projectId }`.
The token route answers `403 app_not_linked` for an App not in `uses` and
`403 agent_viewer` for an agent session. A binding to a static App answers
`409 app_binding_unsupported`. `kortixToken` yields `null` for every refusal
and logs one console warning for `app_not_linked`. The issuer, key
set and audience values are in [Apps reference](/docs/developers#apps-reference).

### Serve many users from one backend

`createKortix` stores its config in one process-wide slot. That is correct for
a browser tab, a CLI or a single-tenant job. On a server that handles requests
for different people at the same time, the last `createKortix` call wins for
every request in flight. Use `@kortix/sdk/server`. It isolates each request's
config in Node's `AsyncLocalStorage` and runs in Node and Bun.

**One client per request.** `createScopedKortix` takes the same options as
`createKortix` and never touches the process-wide config.

```ts
import { createScopedKortix } from '@kortix/sdk/server';

declare function tokenFor(request: Request): Promise<string | null>;

export async function GET(request: Request) {
  const kortix = createScopedKortix({
    backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
    getToken: () => tokenFor(request),
  });
  return Response.json(await kortix.projects.list());
}
```

A scoped client's top-level `kortix.runtime()` throws, because it would resolve
another request's sandbox. Work with a session through its handle,
`kortix.session(projectId, sessionId)`, and its verbs in
[Sessions from code](/docs/developers#sessions-from-code).

**One scope per request.** `runWithKortix(config, fn)` sets the config for
everything `fn` awaits, including calls on a client made with `createKortix`.
`getScopedConfig()` returns the config of the current scope, or `undefined`
outside one.

```ts
import { runWithKortix } from '@kortix/sdk/server';

declare function tokenFor(request: Request): Promise<string | null>;
declare function handleRequest(request: Request): Promise<Response>;

export function handle(request: Request): Promise<Response> {
  return runWithKortix(
    { backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1', getToken: () => tokenFor(request) },
    () => handleRequest(request),
  );
}
```

**A proxy for browser code.** `forwardKortixRequest({ request, upstreamUrl, token })`
forwards one request from your route to the Kortix API with a token your
server holds.

```ts
import { forwardKortixRequest } from '@kortix/sdk/server';

declare function tokenFor(request: Request): Promise<string>;

export async function handle(request: Request): Promise<Response> {
  const url = new URL(request.url);
  const rest = url.pathname.replace(/^\/api\/kortix\//, '');
  return forwardKortixRequest({
    request,
    upstreamUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1/' + rest + url.search,
    token: await tokenFor(request),
  });
}
```

It removes the browser's `cookie` and `host` headers, sets the bearer, keeps
streamed response bodies, and removes `set-cookie` from the answer. When Kortix
is unreachable, it answers `502` with `{ "error": "Upstream request failed" }`.
Point the browser client at the route with any non-null placeholder token:
`createKortix({ backendUrl: '/api/kortix', getToken: async () => 'proxy' })`.
The key stays on the server.

To start sessions for your users with secrets, connector bindings and an
`Idempotency-Key`, see [Sessions from code](/docs/developers#sessions-from-code).

## Sessions from code

A session is one agent run in its own sandbox, on its own git branch. This section drives a session from TypeScript with the framework-free `@kortix/sdk` root entry point. The steps are in lifecycle order: create, wait until ready, send, stream, get the reply, answer, gate on capabilities, control, files, and start sessions for your users. The one error catalog of the SDK is at the end.

Every sample uses the client from [SDK](/docs/developers#sdk). A React app uses `useSession` instead of these calls. See [React](/docs/developers#react).

### The session handle

```ts
const s = kortix.session(projectId, sessionId);
// The same handle: kortix.project(projectId).session(sessionId)
```

- Creating a handle sends no request.
- The session owns its runtime. A handle resolves only the sandbox of its own session. Handles for two different sessions never share a runtime. Handles for one session in one process share its resolved runtime.
- `session_id` is the id of the sandbox and the name of the session's git branch.
- `ensureReady()` returns `sandboxId`. That value is the provider's external id of the sandbox, not `session_id`.
- A session runs the pi runtime. The controls it does not serve are in [Gate on runtime capabilities](#gate-on-runtime-capabilities).

### Create a session

```ts
import { generateSessionId } from '@kortix/sdk';

const project = kortix.project(projectId);
const sessionId = generateSessionId(); // a v4 UUID, known before the create returns

const created = await project.sessions.create({
  session_id: sessionId,
  agent_name: 'support',
  initial_prompt: 'Triage the open issues labeled bug.',
  labels: ['triage'],
  metadata: { app_ticket: 'T-142' },
});
```

Every field is optional. With no input, the session runs the project's default agent and model and waits for a prompt. A create starts to provision the sandbox at once. Write a new secret value or connector credential before the create that needs it.

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `initial_prompt` | The first prompt. The server delivers it when the runtime is up. You do not call `send()` for it. |
| `agent_name` | The agent to run. Default: the project's default agent. |
| `model` | The model id (see [Models](/docs/guide#models)). A model the account cannot use returns `400`. |
| `name` | The session title. Without it, the server makes a title from the first prompt. |
| `labels` | Up to 20 labels, each 1 to 64 characters after trimming. Duplicates drop. Matching is exact and case-sensitive. |
| `metadata` | A JSON object of your own keys, at most 16,384 characters of JSON per write. A server-managed key, for example `source`, `trigger_kind` or `trigger_slug`, returns `400`. Prefix your keys, for example `app_`. |
| `session_id` | A v4 UUID from `generateSessionId()`. A second create with the same id returns `409` `session_already_exists`. |
| `base_ref` | The branch or ref the session branch starts from. Default: the project's default branch. |
| `sandbox_slug` | The sandbox to boot, by its slug in `kortix.yaml`. Default: the agent's sandbox, then the project default, then `default`. |
| `runtime_context`, `connector_bindings`, `inherit_unbound`, `secrets` | Per-customer context, connector accounts and secrets. See [Start sessions for your users](#start-sessions-for-your-users). |

`require_connectors` is accepted and ignored.

Change the name, labels or metadata later:

```ts
const s = kortix.session(projectId, created.session_id);
await s.update({ name: 'Bug triage', labels: ['triage', 'done'], metadata: { app_ticket: null } });

const { items, next_cursor } = await project.sessions.listPage({ labels: ['triage', 'done'] });
```

- `labels` replaces the whole list. `[]` clears it.
- `metadata` merges keys. A `null` value removes a key.
- `name: null` or `name: ''` returns the session to the automatic title.
- `listPage({ labels })` returns only sessions that carry every label. Pass `next_cursor` back as `cursor` for the next page. `list()` returns one page as a bare array.

The full `listPage` options are in [SDK reference](/docs/developers#sdk-reference).

### Wait until ready

```ts
const { runtimeSessionId, runtimeUrl, sandboxId } = await s.ensureReady({ readyTimeoutMs: 300_000 });
```

`ensureReady()` provisions or resumes the sandbox and waits for its runtime.

- It calls `POST .../start` in long polls of up to 30 s, with a 1 s pause, while the answer is `provisioning` or `starting` with `retriable: true`.
- The default deadline is `180_000` ms. Pass `readyTimeoutMs` to change it.
- At the deadline, or at once on a `failed` or `stopped` answer, it throws an `ApiError` with `code: 'RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE'`.
- A `4xx` answer other than `408` or `429` rejects at once with a `SessionStartError` (`status`, `code`, `terminal: true`). A `402` is one example.
- Concurrent calls for one session share one start request. A call on a ready handle returns at once.
- `runtimeSessionId` is the id of the root conversation in the runtime. Events carry it as `sessionID`.

Read the session row with `s.get()`. The row costs no sandbox wake-up. Pass it to `ensureReady()`: a `running` row that names its sandbox and `runtime_session_id` is used without a start request.

```ts
const row = await s.get();
const ready = await s.ensureReady({ session: row });
```

`s.start()` and `s.startOrThrow()` send one start request and return the raw `SessionStartResult` (`stage`, `retriable`, `sandbox`, `failure`). `start()` resolves `null` on a transient failure and rejects with a `SessionStartError` on any other `4xx`. `startOrThrow()` rejects with the `ApiError`, a `402` as `BillingError`. Use them to drive your own progress display. Use `ensureReady()` everywhere else.

| Behavior | Methods |
| --- | --- |
| Call `ensureReady()` themselves | `send`, `abort`, `rewind`, `restoreRewind`, `stream`, `messages`, `pending`, `answerPermission`, `answerQuestion`, `compact`, every `files.*` method |
| Throw `SessionNotReadyError` until the handle has a runtime | `previewUrl`, `proxyUrl`, `sandboxPortUrl` |
| Read only the Kortix API, never the sandbox | `get`, `update`, `cost`, `scope`, `log`, `turn`, `messageAuthors`, `modelUsage`, `prompts.*` |

`s.health()` never throws `SessionNotReadyError`. Before the handle has a runtime, it returns `status: 0`, `ok: false` and `health: null`.

A failed start is a cooldown, not a final verdict. A start after the cooldown tries again. The cooldown grows from 2 to 5 to 10 minutes. After 5 consecutive failures, `ensureReady()` throws `RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE` at once. That verdict expires 30 minutes after the last failure. `s.restart()` clears it at once.

### Send prompts

```ts
const { prompt_id, state, message_id } = await s.send('Refactor the auth module.');
```

- `send()` puts the prompt in the session's durable prompt inbox. The web app and the CLI write to the same inbox.
- `send()` resolves when the prompt is durable, not when the turn ends. Read the reply from the stream or from `s.messages()`.
- `send()` throws an `ApiError` with `code: 'PROMPT_FAILED'` when the inbox row already failed.
- No helper sends a prompt and waits for the reply. [Get the reply](#get-the-reply) shows the pattern.

Pick the model and agent per call or per handle:

```ts
await s.send('Write the tests.', { agent: 'tester' }); // this call only
s.setModel({ providerID: 'kortix', modelID: 'glm-5.3-flash' }); // later send() calls on this handle
s.setAgent('reviewer');
```

Precedence: the `send()` option, then `setModel()` / `setAgent()` on this handle, then the session's saved default. Model ids come from `kortix.projects.modelPicker(projectId)`. Agent names come from `kortix.projects.detail(projectId)`, in `config.agents`. To change the saved model, see [Control the session](#control-the-session).

#### Steer, queue or interrupt

`send()` queues: the prompt waits for the running turn to end. To choose how a prompt reaches a running turn, write it with `s.prompts.create()`:

```ts
import { mintWireMessageId } from '@kortix/sdk';

const messageId = mintWireMessageId();
await s.prompts.create({
  clientMessageId: messageId,
  messageId,
  remintOnDelivery: true,
  delivery: 'steer',
  parts: [{ type: 'text', text: 'Use the staging database.' }],
});
```

| `delivery` | While a turn runs |
| --- | --- |
| `steer` | The running turn reads the prompt at its next step. The turn does not stop. |
| `queue` | The prompt waits for the turn to end, then runs as its own turn. |
| `interrupt` | The turn ends after the running tool. Then the prompt runs as its own turn. |

With no turn running, all three start a turn. A `steer` needs the runtime capability `session.steer` and must come from the member who started the turn. Otherwise the server delivers it as `queue` and sets `steer_fallback` on the inbox row:

- `unsupported`: the runtime does not list `session.steer`.
- `not_prompter`: another member started the running turn.
- `turn_ended`: the turn ended before the prompt reached it.

#### The inbox

The inbox lists only the prompts that the session did not deliver yet.

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `s.prompts.list()` | `{ prompts }`: the waiting prompts, oldest first, each with `state`, `reason` and `steer_fallback`. |
| `s.prompts.edit(promptId, text)` | Replaces the text of a waiting prompt. `409` when the prompt is on its way to the runtime. |
| `s.prompts.interrupt(promptId)` | Turns a waiting prompt into an `interrupt`. |
| `s.prompts.retry(promptId)` | Retries a `failed` prompt. |
| `s.prompts.remove(promptId)` | Removes a prompt and returns it. `409` when the agent already reads it. |
| `s.prompts.hold(held)` | Holds (`true`) or releases (`false`) the whole queue. |

#### Attach files

Upload the file to the project, then send its id in a `file` part:

```ts
import { mintWireMessageId } from '@kortix/sdk';

const report = new File([reportBytes], 'report.pdf', { type: 'application/pdf' });
const upload = await kortix.project(projectId).attachments.upload(report);

const messageId = mintWireMessageId();
await s.prompts.create({
  clientMessageId: messageId,
  messageId,
  remintOnDelivery: true,
  parts: [
    { type: 'text', text: 'Summarize this report.' },
    { type: 'file', attachment_id: upload.attachment_id, filename: upload.filename, mime: upload.mime },
  ],
});
```

- Limits: 1 byte to 50 MiB per file, 100 MiB per message, 20 files per message.
- An upload needs no running sandbox. Before the prompt runs, the server writes each file to `/workspace/uploads/.kortix-inbox/`.
- An upload that no prompt uses expires after 24 hours (`expires_at`).
- A browser composer uses `usePromptAttachments` from `@kortix/sdk/react`.

### Stream events

```ts
const stream = await s.stream({
  onEvent: (event) => {
    /* one runtime event */
  },
  onGapRehydrate: (gapMs) => {
    /* re-read the state you cache */
  },
  signal: abortController.signal, // optional
});
stream.close();
```

`stream()` readies the session, then opens the runtime's event stream through the Kortix API. The transport is server-sent events over a streaming `fetch`. There is no WebSocket.

<Callout type="info">
Open the stream before you call `send()`. A stream that opens after the send can miss the first events of the turn.
</Callout>

Each raw event has `type` and `properties`. `narrowChatEvent(event)` maps the events a chat needs to the typed `KortixChatEvent` union and returns `null` for every other event:

| `KortixChatEvent.type` | When it fires | Payload |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `message.updated` / `message.removed` | A message changed or was deleted. | `message` / `messageID` |
| `message.part.updated` / `message.part.removed` | A part (text, tool call, file) changed or was removed. It carries the full part. | `part` / `messageID`, `partID` |
| `session.status` | A conversation's busy state changed. | `status` |
| `session.idle` | A turn finished. | `sessionID` |
| `session.error` | A turn failed. | `error` |
| `permission.asked` / `permission.replied` | The agent asked for a permission, and the answer. | `requestID`, `permission`, `patterns` / `reply` |
| `question.asked` / `question.answered` | The agent asked a question, and the answer. `question.answered` merges the wire's `question.replied` and `question.rejected`. | `requestID`, `questions` / `outcome`, `answers` |
| `todo.updated` | The agent's todo list changed. Only a runtime that lists `session.todo` sends it. | `todos` |
| `connection` | The stream connected (`server.connected`). | `status` |

Every event except `connection` and `heartbeat-gap` carries `sessionID`. A subagent's conversation sends its own events with its own `sessionID`. `narrowChatEvent` drops `message.part.delta`. To render text as it is typed, read `message.part.delta` from the raw event and append `properties.delta`. `heartbeatGapEvent(gapMs)` builds the union's synthetic `heartbeat-gap` member from `onGapRehydrate`.

The SDK keeps the connection alive:

- Events arrive in batches every 16 ms. Consecutive `message.part.delta` events for one part field in one batch arrive as one event: `properties.delta` holds the joined text, `id` is the last event's id, and `coalesced` lists the events it replaced.
- After a healthy stream drops, the SDK reconnects after 250 ms. Otherwise it backs off from 1 s to 30 s. Every delay gets up to 50% jitter. It resumes with `Last-Event-ID`.
- The sandbox sends a keepalive every 20 s. After 60 s with no frame, the SDK drops the connection and reconnects.
- `onGapRehydrate(gapMs)` runs after a reconnect when the dropped stream had delivered events, or when the gap was longer than 5 s.
- After 8 consecutive hard failures (a stopped or deleted sandbox), the stream stops for good. Open a new stream after the session runs again.

#### Raw streams and React Native

`openEventStream({ url: runtimeUrl, onEvent })` is the primitive under `stream()`. Two calls for one runtime URL share one connection. Its options (`onParked`, `onConnectionChange`, the timeouts and the failure limit) are in [SDK reference > Event stream](/docs/developers#event-stream).

A host whose `fetch` cannot stream a response body, such as React Native, passes its own transport in `createKortix`. The SDK keeps reconnect, resume, backoff and the keepalive check:

```ts
import { createKortix, type RuntimeEventTransport } from '@kortix/sdk';

const eventStreamTransport: RuntimeEventTransport = async function* ({ url, headers, signal }) {
  // Yield { data?, id?, retry? } for each server-sent message of one connection.
  // Return when the server ends the connection.
  // Throw (with `status` for a non-2xx answer) when it fails.
};

const kortix = createKortix({ backendUrl, getToken, eventStreamTransport });
```

A host without `visibilitychange` and `online` events calls `notifyHostSignal('visible' | 'online' | 'retry')`. The stream that `useSession` holds reconnects on it. A `stream()` handle does not listen to it.

### Get the reply

A turn ends with `session.idle` or `session.error` for your `runtimeSessionId`. Wait for one of them, then read the last message:

```ts
import { classifyTurn, narrowChatEvent } from '@kortix/sdk';

const s = kortix.session(projectId, sessionId);
const { runtimeSessionId } = await s.ensureReady();

let settle!: (error?: unknown) => void;
const turnEnded = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {
  settle = (error) => (error === undefined ? resolve() : reject(error));
});

const stream = await s.stream({
  onEvent: (raw) => {
    const event = narrowChatEvent(raw);
    if (event?.type === 'session.idle' && event.sessionID === runtimeSessionId) settle();
    if (event?.type === 'session.error' && event.sessionID === runtimeSessionId) {
      settle(event.error ?? new Error('The turn failed.'));
    }
  },
});

try {
  await s.send('List the open TODO comments in this repository.');
  await turnEnded;
} finally {
  stream.close();
}

const { messages } = await s.messages({ limit: 1 });
const last = messages.at(-1);
const turn = last ? classifyTurn(last) : null;
const text = (turn?.parts ?? [])
  .flatMap((part) => (part.kind === 'text' && !part.synthetic ? [part.text] : []))
  .join('\n');
```

- `classifyTurn(message)` returns `{ parts, error, isEmpty }`. Each part has a `kind`: `text`, `reasoning`, `tool`, `file` and more. `error` is the turn's normalized error.
- `s.turn()` answers without the stream: `turns` (empty when idle), `last_ended`, `recent_failures` and `can_stop`. Poll it when you cannot hold a stream open.
- An agent reply can hold generative UI blocks (`openui` fences). For plain text, pass the text through `genuiToMarkdown(text)` from `@kortix/sdk/genui`. See [Generative UI](/docs/developers#generative-ui).

Read the conversation:

| Method | Returns | Wakes the sandbox |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `s.messages({ limit?, before?, conversationId? })` | A page of messages, oldest first, as `{ info, parts }` (`kortix.transcript.v1`), and `hasMore`. `conversationId` reads a subagent's conversation. | Yes |
| `s.log({ thread?, before_seq?, limit? })` | One page of the stored session log (`kortix.session/2`): the session record, every thread head, and the newest `limit` messages of one thread (default 40, at most 500). Pass the first message's `seq` as `before_seq` for older ones. `null` while the session has no stored log. | No |
| `s.messageAuthors()` | The member or session that sent each message, and the member who stopped each stopped turn. | No |
| `s.modelUsage()` | The model that answered each turn, and `fallback_from` when a fallback model answered. | No |

Use `s.log()` while the runtime is stopped. It reads the API's database, not the sandbox.

### Answer the agent

The agent can stop and wait for a permission or an answer. Read what it waits on, then answer:

```ts
const { statuses, permissions, questions } = await s.pending();

const permission = permissions[0];
if (permission) await s.answerPermission(permission.id, 'once'); // 'once' | 'always' | 'reject'

const question = questions[0];
if (question) await s.answerQuestion(question.id, [['Yes']]); // one answer list per question
```

- `'always'` grants the permission for the rest of this session.
- `answerQuestion(id, null)` dismisses the question.
- The stream announces both with `permission.asked` and `question.asked`. Their `requestID` is the `id` to answer.

### Gate on runtime capabilities

A runtime does not serve every feature. `s.health()` lists what it serves in `health.capabilities`. Hide a control when its capability is absent:

```ts
import { runtimeSupports } from '@kortix/sdk';

await s.ensureReady();
const { health } = await s.health();
const canCompact = runtimeSupports(health?.capabilities, 'session.compact');
```

| Capability | Feature |
| --- | --- |
| `session.subagents` | Subagent child sessions |
| `session.compact` | Summarize the conversation on demand |
| `session.commands` | Project slash commands |
| `session.steer` | A prompt read by the running turn at its next step |
| `session.log.v2` | The event stream serves the session log as `log.*` frames |
| `session.rewind` | Roll back to a message, and restore it |
| `session.fork` | Fork a session at a message |
| `session.mcp` | MCP servers the runtime connects itself |
| `session.todo` | The runtime's todo list |
| `session.shell` | A shell command run as a turn |
| `session.attach` | Attach the runtime's own terminal client |
| `session.config` | A runtime config document a client reads and patches |

- pi lists the first five rows.
- A route for an unlisted feature answers `501` `feature_not_supported`.
- `runtimeSupports` returns `true` before a health answer arrives, and for a list with no `session.*` entry.

### Control the session

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `s.abort()` | Ends the running turn. The session stays. |
| `s.setModel(model)` / `s.setAgent(agent)` | The model and agent for later `send()` calls on this handle only. |
| `s.changeModel(modelId)` | Saves a new model for the session. See below. |
| `s.compact(model?)` | Summarizes the conversation into a shorter context. Needs `session.compact`. |
| `s.restart()` | Stops and starts the sandbox in place. Clears a failed-start verdict. |
| `s.stop()` | Stops the sandbox. The disk, the branch and the conversation stay. |
| `s.recover()` | Continues a lost session (the provider removed its sandbox) on a new sandbox. The next start boots from the branch and loads the saved conversation. |
| `s.delete()` | Deletes the session and its sandbox. Resolves to `{ ok, restore_until }`. The session is restorable for 30 days. After that, its messages, files and branch are deleted. |
| `s.restore()` | Restores a deleted session before `restore_until`. The next start boots a new sandbox from the branch. |
| `s.rewind(messageId)` / `s.restoreRewind()` | Rolls the conversation back to a user message, and undoes that before the next prompt. Needs `session.rewind`, which pi does not list. |
| `s.configState()` / `s.reloadConfig({ force? })` | Reads whether the session runs the current config of the base branch, and loads it. The reload restarts the runtime. During a turn it returns `409` `SESSION_BUSY`; `force: true` ends the turn. |

```ts
const { applied_live, push_failed } = await s.changeModel('glm-5.3-flash');
```

- `changeModel()` restarts the runtime when the model changes on a running session. A running turn ends.
- `applied_live: true`: the running session took the model now. `false`: it applies at the next start.
- `push_failed: true`: the model is stored, but the running runtime still uses the old one.
- On a `stopped`, `failed` or `completed` session it returns `409` `SESSION_NOT_RUNNING`. Only the session owner or a project manager may call it; anyone else gets `403`.
- The Kortix web and mobile apps open a session on its saved model. A model set at create or with `changeModel()` is the model their next message uses, unless the person picks another model in that session. `sessionModelCandidates()` from `@kortix/sdk` is the same ranking.

A refused `restore()` throws an `ApiError` with `code` `session_not_deleted`, `session_sandbox_removal_pending` (retry in a minute) or `session_purged`. A refused `recover()` throws `session_not_lost`, `session_transcript_missing`, `session_sandbox_removal_pending` or `session_log_restore_off`.

Sharing, public links and reminders are in [SDK reference](/docs/developers#sdk-reference).

### Files and previews

`s.files` reads and writes the live sandbox. Every call readies the session first.

| Method | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `list(dir)`, `read(path)`, `readBlob(path)` | Lists a directory. Reads a file as text or as a `Blob`. |
| `status()` | The git status of changed files. |
| `findFiles(query, { type?, limit? })`, `findText(pattern)` | Finds files by name and by content. |
| `write(path, blob)` | Writes a file. It overwrites the file in place. |
| `upload(file, targetPath?, filename?)` | Writes a new file. It never overwrites: a taken name gets a unique name. |
| `create(path)`, `copy(from, to)`, `remove(path)`, `mkdir(dir)`, `rename(from, to)` | Edits the file tree. |

```ts
await s.files.write('/workspace/NOTES.md', new Blob(['# Notes\n']));
const readme = await s.files.read('/workspace/README.md');
```

Preview URLs need a ready handle:

```ts
await s.ensureReady();
const url = s.previewUrl(3000, '/docs'); // a URL a browser tab opens
const fixed = s.proxyUrl('http://localhost:3000/'); // rewrite a localhost URL the agent printed
const direct = s.sandboxPortUrl(3000); // the authenticated API proxy URL, for a bearer-token client
const ports = await s.previews(); // the ports the runtime exposes
```

See [Computers](/docs/guide#computers) for ports and previews.

### Start sessions for your users

Your server holds one Kortix credential and starts sessions for its own customers. Your customers never sign in to Kortix.

1. Use a personal access key (`kortix_pat_…`, acts as you) or a service account key (`kortix_sa_…`, acts as itself and needs a project role first). See [Security](/docs/guide#security).
2. Create the client with `createScopedKortix` from `@kortix/sdk/server`. It never writes the process-global config, so one request never reads the config of another. See [SDK authentication](/docs/developers#sdk-authentication).

```ts
import { createScopedKortix } from '@kortix/sdk/server';

const kortix = createScopedKortix({
  backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
  getToken: async () => process.env.KORTIX_API_KEY!,
});

const created = await kortix.project(projectId).sessions.create({
  agent_name: 'support',
  initial_prompt: 'Answer ticket T-123.',
  labels: ['support', 'customer:c-42'],
  metadata: { app_customer_id: 'c-42', app_ticket_id: 'T-123' },
  runtime_context: { customer_id: 'c-42', plan_tier: 'pro' },
  connector_bindings: { gmail: { connection_id: supportInboxConnectionId } },
  secrets: ['HELPDESK_API_KEY'],
});
```

- Both credential types give the session the origin `backend`. The request body cannot set the origin. A token from inside a session sandbox is never `backend`.
- On a scoped client, `kortix.runtime()` throws. Reach a session through `kortix.session(projectId, sessionId)`.
- Find a customer's sessions with `listPage({ labels: ['customer:c-42'] })`.

#### Retry a create safely

`sessions.create()` sends no `Idempotency-Key`. To retry an SDK create, generate `session_id` with `generateSessionId()` and send the same id again. A `409` `session_already_exists` on the retry means the first create succeeded.

Over HTTP, send an `Idempotency-Key` header:

```bash
curl -X POST "https://api.kortix.com/v1/projects/<project_id>/sessions" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $KORTIX_API_KEY" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -H "Idempotency-Key: example-app:ticket-T-123:reply" \
  -d '{ "agent_name": "support", "initial_prompt": "Answer ticket T-123.", "secrets": ["HELPDESK_API_KEY"] }'
```

- The API answers `201` with the session. A replay with the same key returns the same session.
- A key is 1 to 255 characters of `A-Z a-z 0-9 . _ : + / = -`.
- A key is unique across all of Kortix. A key that another project or account used returns `409` `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_CONFLICT`. Put your own unique ids in the key.
- A replay while the first create still runs answers `202` with `status` and `session_id`.
- A replay with different `connector_bindings`, `secrets` or `runtime_context` returns `409` `IDEMPOTENCY_BINDING_CONFLICT`, `IDEMPOTENCY_SECRETS_CONFLICT` or `IDEMPOTENCY_CONTEXT_CONFLICT`.
- A key whose session was deleted returns `409` `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_SESSION_DELETED`. Use a new key.

#### Pass context, accounts and secrets

`runtime_context` gives the agent non-secret values, such as your customer id.

- Values are strings (at most 4,096 characters), numbers, booleans or `null`.
- Keys are 1 to 64 characters: a lowercase letter, then lowercase letters, digits, `.`, `_` or `-`. A key with a segment such as `token`, `secret`, `password`, `credential`, `api_key`, `private_key`, `authorization` or `cookie` is rejected.
- At most 64 keys and 16 KiB of JSON.
- The sandbox receives the object as JSON in the environment variable `KORTIX_SESSION_CONTEXT`. You set it at create. A rescope does not change it.

`connector_bindings` pins the connector account of each alias, as `{ alias: { connection_id } }`, at most 64 entries. The bindings are all-or-nothing:

- When you set it, an alias you did not bind gets no account. `inherit_unbound: true` lets unbound aliases use the project default.
- A shared (`project`) account works for both credential types.
- A private (`member`) account works only for its owner, and only in a private session. A new session is private. A service account never uses a private account.
- The credential never enters the sandbox. Kortix resolves it on every connector call. See [Connectors](/docs/guide#connectors).

`secrets` narrows the project secrets the sandbox receives, from the agent's grant down to the identifiers you list. It never widens the grant. `[]` delivers no project secrets. Only a `backend`-origin caller may set it. See [Security](/docs/guide#security).

#### Change the scope of a running session

```ts
const scope = await s.scope();
scope.secrets_allowlist; // null: the agent's grant applies
scope.connector_bindings_configured; // false: the session uses the project defaults

const next = await s.rescope({
  secrets: ['HELPDESK_API_KEY'],
  connector_bindings: { gmail: { connection_id: otherConnectionId } },
});
next.dropped_secrets; // the secrets the change removed
next.retroactive; // false when the change removed a secret
```

The answer is the new `SessionScope`, with `added_secrets`, `dropped_secrets` and `dropped_bindings` for this change.

Each field you send replaces its whole previous value. A field you leave out stays as it is.

| Value | `secrets` | `connector_bindings` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `null` | Use the agent's secret grant. | Remove the override and use the project defaults. |
| `[]` / `{}` | No project secrets. | No connector account at all, project defaults included. |
| A list / a map | Exactly these secrets, inside the agent's grant. | Exactly these accounts. |

- `{}` where you meant `null` blocks every connector.
- A connector change applies on the next connector call.
- A secret change restarts the runtime when the delivered set changes.
- A removed secret stops reaching the sandbox. It cannot leave a model context or a process that already read it. Rotate the secret when that matters.
- A rescope cannot widen past the agent's grant.
- Only the session owner or a project manager may change the scope. Anyone else gets `403` with no code.

#### Read usage per session

Usage reads never wake the sandbox:

```ts
const record = await s.cost(); // model and compute totals, tokens, compute time, ledger entries

const page = await kortix.billing.sessionCosts.list({
  projectId,
  from: '2026-10-01T00:00:00Z',
  to: '2026-11-01T00:00:00Z',
  limit: 25,
});
```

`sessionCosts.list` covers every session of the account in the half-open UTC window `[from, to)`. The default window is the last 30 days. Filter by `accountId`, `projectId` or `ownerId`. See [Billing](/docs/guide#billing).

### Errors and retries

#### Session create errors

`sessions.create()` and `POST /projects/:id/sessions` return these errors. The `code` field of the JSON body holds the code, and the SDK copies it to `ApiError.code`.

| Status | Code | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `400` | `INVALID_SESSION_MODEL` | The model id is malformed, or the account cannot use the model. |
| `400` | `INVALID_SESSION_CONNECTOR_BINDINGS` | The binding map is malformed. |
| `400` | `INVALID_SESSION_RUNTIME_CONTEXT` | The runtime context breaks a key, value, entry or size rule. |
| `400` | `INVALID_SESSION_SECRETS` | The secrets list is malformed or has more than 128 identifiers. |
| `400` | `INVALID_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY` | The `Idempotency-Key` header is longer than 255 characters or has a character outside the allowed set. |
| `400` | `UNKNOWN_SANDBOX_TEMPLATE` | The sandbox slug names no sandbox in the project. |
| `402` | `subscription_required` / `insufficient_credits` | The account cannot start a session. |
| `403` | `origin_override_forbidden` | A caller that is not a backend origin sent a secrets list. |
| `403` | `CONNECTOR_NOT_ASSIGNED` | The agent is not granted a connector you bound. |
| `404` | `CONNECTOR_CONNECTION_NOT_FOUND` | The account does not exist for that connector in this project, or the caller may not use it. |
| `404` | `SECRET_IDENTIFIER_NOT_FOUND` | The secrets list names an unknown project secret. |
| `409` | `CONNECTOR_CONNECTION_INACTIVE` | The account or its connector is inactive, disabled or not connected. |
| `409` | `PERSONAL_CONNECTOR_CONNECTION_REQUIRES_PRIVATE_SESSION` | A private account is bound to a session that is not private. |
| `409` | `SECRET_IDENTIFIER_KEY_COLLISION` | Two listed secrets set the same environment variable. |
| `409` | `session_already_exists` | A session with this `session_id` exists. |
| `409` | `repository_disconnected` | The GitHub App can no longer reach the project's repository. |
| `409` | `IDEMPOTENCY_*_CONFLICT` | Another create holds the key, or the key was used before with a different body. |
| `409` | `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_SESSION_DELETED` | The key belongs to a deleted session. |
| `500` | `SESSION_CREATE_FAILED` | The create failed. The body has `retry: true`. Retry it. |
| `503` | `SESSION_MODEL_RESOLUTION_FAILED` | The server could not resolve the default model. Retry it. |

#### Rescope errors

`s.rescope()` returns these errors:

| Status | Code | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `400` | `INVALID_SESSION_SCOPE` | The body is malformed, or it has neither field. |
| `403` | `NOT_IN_AGENT_GRANT` | A secret is outside the agent's grant. |
| `403` | `NOT_GRANTED_CONNECTOR` | A connector is outside the agent's grant. |
| `403` | `SECRET_IDENTIFIER_NOT_AVAILABLE` | A secret does not exist, or the session may not use it. |
| `403` | `CONNECTOR_CONNECTION_NOT_FOUND` / `CONNECTOR_CONNECTION_INACTIVE` | The account is unknown, unusable or inactive. |
| `409` | `AGENT_GRANT_UNRESOLVED` | The server could not read the agent's grant. Retry it. |
| `409` | `SECRET_IDENTIFIER_KEY_COLLISION` | Two listed secrets set the same environment variable. |
| `409` | `PERSONAL_CONNECTOR_CONNECTION_REQUIRES_PRIVATE_SESSION` | A private account is bound to a session that is not private. |

#### Error classes

Every SDK call rejects with an `Error` subclass. Check `instanceof`, then branch on `status` or `code`:

```ts
import { ApiError, BillingError, formatBillingErrorForUI, isAuthFailure } from '@kortix/sdk';

try {
  await kortix.project(projectId).sessions.create({ agent_name: 'support' });
} catch (err) {
  if (err instanceof BillingError) {
    const ui = formatBillingErrorForUI(err); // { alertTitle, alertSubtitle }
  } else if (isAuthFailure(err)) {
    // 401, 403 or no token: sign in again or fix the key
  } else if (err instanceof ApiError) {
    console.error(err.status, err.code, err.details);
  } else {
    throw err;
  }
}
```

| Class | When it throws | Key fields |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ApiError` | Any failed request: an error status, a network failure, a timeout or an abort. | `status`, `code`, `details` (the parsed body), `detail` (the body's FastAPI-style `detail` field), `response`, `url`, `endpoint`, `timeout` |
| `AuthError` (extends `ApiError`) | `getToken()` returned `null`. No request was sent. | `code` is `NO_SESSION` |
| `BillingError` (extends `ApiError`) | Every `402`. | `code`, for example `insufficient_credits`; `detail.message` |
| `RequestTooLargeError` (extends `ApiError`) | A `431`: the request headers are too large, for example from too many files in one request. | `detail.message`, `detail.suggestion` |
| `SessionNotReadyError` | A preview method ran before `ensureReady()`. | `name` is `SessionNotReadyError` |
| `SessionStartError` | `ensureReady()`, `start()` or `startOrThrow()` got a `4xx` other than `408` or `429`. It does not extend `ApiError`. | `status`, `code`, `terminal`. `isSessionStartError(err)` checks it. |

Helpers that classify an error:

| Helper | True when |
| --- | --- |
| `isAuthFailure(err)` | The error is an `AuthError`, or its `status` is `401` or `403`. |
| `isFeatureDisabledError(err)` | A flag-gated route refused: `403` with `code` `FEATURE_DISABLED_CODE` (`feature_disabled`). `featureDisabledKey(err)` names the flag. |
| `isRepositoryDisconnectedError(err)` | The GitHub App can no longer reach the repository: the `code` is `REPOSITORY_DISCONNECTED_CODE` (`repository_disconnected`). The API sends it with `409`. |
| `isSandboxNotReadyError(err)`, `isRuntimeStartingError(err)` | The sandbox is starting, stopped or parked. Show a waiting state and retry. |
| `formatBillingErrorForUI(err)` | Returns `{ alertTitle, alertSubtitle }` for a `BillingError`, else `null`. |

- `code` is the API's error code, or the status as a string.
- An aborted request has `name: 'AbortError'` and `code: 'ABORTED'`. It is not a failure.
- The default timeout is `30000` ms per request. A request past its timeout has `code: 'TIMEOUT'`.
- A `401` is sent once more after `getToken()` runs again. See [SDK authentication](/docs/developers#sdk-authentication).
- Sign-in calls reject with `HeadlessAuthError`. See [SDK authentication](/docs/developers#sdk-authentication).

The SDK retries some requests before your code sees an error:

| Method | Retried | Never retried |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `GET`, `HEAD` | Up to 2 times, after 250 ms and 500 ms, on `502`, `503`, `504` or a network failure | A `500` or any other status |
| `DELETE` | Up to 2 times on a network failure with no response | Any received status |
| `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH` | Never | — |

A session create is a `POST`. To make it retry-safe, see [Retry a create safely](#retry-a-create-safely).

## Projects from code

A project owns its repository, connectors, secrets, triggers and Apps. Sessions run inside it. This section shows one call for each project-level job. Every method is on the project handle, `kortix.project(projectId)`. Creating the handle sends no request. The full method tables are in [SDK reference > The project handle](/docs/developers#sdk-reference).

Each sample assumes one client, made as in [Create the client](/docs/developers#sdk):

```ts
import { createKortix } from '@kortix/sdk';

const kortix = createKortix({
  backendUrl: 'https://api.kortix.com/v1',
  getToken: async () => process.env.KORTIX_API_KEY!,
});
const p = kortix.project(process.env.KORTIX_PROJECT_ID!);
```

### Provision and update a project

`kortix.projects.provision(input)` creates a project with a Kortix-managed git repository. It seeds the Kortix starter, so a session can start at once. The request timeout is 120,000 ms.

| Field | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| `name` | Required. |
| `idempotency_key` | Send the same key on every attempt at one create. A retry then returns the project of the first attempt. The key is scoped to the account. It identifies the attempt, not the payload: a reused key with a different `name` returns the first project. |
| `account_id` | The account that owns the project. Omit it for the caller's default account. |
| `starter_template` | `general-knowledge-worker` or `minimal`. |
| `source_item_id` | Clone a `registry:project` marketplace item instead of the starter. It wins over `starter_template`. |
| `marketplace_items` | Marketplace item ids to install. |
| `default_branch` | The branch of the new repository. Omit it for the server default. |
| `icon` · `icon_glyph` | An emoji string, or a `ProjectGlyph` object. `icon_glyph` wins when both are set. An invalid value is dropped and never fails the create. |

A `409` with code `provision_in_flight` (`PROVISION_IN_FLIGHT_CODE`) means an earlier call with the same key is still running. Retry with the same key.

```ts
import { ApiError, PROVISION_IN_FLIGHT_CODE } from '@kortix/sdk';

const key = crypto.randomUUID();

async function provision() {
  for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 5; attempt++) {
    try {
      return await kortix.projects.provision({ name: 'Northwind support', idempotency_key: key });
    } catch (error) {
      if (!(error instanceof ApiError) || error.code !== PROVISION_IN_FLIGHT_CODE) throw error;
      await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 2_000));
    }
  }
  throw new Error('provision still in flight after 5 attempts');
}

const project = await provision();
console.log(project.project_id, project.default_branch);
```

Read and change the project through its handle:

| Method | Returns |
| --- | --- |
| `p.get()` | The `KortixProject` row: `project_id`, `account_id`, `name`, `repo_url`, `default_branch`, `manifest_path`, `status` (`active` or `archived`). |
| `p.detail()` | The project with its configuration. |
| `p.update(input)` | The updated row. `input` takes `name`, `default_branch`, `manifest_path`, `icon` and `icon_glyph`. Omit a key to keep its value. `icon: null` removes the icon. |
| `p.archive()` | `{ ok }`. The project moves to `status: 'archived'`. |

```ts
await p.update({ name: 'Northwind support (EU)' });
```

### Call a connector

`p.connector(slug)` returns one connector handle. `run(action, args, options?)` returns the action's output without the provider envelope. A connector call is not idempotent, so `run` never retries.

```ts
import { ConnectorApprovalPendingError, ConnectorCallError } from '@kortix/sdk';

const gmail = p.connector('gmail');

try {
  const output = await gmail.run(
    'send_email',
    { to: 'ops@example.test', subject: 'Weekly report', body: 'Attached.' },
    { approvalContext: 'Send the weekly report to the ops list' },
  );
  console.log(output);
} catch (error) {
  if (error instanceof ConnectorApprovalPendingError) {
    console.log('Waiting for approval:', error.approvalUrl ?? error.executionId);
  } else if (error instanceof ConnectorCallError) {
    console.error(error.status, error.code, error.hint);
    if (error.connectUrl) console.error('Connect an account:', error.connectUrl);
  } else {
    throw error;
  }
}
```

`run` options (`ConnectorCallOptions`):

| Option | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| `account` | A connection label or id from `accounts()`, `me` (the caller's own default private account) or `project` (the default account shared with the project). Omit it for the connector default. A named account is never replaced by another. |
| `approvalContext` | What the call does, in the caller's words. A person sees it when a policy holds the call. The provider never receives it. |
| `timeoutMs` | Client deadline. Default 90,000 ms. After a client timeout, the call can still have run. |
| `signal` | Aborts the request. The call can still have run upstream. |

The other handle methods:

| Method | Returns |
| --- | --- |
| `describe()` | The connector with the input and output schema of every action, or `null` when the caller cannot call it. |
| `describe(action)` | One action with its schemas, or `null`. |
| `accounts()` | The accounts the caller may run the connector as, default first. |
| `paginate(action, args, { next, maxPages? })` | An async iterator over the output of each page. `next(output, args)` returns the next page's args, or `undefined` after the last page. `maxPages` defaults to 100. |
| `call(action, args, options?)` | The raw call result. A `200` and a `202` resolve. |
| `uploadAttachment(content, input)` | Stage a file for a later call of this connector. |

`kortix connectors types --out kortix-connectors.d.ts` generates types for `run`, `call` and `paginate`. Without the file, args are any object and outputs are `unknown`.

#### Connector errors

| Error | When | Fields to read |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ConnectorCallError` | The call did not run, or ran and failed. Extends `ApiError`. | `code`: `upstream_<status>` (for example `upstream_429`), `upstream_error` for a failure inside a `2xx`, or a server reason such as `connector_not_connected`, `account_required`, `credential_not_shared`, `credential_undecryptable`, `policy_block`, `upstream_timeout`, `computer_offline`, `invalid_json`, else `connector_error`. Also `reason`, `hint`, `connectUrl`, `availableAccounts`, `requestedAccount`, `upstreamStatus`, `retryAfterSeconds`, `binding`. |
| `ConnectorApprovalPendingError` | A policy holds the call for a person's decision (HTTP `202`). Nothing ran. | `executionId`, `approvalUrl`, `approvalSummary`, `approvalInstructions`, `retryable`. When `retryable` is true, the identical call runs after approval. |
| `ConnectorPageLimitError` | `paginate` reached `maxPages` and more pages exist. `code` is `max_pages_exceeded`. | `nextArgs`: pass them to `paginate` to resume. |

Retry only on `upstreamStatus` `429` or `503`, after `retryAfterSeconds`. Every other error needs a person: connect an account from `connectUrl`, pick an account from `availableAccounts`, or decide an approval.

#### Read a connector in React

`useConnectorQuery(projectId, slug, action, args, options?)` from `@kortix/sdk/react` reads one action as a cached query. Use it only for an action that reads: each fetch runs the action upstream and writes an audit row.

```tsx
import { useConnectorQuery } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

function Inbox({ projectId }: { projectId: string }) {
  const { data, error, isPending } = useConnectorQuery(projectId, 'gmail', 'fetch_emails', { max_results: 10 });
  if (isPending) return <p>Loading</p>;
  if (error) return <p>{error.message}</p>;
  return <pre>{JSON.stringify(data, null, 2)}</pre>;
}
```

| Option | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| `account` | As in `run`. |
| `enabled` | `false` keeps the query idle. A `null` `projectId` also keeps it idle. |
| `staleTime` | Default 30,000 ms. |

The hook retries only an upstream `429` or `503`, 2 times, after `Retry-After`. A window focus never refetches. Pair a `connector_not_connected` error with `useConnectorSetup` to render a connect button. In a Kortix App, the client reads as the viewer: see [Know the viewer in a Kortix App](/docs/developers#sdk-authentication).

#### Manage connectors and policies

Configuration lives under `p.connectors`:

| Task | Call |
| --- | --- |
| List and inspect | `p.connectors.list()` · `.config(slug)` · `.catalog()` · `.search(query)` |
| Add and remove | `p.connectors.create(input)` · `.remove(slug)` · `.discover.install({ id, way?, name?, slug? })` |
| Set base rules | `p.connectors.policies.set(slug, rules)` |
| Set a policy set's rules | `p.connectors.policies.set(slug, rules, { policySet: name })` |
| Create or list policy sets | `p.connectors.policySets.upsert(slug, name, { description? })` · `.list(slug)` |
| Bind a policy set to an agent | `p.setAgentScope(agentName, { policy_sets: { [slug]: name } })` |

A rule is `{ match, action, conditions? }`. `match` is a glob over tool names. `action` is `always_run`, `require_approval` or `block`. `policies.set` replaces the whole list.

```ts
await p.connectors.policies.set('gmail', [
  { match: 'fetch_*', action: 'always_run' },
  { match: 'send_*', action: 'require_approval' },
  { match: 'delete_*', action: 'block' },
]);
```

How project rules, policy sets and base rules combine is in [Connectors](/docs/guide#connectors).

### Store secrets

`p.secrets.upsert(input)` creates or updates a project secret. The value is write-only: no call returns it. With the `runtime` strategy, a session reads the secret as an environment variable named `name`.

```ts
await p.secrets.upsert({ name: 'ACME_API_KEY', value: process.env.ACME_API_KEY! });

const { items } = await p.secrets.list();
for (const secret of items) {
  console.log(secret.identifier, secret.configured, secret.status ?? 'ok');
}
```

| `upsert` field | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| `name` | Required. The environment variable name. |
| `value` | Omit it to keep the stored value. |
| `identifier` | Unique per project. Defaults to `name`. Set it to store a second secret under the same `name`. |
| `strategy` | `runtime` (environment variable), `egress` (enforced at the network boundary), `broker` (the value stays out of the session) or `denied`. |
| `consumer` · `egress_policy` · `handle_prefix` | Delivery settings for `egress` and `broker`. See [Security](/docs/guide#security). |
| `shared_with` | People, groups and agents that may use the shared value. `[]` means everyone in the project. Omit it to keep the current list. |

`list()` returns `{ items, required, optional }`. `required` and `optional` are the environment keys `kortix.yaml` declares. A secret with `status: 'undecryptable'` must be set again with `upsert`. `mine` describes the caller's own value, which `setPersonal(name, { value })` sets.

`p.secrets.setGitCredential({ token })` stores the token for a bring-your-own git repository. It returns `{ configured, provider, git_connection }`.

```ts
await p.secrets.setGitCredential({ token: process.env.GIT_TOKEN! });
```

### Run triggers

A trigger starts a session, or calls an App, on a schedule, a webhook or an App event. `p.triggers.create(input)` returns the whole trigger list (`{ triggers, errors, triggers_paused }`).

```ts
await p.triggers.create({
  name: 'Daily digest',
  slug: 'daily-digest',
  type: 'cron',
  every: '1d',
  prompt_template: 'Summarize yesterday’s support tickets.',
});

const run = await p.triggers.fire('daily-digest');
console.log(run.status, run.session_id, run.run_id);
```

| `create` field | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| `name` · `type` | Required. `type` is `cron`, `webhook` or `app`. The API refuses `monitor` with `400`: monitor triggers were removed. |
| `slug` | Derived from `name` when omitted. It cannot change after create. |
| `prompt_template` | Required unless `target` calls an App. |
| `cron` · `every` · `run_at` · `timezone` | A schedule for `cron`: a 6-field cron expression, a period such as `5m` or `1h` (at least `1m`), or one ISO-8601 instant. `timezone` defaults to `UTC`. |
| `secret_env` | `webhook`: the project secret that signs deliveries. |
| `app` | Required for `app`: the slug of the App whose events fire the trigger. The App must exist. |
| `filter` | Payload paths mapped to the condition each must meet, for example `{ 'event.name': … }`. A payload that fails the filter starts no session. |
| `target` | `{ app, path?, method?, payload? }`: call an App instead of an agent. |
| `agent` · `model` | The agent (default `default`) and a `provider/model` override. |

Every field, including session reuse and rate caps, is in [Automations](/docs/guide#automations).

| Call | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `p.triggers.update(slug, input)` | Change a trigger. `null` clears a nullable field. |
| `p.triggers.fire(slug, { payload?, dryRun? })` | Run one trigger now, also while triggers are paused. `payload` deep-merges over the manual payload. `dryRun: true` writes nothing and returns `rendered_prompt`, `filter_matches`, `session_key` and `payload`. A real fire returns `status` (`fired`, `queued`, `deduped` or `failed`), `session_id`, `command_id` and `run_id`. |
| `p.triggers.runs(slug, { cursor?, limit? })` | One trigger's runs, newest first: `{ runs, next_cursor }`. `limit` is 1 to 100, default 25. A page can hold fewer than `limit` runs while `next_cursor` is set. Follow `next_cursor` until it is `null`. |
| `p.triggers.replay(slug, runId, { confirmUnverified? })` | Fire again with the run's stored payload and the current trigger. `409 run_in_progress` while the run is queued or running. `409 payload_unverified` for an unverified webhook payload without `confirmUnverified: true`. `422 payload_truncated` for a payload over 64 KiB. |
| `p.triggers.setActivation(paused)` | `true` pauses every trigger of the project. `false` resumes them. A manual `fire` still works. |

```ts
const { runs } = await p.triggers.runs('daily-digest', { limit: 10 });
const failed = runs.find((r) => r.status === 'failed');
if (failed) await p.triggers.replay('daily-digest', failed.run_id);
```

### Resolve approvals

A policy rule with `require_approval` holds a connector call until a person decides. `p.approvals.list()` returns `{ count, approvals }`. Each approval carries `execution_id`, `action`, `session_id`, `requested_at` and `expires_at`.

`p.approvals.resolve(executionId, decision, { note? })` decides one call. `decision` is `'approve'` or `'deny'`. `note` goes to the agent as an instruction. A project manager or the person who started the session may decide. A decision covers that one call only. To run a tool without approval, write an `always_run` rule.

```ts
const { approvals } = await p.approvals.list();
for (const approval of approvals) {
  if (approval.action === 'gmail.send_email') {
    await p.approvals.resolve(approval.execution_id, 'approve');
  }
}

await p.approvals.resolve('<execution_id>', 'deny', { note: 'Send it to the ops list only.' });
```

The `executionId` of a held call from code is `ConnectorApprovalPendingError.executionId`. After approval, repeat the identical `run`. `p.approvals.sessionsNeedingInput()` returns `{ total, sessions }`: the count of held calls per session. The Review inbox (`p.review.*`) is in [SDK reference > The project handle](/docs/developers#sdk-reference).

### Deploy and operate Apps

An App is a service the project hosts. `p.apps` creates it, uploads a build, deploys a release and runs its machines.

<Steps>
<Step title="Create the App">

```ts
const app = await p.apps.create({ slug: 'hello', name: 'Hello App' });
```

`slug` and `name` are required. `cpu`, `memory_gb`, `disk_gb`, `idle_timeout_seconds`, `always_on` and `uses` are optional. `external_url` lists an App that is hosted elsewhere and never deployed.

</Step>
<Step title="Register a build">

```ts
const { artifact } = await p.apps.artifacts.register({
  kind: 'oci_image',
  image: 'docker.io/hashicorp/http-echo:1.0',
});
```

For a `.tar.gz` build, call `artifacts.uploadArchive(bytes)` instead. It registers a `kind: 'archive'` artifact, uploads the bytes, and finalizes the artifact with `sha256` and `size_bytes`. It returns the `AppArtifact`.

</Step>
<Step title="Deploy a release">

```ts
const deployment = await p.apps.deployments.create(app.app_id, {
  artifact_id: artifact.artifact_id,
  source: {
    kind: 'oci_image',
    image: 'docker.io/hashicorp/http-echo:1.0',
    command: ['http-echo', '-listen=:5678', '-text=Hello'],
    port: 5678,
  },
});
```

`source.kind` is `static`, `bundle`, `dockerfile` or `oci_image`. `environment` holds non-secret values. `secrets` maps an environment key to a project secret identifier.

</Step>
<Step title="Wait for the release">

```ts
let status = deployment.status;
while (!['ready', 'failed', 'cancelled'].includes(status)) {
  await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 3_000));
  status = (await p.apps.deployments.get(app.app_id, deployment.deployment_id)).deployment.status;
}
console.log(status, app.url);
```

A release moves through `queued`, `validating`, `building`, `provisioning` and `checking` to `ready`, `failed` or `cancelled`. `deployments.logs(appId, deploymentId, { after?, limit? })` returns `{ entries, next_cursor }`.

</Step>
</Steps>

Operate the App:

| Call | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `p.apps.start(appId)` · `p.apps.stop(appId)` | Start or stop the App. Returns the `App`. |
| `p.apps.deployments.list(appId)` | Every release of the App. |
| `p.apps.rollback(appId, deploymentId)` | Queue a new release from an earlier release's artifact and config. Returns the `App` with that `deployment`. |
| `p.apps.scale(appId, { app: 2 })` | Machines per process group, 0 to 20, applied in the background. A group with a volume runs 1 machine unless `{ newVolumes: true }` is passed as the third argument. |
| `p.apps.exec(appId, { command, machine_id?, timeout_s? })` | Run one command in a machine. Project admins only. `timeout_s` is 1 to 300, default 60. Returns `exit_code`, `stdout`, `stderr` (up to 1 MiB each) and `truncated`. |
| `p.apps.deployments.remove(appId, deploymentId)` | Delete a release that is not live. `409 deployment_live` for the live release, `409 deployment_in_progress` for a running build. |

```ts
const result = await p.apps.exec(app.app_id, { command: ['sh', '-c', 'uptime'] });
console.log(result.exit_code, result.stdout);
```

Machines, volumes, access, App tokens and `authEnv` are in [SDK reference > The project handle](/docs/developers#sdk-reference). App configuration in `kortix.yaml` is in [Apps reference](/docs/developers#apps-reference).

### Files, git and change requests

These calls read and write the project repository. To work with the files of a running session, use `s.files` from [Sessions from code](/docs/developers#sessions-from-code).

| Call | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `p.files.listDirectory({ path?, ref?, cursor?, limit? })` | One folder level: `{ entries, truncated, next_cursor }`. With `cursor` or `limit`, one page holds `limit` entries (default 500, at most 2,000). Without them, one response holds up to 5,000 entries. |
| `p.files.read(path, ref?)` | `{ path, ref, content, revision? }`. A server older than the `revision` field omits it. |
| `p.files.write({ path, ref, content, expected_revision })` | Commit one file. `expected_revision` is the `revision` from `read`. Returns `{ commit_sha }`. |
| `p.files.search(query, { content?, ref?, limit? })` | Search file names, or contents with `content: true`. |
| `p.git.commits()` · `p.git.commitDiff(sha)` · `p.git.branches()` | The log, one commit's diff, the branches. |
| `p.changeRequests.list(status?, { limit?, cursor? })` | One page, newest number first: `{ change_requests, next_cursor }`. `status` is `open`, `merged`, `closed` or `all`. `limit` defaults to 50. Above 500 the server answers `400`. |
| `p.changeRequests.open({ title, head_ref, base_ref?, description? })` | Open a change request from a branch. |
| `p.changeRequests.merge(crId, { message? })` · `.close(crId)` | Merge or close it. |

Edit one file without overwriting a concurrent change:

```ts
const file = await p.files.read('README.md');
await p.files.write({
  path: 'README.md',
  ref: file.ref,
  content: `${file.content}\nOwned by the Acme support team.\n`,
  expected_revision: file.revision ?? '',
});
```

Review open change requests:

```ts
const { change_requests } = await p.changeRequests.list('open', { limit: 20 });
for (const cr of change_requests) console.log(cr.number, cr.title, cr.head_ref);
```

How sessions create branches and change requests is in [Git](/docs/developers#git).

## React

`@kortix/sdk/react` is the React layer of the SDK. One hook, `useSession`, owns one session view: start, stream, transcript, prompts and replies.
