The layer reads the client you create in [SDK > Create the client](/docs/developers#create-the-client) and adds no second client. It runs in React and React Native.

This section covers one session view, from setup to drawing every message yourself. The full hooks catalog for accounts, projects, triggers, Apps and access checks is in [SDK reference](/docs/developers#sdk-reference). The prebuilt components are in [Kortix UI](/docs/developers#kortix-ui).

### Set up

<Steps>
<Step title="Install the peers">
`react` 18 or later and `@tanstack/react-query` `^5.75.2` are optional peers of `@kortix/sdk`. The React layer needs both.

```bash
npm install @kortix/sdk react @tanstack/react-query
```
</Step>
<Step title="Import the client module once">
`createKortix()` writes a process-global config. Every hook reads that config, so no hook takes a client argument. Import the module that creates the client before the first hook renders.

```ts
// lib/kortix.ts
import { createKortix } from '@kortix/sdk';

export const kortix = createKortix({
  backendUrl: 'https://api.example.test/v1',
  getToken: async () => readSessionToken(),
});

declare function readSessionToken(): Promise<string | null>;
```

The options and the "one client per host" rule are in [Create the client](/docs/developers#create-the-client). The sources of `getToken` are in [SDK authentication](/docs/developers#sdk-authentication).
</Step>
<Step title="Mount QueryClientProvider">
`useSession`, the prompt hooks and most data hooks are React Query hooks. They throw without a `QueryClientProvider`. Keep one `QueryClient` per host.

```tsx
'use client';
import '@/lib/kortix';
import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { useState, type ReactNode } from 'react';

export function Providers({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
  const [queryClient] = useState(() => new QueryClient());
  return <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>{children}</QueryClientProvider>;
}
```
</Step>
<Step title="Optional: KortixProjectProvider">
The SDK reads no router. Some hooks need "the project on screen", for example the runtime provider and local-config hooks. `KortixProjectProvider` supplies that id, and `useKortixRouteProjectId()` reads it. Outside the provider the id is `null`. `useSession` does not need it.

```tsx
import { KortixProjectProvider } from '@kortix/sdk/react';
import type { ReactNode } from 'react';

export function ProjectScope({ projectId, children }: { projectId: string | null; children: ReactNode }) {
  return <KortixProjectProvider projectId={projectId}>{children}</KortixProjectProvider>;
}
```
</Step>
</Steps>

### useSession

`useSession(projectId, sessionId, options?)` runs one session view end to end. It long-polls `POST …/start` until the sandbox is ready, opens the event stream, reads saved history, syncs messages and status, and sends prompts. Call it once per session view. The sandbox is ready when `/start` answers `stage: 'ready'`. The hook runs no separate health check.

```tsx
import { useSession } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

export function SessionView({ projectId, sessionId }: { projectId: string; sessionId: string }) {
  const s = useSession(projectId, sessionId);

  if (s.messages.length === 0 && s.phase !== 'ready') {
    if (s.phase === 'error') return <NotRunning stage={s.stage} onRetry={s.retry} />;
    if (s.conversationEmpty) return <Composer disabled onSend={s.send} />;
    return s.savedTranscript === 'none' ? <Booting stage={s.stage} /> : <MessageSkeleton />;
  }

  return (
    <>
      {s.messages.map(({ info, parts }) => (
        <Message key={info.id} info={info} parts={parts} />
      ))}
      {s.sendError && <p role="alert">{s.sendError.message}</p>}
      <Composer
        disabled={s.phase !== 'ready'}
        busy={s.isBusy}
        canStop={s.canStop}
        onSend={s.send}
        onStop={() => void s.cancel()}
      />
    </>
  );
}
```

`NotRunning`, `Composer`, `Booting`, `MessageSkeleton` and `Message` are your components.

#### The render rule

While the session starts, pick the view by this rule:

| State | Render |
| --- | --- |
| `messages` has rows | The transcript. Saved history paints before the sandbox wakes. |
| No rows and `conversationEmpty` is `true` | The composer. The session has no messages and nothing to wait for. |
| No rows and `savedTranscript` is `'loading'` | Placeholder rows. A saved copy is on its way. |
| No rows and `savedTranscript` is `'none'` | A boot screen with `stage`. Nothing can show until the runtime answers. |
| `phase` is `'error'` | `startError` or `failure`, with a Retry button that calls `retry()`. |

Saved history is the copy of the session log that the API stores. A stopped sandbox does not block it. The first read holds the newest 40 messages, and `loadOlder()` pages back. An unknown `savedTranscript` is `'loading'`, never `'none'`. To show a session before `useSession` binds it, for example on a connecting screen, call `readSavedSessionCopy({ projectId, sessionId, runtimeSessionId })` from `@kortix/sdk`. It never throws. Its `empty` field is `true` when the copy proves the conversation empty and no turn ended or is open: show the composer then. The function reads the session log. `useSession` reads that log page again when it binds.

`readSavedSessionCopy` resolves `{ log, empty, envelope }`. `log` is the newest session-log page (`GET …/log`). A new session with no stored log yet answers `404` `session_log_missing`: `log` is `null`, and nothing else is read. `envelope` is deprecated.

A sub-agent's saved history: `useSessionSync(childRuntimeSessionId, { kortixSessionScope: sessionId, savedChild: true })`. It reads the session log, and the thread comes from `useSessionLogThread(parentSession, childRuntimeSessionId)`.

#### Returns

| Field | Type | What it holds |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `phase` | `'starting' \| 'ready' \| 'error'` | The overall state. Enable sends at `ready`. `error` when `stage` is `failed` or `stopped`, or `/start` failed. |
| `stage` | `string \| null` | The raw `/start` stage: `provisioning`, `starting`, `ready`, `stopped` or `failed`. For boot screens. |
| `startError` | error or `null` | The terminal `/start` failure. Render it instead of a spinner. |
| `failure` | object or `null` | The typed terminal provisioning failure from `/start`. |
| `retry` | `() => void` | Polls `/start` again. For a Retry button. |
| `messages` | `{ info, parts }[]` | The transcript. Parts stream in live. |
| `savedTranscript` | `'loading' \| 'shown' \| 'none'` | Whether saved history can show before the sandbox wakes. |
| `conversationEmpty` | `boolean` | The saved copy proves the session has no messages, no turn ended since, and nothing is open or queued. |
| `freshness` | `'idle' \| 'loading' \| 'fresh' \| 'stale' \| 'error'` | The transcript read state. `loading` or `error` with no rows is a wait or a failure, not an empty session. |
| `retryTranscript` | `() => void` | Reads the transcript tail again. |
| `hasOlder`, `isLoadingOlder`, `loadOlder` | `boolean`, `boolean`, `() => Promise<void>` | Paging toward older messages. |
| `status` | `SessionStatus` | The runtime status of the session. |
| `working` | `WorkingProjection` | `{ state: 'idle' \| 'working', pendingDelivery?, source, turnId, since, serverOpenTurnToken }`. The one working answer. |
| `isBusy` | `boolean` | `working.state === 'working'`. |
| `canStop` | `boolean` | `false` only when the server's `/turn` answer says `can_stop: false`. In a shared session only the member who started the turn, the session owner or a project manager may stop it. Show no Stop then. |
| `presenceAwake` | `{ until: Date, capAt: Date } \| null` | With `browserPresence`: `until` is when the sandbox stops after the latest presence renewal, and `capAt` is the latest time presence can keep it awake. `null` while the person is absent, for a person who may not start the session, once the session start reports the sandbox not ready, and from the start of a turn until the next renewal answers. The hook reads it again every 5 minutes while the person is present, when a turn ends, and when the sandbox becomes ready. |
| `isSending` | `boolean` | A `send` request is in flight. |
| `sendError` | `KortixSendError \| null` | The last `send` failure. A new `send` clears it. |
| `questions`, `permissions` | arrays | Open agent questions and tool-approval requests. A permission here is one tool approval, not an access-control permission. |
| `hasPending` | `boolean` | At least one question or permission is open. |
| `diffs`, `todos` | arrays | Live file diffs and the todo list. `todos` stays empty unless the runtime lists `session.todo`. |
| `rewindMessageId`, `rewindPending`, `rewindError` | `string \| null`, `boolean`, `KortixSendError \| null` | The staged rewind, a rewind or restore in flight, and its last failure. |
| `models` | `FlatModel[]` | Selectable models. |
| `agents`, `defaultAgent`, `commands` | arrays, `string \| null`, array | Selectable agents, the default agent and slash commands. Available before the sandbox starts. |
| `agentName` | `string \| null` | The agent the session was created with, from the first `/start` answer. `null` when none was bound. |
| `picks` | `SessionPicks` | This session's chosen `model`, `agent` and `variant`, with `setModel`, `setAgent` and `setVariant`. `send` uses them. |
| `runtimeSessionId` | `string \| null` | The runtime's root session id, or `null` while it resolves. |
| `runtimeUrl` | `string \| null` | The session runtime's proxy URL, or `null` until `stage` is `ready`. For a direct runtime call. |

#### Actions

| Action | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `send(text, override?)` | Puts a prompt in the session's prompt inbox. The message shows at once. `override` is `{ model?, agent?, variant? }` for this message only. Before `phase` is `ready` it does nothing. It never throws: a failure goes to `sendError`. |
| `sendParts(parts, override?)` | Sends text and file parts. `override` also takes `clientMessageId`: send a failed submission again with the same value, and the server delivers it once. Throws a not-ready error before `ready`. |
| `cancel()` | Stops the running turn. It holds the queued prompts first, so a queued prompt does not start the next turn. Resolves `{ status: 'aborted' }`, `{ status: 'timed-out' }`, `{ status: 'failed', error }`, or `{ status: 'skipped' }` before the runtime is bound. It never rejects. |
| `runCommand(command, args, options?)` | Runs a project slash command. `args` is a string. `options` is `{ agent?, model?, variant? }`. Before `ready` it resolves and does nothing. Gated by `session.commands`. |
| `rewind(messageId)` | Rewinds the session to a user message and restores file state. Throws while the session is busy. Gated by `session.rewind`: pi does not serve it. |
| `restoreRewind()` | Restores the removed messages before another prompt commits the rewind. Gated by `session.rewind`. |
| `answerQuestion(id, answers)` | Answers an open question. `answers` is `string[][]`, one array per question. |
| `rejectQuestion(id)` | Rejects an open question. |
| `answerPermission(id, reply, message?)` | Answers a tool approval. `reply` is `'once'`, `'always'` or `'reject'`. |

The three reply actions remove the item only after the server accepts the reply. On failure they throw a `KortixSendError`, and the item stays open. `answerQuestion`, `rejectQuestion` and `answerPermission` are also exported from `@kortix/sdk/react` as plain functions. Hide gated actions with [`useRuntimeSupports`](/docs/developers#gate-controls-by-capability). `removeQuestion` and `removePermission` are deprecated: they never reach the agent, so the run stays blocked.

#### Options

| Option | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `enabled` | `true` | `false` holds the whole hook: no `/start`, no stream. Use it until a precondition passes, for example a billing check. |
| `waitMs` | `15000` | The long-poll budget in ms that the hook sends to `/start`. The server clamps it. |
| `initialRuntimeSessionId` | `null` | A runtime session id your server already holds. The transcript renders from it while `/start` runs. The `/start` answer wins. |
| `replayStartStash` | `true` | Sends the first prompt that a "new session" screen saved, once the session is ready and empty. Set `false` when your host hands off the first message itself. |
| `chatEngine` | `true` | `false` when your app mounts its own chat surface for this session. Then `messages`, `diffs` and `todos` are empty, `questions` and `permissions` stay live, and `replayStartStash` is off. |
| `subscribeMessages` | `true` | `false` re-renders the caller only when the transcript changes shape, not on each streamed delta. Draw the rows in a child with `useSessionMessages`. See [Render messages yourself](/docs/developers#render-messages-yourself). |
| `browserPresence` | `false` | Holds this tab's presence lease while a person uses the page (input in the last 10 minutes). The lease routes notifications and fills `presenceAwake`. The sandbox stops the idle window (15 minutes by default) after its latest turn, with the page open or not. |
| `presenceAlerts` | `false` | `true` tells the server that this tab shows its own notifications, so the server skips phone and Web Push while the tab is in use. Pass `true` only when the tab's browser notifications are on and permitted. Needs `browserPresence`. |
| `presencePageExit` | `false` | Ends the presence lease when the page closes. With `false`, the lease ends at its 90-second expiry. Needs `browserPresence`. |
| `repositoryMode` | none | `'previous'` is accepted for callers built against an older `/start`. The server reads it as telemetry only. It changes nothing. |

### Send and handle errors

#### KortixSendError

`sendError`, `rewindError` and the reply actions use one type, `KortixSendError`: `{ kind, message, cause }` plus one detail field per kind. `message` is ready to display.

| `kind` | Meaning | Detail field |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `billing` | The API refused the turn with `402`. | `billing`: the parsed `BillingError`. |
| `connector` | The session needs a connector that has no usable connection. The API refused the turn before the sandbox saw it. | `connectors`: `{ id, slug, name, authorization_strategy }[]`. `project` means one shared connection fixes it. `user` means the person the session runs as must connect. |
| `runtime-not-ready` | The sandbox is still starting. Try again when `phase` is `ready`. | none |
| `runtime-error` | Any other failure. | `gateway`, when the LLM gateway answered: `{ provider?, code?, suggestion?, upstreamStatus?, requestId? }`. |

`classifySendError(error)` turns any thrown value into a `KortixSendError`. Use it in a `catch` around `sendParts`, `rewind` or a reply action.

```tsx
import { classifySendError, type UseSessionResult } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

async function approve(session: UseSessionResult, permissionId: string, report: (message: string) => void) {
  try {
    await session.answerPermission(permissionId, 'once');
  } catch (error) {
    const failure = classifySendError(error);
    if (failure.kind === 'connector') report(`Connect ${failure.connectors?.map((c) => c.name).join(', ')}`);
    else report(failure.message);
  }
}
```

#### useSessionSend

`useSession().send` covers a composer that sends text. `useSessionSend(runtimeSessionId, options?)` is the lower-level send for a host that builds its own parts, for example after an upload step. It returns `{ send, stop, isSending, isStopping, sendError }`.

| Option | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `workingSessionId` | The Kortix session id. Pass it, because the hook's first argument is the runtime session id. Send and stop receipts are filed under this id. |
| `projectId` | The project id. `stop()` then holds the session's prompt inbox before it aborts. |
| `classify` | Replaces `classifySendError`. |

`send(parts, options?, callOptions?)` resolves `{ ok: true }` or `{ ok: false, error, cause }`, where `error` is a `KortixSendError`. It shows the message at once. On failure it reads the runtime's messages again, and it removes the message when that read fails or returns nothing. `stop()` resolves `{ status: 'skipped' }` while an earlier stop is still in flight.

### Pending prompts and turn outcomes

`send` covers a composer that sends one prompt at a time. The hooks below add a queue, steering of a running turn, and the reason a turn ended. They take the same `projectId` and `sessionId` as `useSession`.

#### useSessionPrompts

`useSessionPrompts(projectId, sessionId, options?)` reads and writes the session's prompt inbox on the server. The server delivers pending prompts in order. A control stream pushes queue changes. A poll runs only while that stream is down. `options` is `{ enabled?, pollMs? }`.

| Member | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `prompts` | The pending rows (`SessionPrompt[]`). Each row has `prompt_id`, `state` (`queued`, `delivering`, `waiting`, `failed`), `delivery`, `placement`, `full_text` and `last_error`. A delivered prompt leaves the list. |
| `enqueue(input)` | Adds one prompt. It resolves when the prompt is stored, not when it is delivered. |
| `edit(promptId, text)` | Replaces a waiting row's text. The row keeps its place. |
| `remove(promptId)` | Removes a waiting row and returns it, so an undo can enqueue it again. |
| `interrupt(promptId)` | Turns a waiting row into an `interrupt`: the running turn ends after its running tool, then this row runs. |
| `retry(promptId)` | Runs this row next. Use it for a `failed` row. |
| `hold(held)` | Holds or releases the whole queue. A new send or `retry` releases it. |

`edit`, `remove` and `interrupt` throw a `409` for a row that is already on its way to the runtime.

`enqueue` takes `delivery` (`steer`, `queue` or `interrupt`), and an optional `placement`. The delivery modes are in [Sessions from code > Send prompts](/docs/developers#send-prompts). `placement` sets only where the pending row shows: `transcript` in the message list, implied by `interrupt`, or `composer` in the list above the input, implied by `steer` and `queue`.

#### useSessionWorking

`useSessionWorking(projectId, sessionId, options?)` returns the same `WorkingProjection` as `useSession().working`, for a component that does not hold the `useSession` result. `options` is `{ enabled?, runtimeSessionId? }`. Pass `runtimeSessionId` so the hook reads that runtime's status events. It stays `working` while a prompt waits in the inbox, and it sets `pendingDelivery: true` while that prompt is still on its way. Show Stop whenever `state` is `'working'`.

#### Why a turn ended

The transcript of an interrupted turn carries only an abort error. The API records the cause that the sandbox named, for example its memory guard. A stop that a person asked for is never a failure.

```tsx
import { turnEndNotice } from '@kortix/sdk';
import { useSessionTurnOutcome } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

function TurnEndLine(props: {
  projectId: string;
  sessionId: string;
  userMessageId: string;
  hasError: boolean;
  isAbort: boolean;
}) {
  const outcome = useSessionTurnOutcome(props.projectId, props.sessionId);
  const notice = turnEndNotice(outcome, props.userMessageId, { hasError: props.hasError, isAbort: props.isAbort });
  if (!notice) return null;
  if (notice.kind === 'sandbox-memory') return <p>The computer ran out of memory ({notice.usedPct ?? '?'}% used).</p>;
  if (notice.kind === 'cause') return <p>{notice.message}</p>;
  return <p>The turn stopped without a recorded cause.</p>;
}
```

- Pass the id of the user message that started the turn.
- `hasError` is `true` when the transcript shows an error on that turn. `isAbort` is `true` when that error is an abort.
- `turnEndNotice` returns `{ kind: 'sandbox-memory', usedPct, detail }`, `{ kind: 'cause', name, message }`, `{ kind: 'unexplained' }` or `null`. `null` means: show what the transcript shows. A non-abort transcript error always returns `null`.
- `useSessionTurnOutcome` makes no request. It reads the `/turn` cache that `useSessionWorking` keeps fresh.
- `turnEndCause(outcome, messageId)` returns the raw recorded cause, or `null`.

#### Turn-end notifications

`useSession` calls the `onNotify` option of `createKortix` when a turn ends: `{ kind: 'task-complete', sessionId, sessionTitle }` or `{ kind: 'session-error', sessionId, errorTitle, sessionTitle }`. Open questions and tool approvals raise `{ kind: 'question', sessionId, questionText, sessionTitle }` and `{ kind: 'permission', sessionId, toolName, sessionTitle }`. Show a notification only when the person is not looking at that session.

While the view is mounted, its event stream raises these events. When the view unmounts with a turn still running, `useSession` hands the turn to a watch:

1. The watch listens for `kortix.control.turn` frames on the session's control stream. A hidden window that throttles its timers still receives the end.
2. While that stream is down, it reads `GET …/turn` every 5 s, and every 15 s after 5 minutes. It stops after 12 hours.
3. When no turn is open, it raises `task-complete`, or `session-error` with the recorded cause. A stop that a person asked for raises nothing.
4. These events also carry `projectId`. `task-complete` also carries `watchedSinceMs`, the watch start in the host's `Date.now()`. A host that relays completions between tabs skips one it already showed after that time.
5. A new view of the session ends the watch, so one turn end is announced once.

A host without `onNotify` gets no watch and no extra `/turn` reads.

### Gate controls by capability

`useRuntimeSupports(capability)` returns whether the open session's runtime serves a capability. It reads the `capabilities` list of the runtime's last `/kortix/health` answer. Hide a control whose capability is absent, instead of letting it fail with `501`.

```tsx
import { useRuntimeSupports, type UseSessionResult } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

function RewindButton({ session, messageId }: { session: UseSessionResult; messageId: string }) {
  const canRewind = useRuntimeSupports('session.rewind');
  if (!canRewind) return null;
  return (
    <button disabled={session.isBusy} onClick={() => void session.rewind(messageId)}>
      Rewind
    </button>
  );
}
```

The hook returns `true` until the runtime first answers, and for a runtime that lists no `session.*` capability. The capability list, what pi serves, and the framework-free `runtimeSupports(caps, name)` are in [Sessions from code > Gate on runtime capabilities](/docs/developers#gate-on-runtime-capabilities).

### Render messages yourself

Start with `SessionThread` from `@kortix/ui/session`. It renders the whole chat from one `useSession` result: the transcript, tool calls, [Generative UI](/docs/developers#generative-ui) blocks, pending prompts, the send error and the composer. Setup, styles, props and tool-row overrides are in [Kortix UI > Add a session](/docs/developers#add-a-session). Use the headless kit below only when you draw every part with your own components.

#### Keep the view still while a turn streams

A streaming turn changes the transcript about every 16 ms. Call `useSession(projectId, sessionId, { subscribeMessages: false })` in the view. Read the rows in the transcript component with `useSessionMessages(session)`, and pass the whole `useSession` result. Only that component then re-renders per delta. `useSessionMessages(session, { throttleMs })` delivers changes at most once per `throttleMs`, and the last change always lands.

#### Classify and render parts

`useChatTurns(messages)` returns one `TurnView` per message: `{ message, parts, error?, isEmpty }`. It is memoized on the `messages` array. `renderParts(parts, renderers)` calls the renderer for each part's `kind`. Its type requires one renderer per kind, so a new kind fails your build, not your UI.

```tsx
import { toolViewModel } from '@kortix/sdk';
import { renderParts, useChatTurns, useSessionMessages, type UseSessionResult } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

export function Transcript({ session }: { session: UseSessionResult }) {
  const messages = useSessionMessages(session, { throttleMs: 50 });
  const turns = useChatTurns(messages);
  return (
    <ol>
      {turns.map((turn) =>
        turn.isEmpty ? null : (
          <li key={turn.message.info.id} data-role={turn.message.info.role}>
            {renderParts(turn.parts, {
              text: (p) => (p.synthetic ? null : <Markdown key={p.id} text={p.text} />),
              reasoning: (p) => <Thinking key={p.id} text={p.text} />,
              tool: (p) => <ToolCard key={p.id} view={toolViewModel(p.tool)} status={p.tool.status} />,
              file: (p) => <Attachment key={p.id} name={p.filename} url={p.url} isImage={p.isImage} />,
              subtask: (p) => <Subtask key={p.id} description={p.description} agent={p.agent} />,
              retry: (p) => <p key={p.id}>Retry {p.attempt}: {p.message}</p>,
              compaction: (p) => <p key={p.id}>Earlier messages were summarized.</p>,
              patch: () => null,
              snapshot: () => null,
              agent: () => null,
              step: () => null,
              unknown: () => null,
            })}
            {turn.error && <p role="alert">{turn.error.message}</p>}
          </li>
        ),
      )}
    </ol>
  );
}
```

`Markdown`, `Thinking`, `ToolCard`, `Attachment` and `Subtask` are your components.

| Part `kind` | Fields |
| --- | --- |
| `text` | `id`, `text`, `synthetic`. A `synthetic` part is internal to the runtime: do not render it as a chat bubble. |
| `reasoning` | `id`, `text` |
| `tool` | `id`, `tool: ToolView` |
| `file` | `id`, `filename?`, `mime`, `url`, `isImage`, `isPdf` |
| `subtask` | `id`, `description`, `agent`, `prompt`, `model?` |
| `patch` | `id`, `hash`, `files`, `fileCount` |
| `snapshot` | `id`, `snapshot` |
| `agent` | `id`, `name` |
| `retry` | `id`, `attempt`, `message`, `createdAt` |
| `compaction` | `id`, `auto`, `overflow`, `tailStartId?` |
| `step` | `id`, `phase: 'start' \| 'finish'`, `snapshot?`, `reason?`, `cost?`, `tokens?` |
| `unknown` | `raw`: a part type this SDK version does not know. Classification never throws. |

`classifyPart(part)` and `classifyTurn(message)` from `@kortix/sdk` are the classifiers behind `useChatTurns`, for code outside React. `classifyTurn` returns `{ parts, error?, isEmpty }`. `isEmpty` is `true` when the message has no error and no visible part.

#### Read a tool call

A `tool` part carries a `ToolView`: `name`, `kind`, `title`, `status` (`pending`, `running`, `done`, `error`), `input`, `output`, `error`, `outputParsed` (the parsed JSON output, for output up to 256 KiB), `outputText`, `files`, `diff`, `diagnostics`, `answers` and `childSessionId`. A tool that reports `success: false` inside a completed state has `status: 'error'`. Read these fields, not the runtime's own tool metadata.

| Function | Returns |
| --- | --- |
| `toolViewModel(view)` | One shape per tool family, keyed by `kind`. See the table below. |
| `toolKind(name)` | One `ToolKind` for any tool name: `read`, `list`, `glob`, `grep`, `write`, `edit`, `apply_patch`, `bash`, `pty`, `web_search`, `webfetch`, `media`, `show`, `task`, `delegate`, `sessions`, `todowrite`, `question`, `memory`, `connectors`, `automations`, `projects`, `skill`, `context`, `retired` or `other`. |
| `toolInfo(name)` | `{ label, category }`. `category` is `shell`, `files`, `search`, `edit`, `web`, `task` or `other`. |
| `getToolInfo(name, input?)` | `{ icon, title, subtitle }` for a tool card. An unknown tool gets its raw name as `title`. |
| `humanizeToolName(name)` | A readable label for a tool name with no known label. `toolInfo` falls back to it. |
| `toToolView(call)` | A `ToolView` from one v1 tool part or one session-log `ToolCallBlock`. |
| `getPermissionForTool(permissions, callId)`, `getQuestionForTool(questions, callId)` | The open tool approval or question that belongs to a tool call. |
| `inputPath(input)` | A file tool's path, read from `filePath`, `file_path` or `path`. |

`toolViewModel` shapes:

| `kind` | Fields |
| --- | --- |
| `web-search` | `query`, `results?`, `answer?`, `error?` |
| `shell` | `command`, `stdout?`, `exitCode?` |
| `file-read` · `file-write` | `path`, `preview?` |
| `file-edit` | `path`, `diff?`: lines of `{ type: 'added' \| 'removed' \| 'unchanged', text }` |
| `search` | `pattern`, `matches?` |
| `task` | `description`, `agent?` |
| `todo` | `items` |
| `question` | `questions`, `answers?` |
| `generic` | `label`, `inputPretty?`, `outputPretty?`. Every other tool. Always safe to render. |

```ts
import { classifyTurn, toolViewModel } from '@kortix/sdk';
import type { MessageWithParts } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

declare const message: MessageWithParts;

for (const part of classifyTurn(message).parts) {
  if (part.kind !== 'tool') continue;
  const view = toolViewModel(part.tool);
  if (view.kind === 'shell') console.log(view.command, view.exitCode);
}
```

#### Render blocks from the session log

The session log (`kortix.session/2`) is the runtime-neutral record of a session: threads, messages by `seq`, and typed blocks. Render from it when you need sub-agent threads, turn grouping or the full tool-call record. `useSession` loads the log. These hooks start no request of their own, except the first page of a sub-agent thread.

| Hook | Returns |
| --- | --- |
| `useSessionLogThread(session, threadId?, { throttleMs? })` | One `SessionLogThread`: the root thread, or the sub-agent thread that `threadId` names. `null` before the first message. |
| `useSessionLogOlder(session, threadId?)` | `{ hasOlder, isLoadingOlder, loadOlder }` for one thread. For the root thread, `useSession().loadOlder` does the same. |
| `useSessionLog(session)` | The whole `SessionLog`. It changes with every message. |
| `useSessionLogRecord(session)` | The session fields without threads, for example `restore_grade`, `grade_counts`, `harness` and `ext`. `null` before the log loads. It does not change on message updates. |

`sessionLogTurns(thread, options?)` from `@kortix/sdk` groups a thread into one `SessionLogTurn` per user message: `id`, `user`, `assistant`, `blocks`, `status`, `error`, `response` (the last text block), `live`, `queued` and `retrying`, among others. Pass `busy` so the running turn stays live.

```tsx
import { sessionLogCallStatus, sessionLogOpenCall, sessionLogTurns, toolCallStatus } from '@kortix/sdk';
import { useSessionLogThread, type UseSessionResult } from '@kortix/sdk/react';

export function BlockTranscript({ session }: { session: UseSessionResult }) {
  const thread = useSessionLogThread(session, undefined, { throttleMs: 50 });
  if (!thread) return null;
  const turns = sessionLogTurns(thread, { busy: session.isBusy });
  return (
    <ol>
      {turns.map((turn) => (
        <li key={turn.id}>
          {turn.blocks.map((block) => {
            if (block.type === 'text') return <p key={block.id}>{block.text}</p>;
            if (block.type !== 'tool_call') return null;
            const call = sessionLogOpenCall(block);
            return (
              <p key={block.id}>
                {call.name}: {sessionLogCallStatus(block, turn.live)} ({toolCallStatus(call)})
              </p>
            );
          })}
        </li>
      ))}
    </ol>
  );
}
```

Read a `ToolCallBlock` with these functions:

| Function | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `toolCallStatus(call)` | `pending`, `running`, `completed` or `error`. |
| `sessionLogCallStatus(call, live)` | The status to render: `pending`, `running`, `complete`, `error` or `interrupted`. An open call on a turn that is not live is `interrupted`. |
| `sessionLogOpenCall(call)` | The call in its open shape when the runtime closed it without a result. Render calls through it, so a live turn draws each call the same way. |
| `toolCallRawOutput(call)` | A completed call's output text. `''` for any other call. |
| `toolCallError(call)` | A failed call's error text. `undefined` for a call that did not fail. |
| `subAgentCalls(thread)` | The tool calls a sub-agent row lists, in order. |
| `toolCallBlock(part)` | The `ToolCallBlock` of a tool part from `useSessionMessages`. |

A pending permission or question matches a call by `call.call_id`: `getPermissionForTool(permissions, call.call_id)`. The segment, burst, cost, restore and full-text helpers over blocks (`sessionLogTurnSegments`, `sessionLogBurstSteps`, `sessionLogThreadMetrics` and others) are in [SDK reference > Session log helpers](/docs/developers#session-log-helpers).

#### Next: rich blocks

An agent reply can hold `openui` blocks: charts, tables, forms and other components inside a text part. `SessionThread` renders them. A custom renderer finds and renders them with `@kortix/sdk/genui/react`, or turns them into plain Markdown with `genuiToMarkdown` from `@kortix/sdk/genui`. See [Generative UI](/docs/developers#generative-ui).

### Fast opens and offline

#### Start the open read early

`prefetchSessionOpen(queryClient, projectId, sessionId)` starts the read that a session view needs before the view mounts. It sends one `GET …/snapshot?transcript=0` request for the session row, the current turn and the prompt queue. `useSession` then uses that request instead of its own.

```tsx
import { prefetchSessionOpen } from '@kortix/sdk/react';
import { useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import type { ReactNode } from 'react';

export function SessionLink(props: { projectId: string; sessionId: string; children: ReactNode }) {
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
  const warm = () => void prefetchSessionOpen(queryClient, props.projectId, props.sessionId);
  return (
    <a href={`/sessions/${props.sessionId}`} onPointerEnter={warm} onFocus={warm} onTouchStart={warm}>
      {props.children}
    </a>
  );
}
```

- Call it on hover, focus or touch of a session link, or when a route names a session.
- It never calls `/start`, so it never wakes a sandbox.
- It never rejects.
- A second call for the same session within 30 seconds sends nothing.

#### Render from the device on a reload

Two device stores keep what a person already saw, so a reload or a cold start paints it in the first frame. The server's answer then refreshes it in place. Both stores are per user and bounded. Both come from `@kortix/sdk`.

| Store | Keeps | Default bounds |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `createPersistedQueryCache({ storage, userId, shouldPersist })` | React Query results that `shouldPersist` accepts. `isPersistableQueryKey` accepts accounts, projects, project detail, session lists and session rows. A restored entry refetches when it is first read. | 1,000,000 characters, 7 days |
| `createSavedCopyStore({ storage, userId })`, registered with `setSavedCopyStore` | The saved session copies the server sent. `useSession` paints a kept copy before the server answers. | 12 sessions, 1,500,000 characters, 300,000 characters per copy, 14 days |

```ts
import type { QueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import {
  browserKeyValueStorage,
  createPersistedQueryCache,
  createSavedCopyStore,
  isPersistableQueryKey,
  setSavedCopyStore,
} from '@kortix/sdk';

export function adoptDeviceCaches(queryClient: QueryClient, userId: string) {
  const storage = browserKeyValueStorage(); // null where localStorage is blocked or absent
  if (!storage) return null;
  const cache = createPersistedQueryCache({ storage, userId, shouldPersist: isPersistableQueryKey });
  cache.restore(queryClient); // synchronous with localStorage
  const stop = cache.persist(queryClient);
  window.addEventListener('pagehide', () => void cache.flush());
  const copies = createSavedCopyStore({ storage, userId });
  setSavedCopyStore(copies);
  return { cache, copies, stop };
}

export type DeviceCaches = ReturnType<typeof adoptDeviceCaches>;
```

- `persist` writes at most once per second. Call `flush()` on `pagehide`, so a reload keeps the last change.
- On React Native, pass AsyncStorage as `storage`. `restore` then returns a promise.
- The saved-copy store keeps only copies the server sent, never the live transcript. A stopped turn cannot come back as a running turn.
- A full, corrupt or missing store never throws. The app then opens from the server.
- `isPersistableQueryKey` never keeps a permission probe (`useCan`).

### Sign-out and identity changes

The SDK keeps per-person session state in memory: transcripts, open questions and tool approvals, turn receipts, open-read prefetches and model picks. Clear it on every identity change: a sign-out, a different person signing in, and a sign-in in another tab that changes the person without a page load.

```ts
import type { QueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { setSavedCopyStore } from '@kortix/sdk';
import { resetIdentityState } from '@kortix/sdk/react';
import type { DeviceCaches } from './device-caches';

export async function onIdentityChange(queryClient: QueryClient, device: DeviceCaches) {
  setSavedCopyStore(null);
  device?.stop();
  queryClient.clear();
  resetIdentityState();
  await device?.cache.clear();
  await device?.copies.clear();
}
```

`resetIdentityState()` never throws. It also removes the model picks from `localStorage`. It does not clear React Query, your own stores, or the token source you passed to `createKortix`. Reset those in the same handler. Neither device store restores data for another person, but a signed-out device must not keep it.

## Generative UI

`SessionThread` renders generative UI blocks with no code, and `UnifiedMarkdown` renders them when you pass `genui`. Both come from [Kortix UI](/docs/developers#kortix-ui). This section is for hosts that render blocks themselves: a custom web renderer, a native app, or a text channel such as a CLI, email or a bot.

### What a block is

The agent answers in markdown. When it needs structure, it adds a fenced block with the language tag `openui`: a ranked recommendation, a comparison, a chart from tool data, places on a map. Short answers, explanations and code stay prose.

````markdown
Two options fit the brief.

```openui
root = Compare([acme, northwind], ["Price", "Seats"], "Acme")
acme = CompareItem("Acme", ["120", "40"])
northwind = CompareItem("Northwind", ["95", "25"])
```
````

The SDK reads a reply in two shapes:

| Type | Shape | Use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `GenuiSegment` | `{ kind: 'markdown', text }` or `{ kind: 'genui', code, version, closed }` | One piece of a reply, in order. `closed: false` marks the last block while it streams. |
| `GenuiNode` | `{ id, type, props, partial }` | One validated component instance. `id` is a stable React key. `type` is a catalog name such as `BarChart`. `partial: true` means the model has not finished the statement. |

A block holds data only. The SDK parses it, validates it against the catalog, and gives you a tree of `GenuiNode`s. You map each `type` to a component.

The fence tags `openui` and `openui-lang` mean schema version 1. The tag `openui-v<N>` names version `N`. Any other tag is an ordinary code block.

### Turn it on

Generative UI is the `genui` project flag. It is on by default. The API setting `GENUI_ENABLED=false` turns it off for every project. The flag decides one thing: whether new sessions of the project list the `kortix-genui` system skill.

- Flag on: a new session lists `kortix-genui` by name and a one-line description. The agent loads the full catalog only when an answer needs a block. A turn without blocks does not load the catalog.
- Flag off: the skill is hidden and the agent writes markdown only. Blocks already in a transcript still render.
- An agent sees the skill only when its `skills` grant in `kortix.yaml` is `all` or names `kortix-genui`. The default grant is `none`. The starter agents grant `all`. See [kortix.yaml reference](/docs/developers#kortixyaml-reference).

Set the flag from code with `updateFeatureFlag`:

```ts
await kortix.project(projectId).updateFeatureFlag('genui', false); // off
await kortix.project(projectId).updateFeatureFlag('genui', null);  // back to the default
```

The caller needs `project.settings.write`. A running session keeps its skill list until a new session starts. The full method is in [SDK reference](/docs/developers#sdk-reference).

The viewer has a separate switch. Pass `enabled={false}` to `GenuiBlock` and the block renders as markdown.

### Install the peers

`@kortix/sdk/genui` and `@kortix/sdk/genui/react` need two optional peer dependencies. Without them, the import fails.

```bash
npm i zod @openuidev/lang-core@0.3.1
```

`@kortix/sdk/genui/react` also needs `react` 18 or later. `@openuidev/lang-core` sends one pseudonymous PostHog event at install time. Set `OPENUI_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1` or `DO_NOT_TRACK=1` to turn it off.

| Entry point | Holds | Loads the peers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `@kortix/sdk/genui/fence` | Fence detection: `splitGenui`, `separateGenuiClosers`, `genuiVersionOf`, `genuiVersionFromClassName` | No |
| `@kortix/sdk/genui` | Parser, text conversion, prompt, `safeUrl`, version constants, and the fence functions | Yes |
| `@kortix/sdk/genui/react` | `GenuiBlock`, `useGenuiParse`, `defaultGenuiComponents` | Yes |

The root `@kortix/sdk` and `@kortix/sdk/react` entries never load the peers.

### Detect blocks without the parser

`@kortix/sdk/genui/fence` never imports `zod` or `@openuidev/*`. Put it in your main bundle. Load the renderer lazily when a reply holds a block.

| Function | Returns |
| --- | --- |
| `splitGenui(text)` | `GenuiSegment[]`: markdown and block segments in order. Only top-level fences count. A fence inside a blockquote or a list item stays markdown. An `openui` tag inside another fence stays markdown. |
| `separateGenuiClosers(text)` | The text with a fence closer that the model glued to the last statement moved onto its own line. Run it before any markdown parser or `splitGenui`. Returns the same string when nothing changes. |
| `genuiVersionOf(tag)` | The schema version for a fence tag, or `null` when the fence is not generative UI. |
| `genuiVersionFromClassName(className)` | The same, for a markdown renderer's `language-…` class name. |

```ts
import { separateGenuiClosers, splitGenui } from '@kortix/sdk/genui/fence';

const hasBlock = splitGenui(separateGenuiClosers(text)).some((segment) => segment.kind === 'genui');
```

### Render blocks in React

`GenuiBlock` renders one block. It never throws and never shows the block source. This sample splits a reply into segments and renders each one. It uses `react-markdown` for prose; any markdown renderer works.

```tsx
import Markdown from 'react-markdown';
import { separateGenuiClosers, splitGenui } from '@kortix/sdk/genui/fence';
import { defaultGenuiComponents, GenuiBlock } from '@kortix/sdk/genui/react';

// A module constant: GenuiBlock needs a stable renderMarkdown.
const renderMarkdown = (markdown: string) => <Markdown>{markdown}</Markdown>;

export function AssistantText({ text, streaming }: { text: string; streaming: boolean }) {
  const segments = splitGenui(separateGenuiClosers(text));
  return (
    <>
      {segments.map((segment, index) =>
        segment.kind === 'markdown' ? (
          <Markdown key={index}>{segment.text}</Markdown>
        ) : (
          <GenuiBlock
            key={index}
            code={segment.code}
            version={segment.version}
            streaming={streaming && !segment.closed}
            components={defaultGenuiComponents}
            renderMarkdown={renderMarkdown}
          />
        ),
      )}
    </>
  );
}
```

To render blocks from a markdown renderer's `code` override instead, read the version with `genuiVersionFromClassName(className)`. A `null` version means an ordinary code block. Pass `streaming: true` only to the block whose fence is still open.

#### `GenuiBlock` props

| Prop | Required | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `code` | Yes | The fence body so far. |
| `streaming` | Yes | `true` while this block's fence is open. Pass `false` when the fence closes, so the block settles while the reply goes on. |
| `components` | Yes | A `GenuiComponentMap`: block name to your component. A missing name renders that node as markdown. |
| `renderMarkdown` | Yes | `(markdown: string) => ReactNode`, for fallbacks. Pass a stable function (a module constant or `useCallback`). A new function re-renders the block's markdown parts and node views. |
| `version` | No | The schema version from the fence tag. Default: `GENUI_SCHEMA_VERSION`. A version this build does not render shows `GENUI_UNSUPPORTED_NOTE`. |
| `enabled` | No | Default `true`. `false` renders the block as markdown (a viewer setting). |
| `renderPending` | No | `(node: GenuiNode) => ReactNode` for a node the model has not finished. Default: nothing. Return a fixed-height box for a chart or a table, so the layout does not jump. |
| `onSettled` | No | Fires once per block, when it stops streaming, with a `GenuiBlockEvent`: `outcome` (`rendered`, `fallback`, `parse_error`, `render_error`, `unsupported`), `components` (sorted names), `msToFirstPaint`, `issueCount`. It never holds content. |

A settled block with no valid root renders a one-line note: `GENUI_CUT_OFF_NOTE` when the stream ended inside a statement, else `This content could not be displayed.` A component that throws renders the block's markdown. A throw while the block streams gets one more try when the block settles.

#### Components

`defaultGenuiComponents` holds unbranded components for all 19 blocks. They render semantic HTML themed by CSS variables: `--genui-fg`, `--genui-muted`, `--genui-border`, `--genui-surface`, `--genui-accent`, `--genui-radius`. Charts and maps render as data tables and place lists. A host that wants drawn charts passes its own components for those names.

Each component gets `GenuiComponentProps`:

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `node` | The `GenuiNode`. |
| `props` | The validated props. Child slots hold `GenuiNode[]`. |
| `renderChild` | `(node: GenuiNode) => ReactNode`. Renders a child node through the same map. |
| `streaming` | The block is still streaming. |

Replace one name and keep the defaults for the others:

```tsx
import type { GenuiNode } from '@kortix/sdk/genui';
import { defaultGenuiComponents, type GenuiComponentMap, type GenuiComponentProps } from '@kortix/sdk/genui/react';

function Callout({ props }: GenuiComponentProps) {
  return (
    <aside data-tone={props.tone}>
      {props.title ? <strong>{props.title}</strong> : null}
      <p>{props.body}</p>
    </aside>
  );
}

function Stack({ props, renderChild }: GenuiComponentProps) {
  const children = props.children as GenuiNode[];
  return <div style={{ display: 'flex', flexDirection: props.direction === 'row' ? 'row' : 'column', gap: 12 }}>{children.map(renderChild)}</div>;
}

export const components: GenuiComponentMap = { ...defaultGenuiComponents, Callout, Stack };
```

Define the map once at module level. A React Native host passes a map of native components and a `renderMarkdown` that returns native nodes. `GenuiBlock` uses no DOM API.

`useGenuiParse(code, version, streaming)` returns the `GenuiParseResult` that `GenuiBlock` renders. Use it to build your own block shell. It keeps one parser per block.

### Parse and stream

`@kortix/sdk/genui` parses without React.

| Function | Returns |
| --- | --- |
| `parseGenui(code, version?)` | A `GenuiParseResult` for a finished block. |
| `createGenuiParser(version?)` | A `GenuiParser`. `update(code, streaming)` takes the full block text so far and parses only the new text. The same input returns the same result object. Unchanged nodes keep their identity across calls. |

`GenuiParseResult` holds `root` (the root `Stack`, or `null`), `pending` (statement names referenced but not written yet), `issues` and `streaming`.

`issues` lists everything the parser dropped or repaired. Use it for logs and debugging, never for the user. A result holds at most 50 issues, plus a `cut-off` issue when the cap drops it. Each `GenuiIssue` has a `code`, a `message` and, where known, `component` and `statementId`.

| `GenuiIssueCode` | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `schema` | A node broke its field limits. It is dropped. Its valid children move to the parent slot. |
| `unknown-component` | The name is not in this build's catalog. |
| `wrong-child` | The slot does not accept this component. |
| `url` | An unsafe link or image URL. |
| `depth` | Container nesting deeper than 4. |
| `too-many-nodes` | The block expands to more than 500 nodes. |
| `too-large` | The block source is longer than 65,536 characters. It is not parsed. |
| `version` | The fence names a schema version this build does not render. |
| `no-root` | The settled block has no valid root. |
| `cut-off` | The stream ended inside a statement. |
| `unsupported-statement` | A `Query`, `Mutation` or `$state` statement. It is ignored and never runs. |
| `parse-failed` | The parser failed on this input. Nothing renders. |

A node that fails is dropped and its siblings still render.

### Render in text hosts

A host that cannot render UI converts blocks to markdown. The output is deterministic and never holds block source.

| Function | Converts |
| --- | --- |
| `genuiToMarkdown(text, { streaming? })` | A whole reply. Blocks inside blockquotes and list items convert too. Text without a block comes back unchanged. |
| `genuiBlockToMarkdown(code, version?, { streaming? })` | One block body. |
| `genuiNodeToMarkdown(node)` | One `GenuiNode` and its children. An unknown or partial node gives `''`. |
| `genuiA11yText(node)` | Screen-reader text for a chart or map node, else `null`. |

Charts become tables with a `Source:` line. Maps become place lists. Tabs and accordions expand. Steps become a numbered list. A Checklist becomes a task list (`- [ ]`). For a reply that still streams, pass `{ streaming: true }`: the unfinished last block gets no cut-off note.

```ts
import { genuiToMarkdown } from '@kortix/sdk/genui';

const markdown = genuiToMarkdown(replyText);
```

The Kortix CLI prints session replies this way. Treat the output as untrusted markdown and render it through the same sanitizer as prose. To get the reply text of a session, see [Sessions from code](/docs/developers#sessions-from-code).

### Teach another model the format

| Name | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `buildGenuiPrompt()` | The `kortix-genui` skill body as a string: syntax, component signatures, rules and examples. Give it to a model outside Kortix as a system prompt. |
| `GENUI_PROMPT_VERSION` | An 8-character hex hash of that prompt. Store it with evaluation results, so a prompt change is visible. |
| `GENUI_SCHEMA_VERSION` | `1`. The schema version this build renders. |

### Blocks

The catalog has 19 top-level blocks. Child nodes appear only inside their parent.

| Block | Holds | Limits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Stack` | Layout of blocks; `direction` `col` or `row`; `layout` `list` or `grid`. The parser wraps every root in a `Stack`. | 1–12 children |
| `Card` | One option with an image, text, badges and a rating | up to 4 `Badge`s; rating 0–5 |
| `Stat` | One key number with a trend | |
| `StatRow` | Key numbers in a row | 2–4 `Stat`s |
| `Table` | Rows to scan | 1–8 columns, up to 50 rows, cells up to 200 characters |
| `Compare` | Side-by-side options; `winner` names the pick | 2–4 `CompareItem`s, up to 12 specs |
| `RankedList` | A ranked recommendation, best first | 1–10 `RankedItem`s |
| `BarChart` | Amounts across categories | 1–24 categories, 1–4 `Series` |
| `LineChart` | A trend over time | 2–365 x labels, 1–4 `Series` |
| `PieChart` | Shares of a whole | 2–6 `Slice`s |
| `Map` | Places and an optional route | 1–25 `Marker`s, zoom 1–18, route up to 500 points |
| `Steps` | A process or journey in order | 1–12 `Step`s |
| `Checklist` | Items the reader ticks off | 1–20 items, up to 120 characters each |
| `Tabs` | Views the user switches between | 2–5 `Tab`s, each with 1–8 blocks |
| `Accordion` | Sections a reader may skip | 1–10 `AccordionItem`s, each with 1–8 blocks |
| `Badge` | A status tag; tone `neutral`, `good`, `warn`, `bad` | label up to 24 characters |
| `Callout` | A takeaway or warning; tone `info`, `warn`, `success` | body up to 400 characters |
| `Image` | A picture with optional caption and text | |
| `Link` | A link to a source or document | label up to 80 characters |

Child nodes: `Tab`, `AccordionItem`, `CompareItem`, `RankedItem`, `Series` (up to 365 values), `Slice`, `Marker`, `Step`.

Limits for every block:

- Container nesting: 4 levels.
- Nodes per block after references expand: 500.
- Block source: 65,536 characters.
- URL length: 2,048 characters.

An older SDK does not know a block added later. It drops that node with `unknown-component`. Update `@kortix/sdk` to render new blocks.

### Safety

- Blocks hold data, never code. `Query`, `Mutation` and `$state` statements never run.
- A block cannot call tools or change state. A Checklist keeps its ticks in the component only. Nothing is stored or sent.
- Every link and image URL passes through `safeUrl(raw)`. It returns a normalized absolute `http` or `https` URL, or `null`. It rejects URLs with credentials and URLs longer than 2,048 characters. Use it in your own components for any URL you render.
- `GENUI_CUT_OFF_NOTE` is `'Response was cut off.'`. It shows under a settled block whose stream ended inside a statement.
- `GENUI_UNSUPPORTED_NOTE` is `'This content needs a newer version of Kortix.'`. It shows in place of a block with a schema version this build does not render.

## Kortix UI

`@kortix/ui` is the React component library of the Kortix web app. It builds on [`@kortix/sdk/react`](/docs/developers#react) and `@kortix/sdk/genui/react`. It ships the primitives (buttons, forms, overlays) and the whole session experience as modules: the session workspace, the thread, the composer, files, the terminal, the project home and the session list. Kortix Apps, white-label hosts and your own app render the same components.

The components read the one client you created in [Create the client](/docs/developers#create-the-client). `@kortix/ui` adds no second client and no second setup. Components are Tailwind CSS v4 class strings that read CSS variables. You re-theme them with tokens, never by copying a component.

### Install and peers

```bash
npm i @kortix/ui @kortix/sdk @tanstack/react-query @openuidev/lang-core@0.3.1 zod @phosphor-icons/react@2.1.10 react@19 react-dom@19
```

Install the same release version of `@kortix/ui` and `@kortix/sdk`. Version rules are in [Versions and stability](/docs/developers#versions-and-stability). The module entries (`@kortix/ui/session`, `/workspace`, `/theme` and the others in [Modules](/docs/developers#modules)) ship from 0.14.4; 0.14.3 has the root, the component subpaths and the stylesheets. Inside the [kortix-ai/suna](https://github.com/kortix-ai/suna) repository, add `"@kortix/ui": "workspace:*"` to your package's dependencies instead.

| Peer | Required for |
| --- | --- |
| `react` >= 19, `react-dom` >= 19 | Every entry. |
| `@kortix/sdk`, `@tanstack/react-query` 5 | `@kortix/ui/session`, `@kortix/ui/workspace` and every other entry that renders project or session data. Mount one `QueryClientProvider` ([Set up](/docs/developers#set-up)). |
| `@openuidev/lang-core@0.3.1`, `zod` | The session thread, because it renders [Generative UI](/docs/developers#generative-ui) blocks. Both are optional peers of `@kortix/sdk`. Without `@openuidev/lang-core`, `vite build` fails with `Missing export` in `@kortix/sdk/dist/genui`. |
| `tw-animate-css`, `tailwind-scrollbar`, `tailwind-scrollbar-hide` | `@kortix/ui/tailwind.css` in a Tailwind v4 host. A host without Tailwind does not install them. |
| `@phosphor-icons/react@2.1.10` | Your own icons. Pin the version `@kortix/ui` uses, so `IconProvider` reaches your icons too. |

<Callout type="info">
Name `react@19 react-dom@19` in the install. An unversioned `react` lets npm pick React 18 to satisfy the React 18 peer of `cmdk@0.2.1`, and the install does not finish. `Command` works on React 19. A host with `strict-peer-dependencies` must allow the React peer of `cmdk`.
</Callout>

#### Import map

| Import | Holds |
| --- | --- |
| `@kortix/ui` | The core: `UiProvider`, `useUiStrings`, `useUiLink`, `useUiBrand`, `defaultUiStrings`, `SessionIdsProvider`, `setUiRoutes`, `setUiTelemetry`, `setUiHostEnv`, `lazyClient`, `cn`, `useIsDark`, `useIsMobile`, `useDebounce`, and the primitives listed in [Components and modules](/docs/developers#components-and-modules). |
| `@kortix/ui/<component>` | One component file, for example `@kortix/ui/modal` or `@kortix/ui/toast`. Heavy components (charts, maps, data grids, the emoji picker) load only from their own subpath. |
| `@kortix/ui/<module>` | One curated entry per module, for example `@kortix/ui/workspace` or `@kortix/ui/session`. See [Modules](/docs/developers#modules). |
| `@kortix/ui/theme` | `KortixTheme`, `themeToCss`, `themeToCssVars`, `ThemeScope`. See [Theming](/docs/developers#theming). |
| `@kortix/ui/tailwind.css`, `/tokens.css`, `/theme.css`, `/styles.css`, `/fonts.css` | The stylesheets. See [Add the styles](/docs/developers#add-the-styles). |

#### Server rendering

These entries import CSS (KaTeX and others) from JavaScript: `session`, `composer`, `session-panel`, `workspace`, `files`, `file-viewer`, `markdown`, `genui`, `customize`. Plain Node cannot evaluate them and fails with `ERR_UNKNOWN_FILE_EXTENSION ".css"`. Use one of these fixes:

- A Vite SSR host adds `ssr: { noExternal: ['@kortix/ui'] }`.
- A Next.js host lists `@kortix/ui` in `transpilePackages`.
- Any host loads the block on the client only with `lazyClient`.

The root and the `theme`, `i18n`, `icons`, `diff`, `changes`, `terminal`, `browser`, `apps` and `access` entries evaluate in Node.

### Add the styles

Load the styles once, in your main stylesheet or entry file. Pick the tab for your host.

<Tabs>
<Tab title="Tailwind CSS v4 host">
Import `@kortix/ui/tailwind.css` after Tailwind. It brings the tokens (`tokens.css`), the component layer (`theme.css`), the animation utilities and the scrollbar plugins. `theme.css` adds an `@source` rule, so Tailwind scans the package for class names.

```css title="app.css"
@import 'tailwindcss';
@import '@kortix/ui/tailwind.css';
```

Your own classes resolve against the same tokens: `bg-background`, `text-muted-foreground`, `border-border`.

To bring your own token values, import the parts one by one. Every line except `tokens.css` is required. Without `tw-animate-css` and the two plugins, overlays lose their enter and exit animations and `scrollbar-hide` does nothing. The build reports no error.

```css title="app.css"
@import 'tailwindcss';
@import 'tw-animate-css';
@import '@kortix/ui/tokens.css'; /* optional: or set every variable yourself */
@import '@kortix/ui/theme.css';
@plugin 'tailwind-scrollbar';
@plugin 'tailwind-scrollbar-hide';
```

<Callout type="warning">
`tokens.css` sets `--spacing: 0.23rem`, 8% tighter than the Tailwind default of `0.25rem`. Your own spacing utilities follow it: `p-4` is 14.72px, not 16px. To keep the Tailwind default for your classes, set `--spacing: 0.25rem` in `:root` after the import. The components then render 8% larger.
</Callout>
</Tab>
<Tab title="Host without Tailwind">
Import one prebuilt file. It holds the reset, the tokens and every class the components use.

```ts title="main.tsx"
import '@kortix/ui/styles.css';
```

It holds no class that only your code uses. Style your own markup with plain CSS that reads the variables, for example `var(--border)`.
</Tab>
</Tabs>

- Dark mode is the `dark` class on `<html>`. `useIsDark()` returns the current state.
- Both paths style the page: `body` paints `--background` and `--foreground`, borders default to `--border`, native controls follow `color-scheme`, text selection reads `--selection`, and interactive elements show the pointer cursor. You add no base rules.
- `@kortix/ui/fonts.css` loads the Roobert font. It is for Kortix-owned surfaces only. Every other host uses the system font stack (`--font-sans-system`) or sets `fonts.sans` in its [theme](/docs/developers#theming).

### Mount the providers

Mount the providers once, at the root of your app, inside your router and inside one `QueryClientProvider`.

```tsx title="providers.tsx"
import { UiProvider, type UiLinkComponent } from '@kortix/ui';
import { IconProvider } from '@kortix/ui/icon-provider';
import { Toaster } from '@kortix/ui/toast';
import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { useState, type ReactNode } from 'react';
import { Link } from 'react-router-dom';

import './kortix'; // creates the one client: see SDK > Create the client

// UiProvider calls `link` with anchor props, `href` and a Next.js-style `prefetch` hint.
const RouterLink: UiLinkComponent = ({ href, prefetch: _prefetch, ...props }) => (
  <Link to={href} {...props} />
);

export function Providers({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
  const [queryClient] = useState(() => new QueryClient());
  return (
    <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
      <UiProvider link={RouterLink}>
        <IconProvider>
          {children}
          <Toaster />
        </IconProvider>
      </UiProvider>
    </QueryClientProvider>
  );
}
```

| Provider | What it does | Without it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `UiProvider` (`@kortix/ui`) | Connects the components to your router, copy, brand and host features. Options are in the next table. It also loads the animation features. | English copy, plain `<a>` and `<img>`, full page loads, the Kortix logo, no viewer, no slots. Expanded rows stay closed. |
| `IconProvider` (`@kortix/ui/icon-provider`) | Gives every Phosphor icon the Kortix weight and size. | Phosphor defaults. |
| `<Toaster />` (`@kortix/ui/toast`) | Renders the toasts that `successToast`, `errorToast` and the other helpers in `@kortix/ui/toast` raise. | No toast appears. |
| `TooltipProvider` (`@kortix/ui/tooltip`) | Optional. Sets one hover delay for the tooltips below it, for example `delayDuration={200}`. `Hint` mounts its own provider with a 300 ms delay. | Each tooltip uses its own delay. |

`setUiTelemetry` (`@kortix/ui`) is not a provider. Call it once at module load with `track`, `captureException`, `captureMessage` and `addBreadcrumb`. Declare `UiTelemetryRegister { event: YourEvents }` so `tsc` checks event names. Without it, `track` does nothing and `captureException` logs to the console.

#### `UiProvider` options

Every option is optional. Pass objects with a stable identity.

| Option | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `link` | Your router's link component, called with `href`. |
| `navigation` | Your router as four hooks: `useRouter` (`push`, `replace`, `prefetch`, `back`), `usePathname`, `useSearchParams`, `useParams`. Add `observesHistory: true` when your router observes `history.pushState` (Next.js does, React Router does not). A Next.js host passes the `next/navigation` hooks. |
| `strings` | The fallback copy of the primitives: `confirm`, `cancel`, `close`, `commandPaletteTitle`, `commandPaletteDescription`. Pass only the keys you translate. |
| `i18n` | The session UI's translations: `useTranslations` and `useLocale` (next-intl signatures), or `locale` and `messages`. Default: the bundled English catalog of `@kortix/ui/i18n`. |
| `image` | Your image component. Default: a plain `<img>`. |
| `useBrand` | A hook that returns your logo URLs. `KortixLogo` then renders your marks. |
| `useViewerId` | A hook that returns the signed-in user's id. Without a viewer, a session several people write in names no sender. |
| `useViewer` | A hook that returns the signed-in user as `{ id, email?, metadata? }`. The UI reads `name`, `full_name` and `avatar_url` from `metadata`. |
| `updateViewerMetadata` | Saves UI preferences on the signed-in user, so they follow the user across devices. Without it, they stay in the browser. |
| `components` | Host features the session UI opens but does not own: `ShareSessionModal` (exported by `@kortix/ui/workspace`), `ProjectProviderModal`, `ConnectorConnectModal`, `HomeAnnouncement`, `Wallpaper`. A control whose slot is missing is hidden. |
| `resolvePreviewUrl` | `(request) => Promise<string \| null>`. Returns the URL the session browser frames, with your host's own credentials. `null` keeps the SDK preview URL. Use it when the browser must not hold a Kortix token. |
| `config` | Host feature switches. See the next table. |

| `config` key | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `connectorsEnabled` | `true` | `false` hides the hosted app-connector catalog. Custom connectors stay. |
| `managedModelsEnabled` | `false` | `true` when the deployment serves its managed model lineup. The model picker lists it. |
| `terminalEnabled` | `true` | `false` hides the Terminal, for a host whose proxy cannot upgrade a WebSocket. |
| `cliConnectEnabled` | `true` | `false` hides the Terminal's "Connect from your machine" bar, for a host that serves no `<origin>/install` and ships no Kortix CLI. |
| `publicSharesEnabled` | `true` | `false` hides every public-link control, for a host that serves no page at `/share/session/<token>`. |
| `accountApiEnabled` | `true` | `false` for a host that fronts one operator key and blocks the account routes. No IAM, billing or session-oversight request runs. Every permission gate shows its control, and the server enforces each route. |
| `modelPickerEnabled` | `true` | `false` hides the model and reasoning-effort pickers. The session runs its default model. |
| `contextMeterEnabled` | `true` | `false` hides the context-usage ring. |
| `sessionOverridesEnabled` | `true` | `false` hides the session-overrides gear (sandbox, secrets, connectors, folders). |
| `runtimeSlashEnabled` | `true` | `false` removes the runtime's skills, commands and Compact session from the `/` menu. |
| `useSlashActions` | none | A hook that returns your own `/` menu rows: `{ id, label, description, group, onSelect }`. |
| `offeredAgents`, `agentLabels` | every agent, title-cased names | The agents the agent picker offers, and their display names. |
| `takeFiles`, `attachAccept` | upload as prompt attachments | `takeFiles(files)` takes attached, dropped and pasted files into your own store. `attachAccept` is the picker's `accept` list. |
| `commandPaletteShortcut`, `settingsShortcut` | `false` | `true` when your host opens a command palette on ⌘K or settings on ⌘,. The composer then hints at them. |
| `providerIconBaseUrl` | models.dev | Where your host serves provider logos, as `<base>/<icon>.svg`. The Kortix provider draws the `useBrand` icon. |
| `deploymentVersion` | latest | The release version for version-pinned CLI install commands. |

Billing gates are not a `UiProvider` option. They read `billingEnabled` from [`createKortix`](/docs/developers#create-the-client). A custom host leaves it off, and no upgrade control renders.

### Add a session

A session needs one [`useSession`](/docs/developers#usesession) result. Pass it to a component; there is no second chat hook and no transport to write.

`SessionWorkspace` (`@kortix/ui/workspace`) renders the full session screen of the Kortix web app: the thread and the resizable side panel its actions open (files, the file viewer, the browser, the terminal, changes). On a phone the panel is a sheet. The parent sets the size.

```tsx title="session.tsx"
import { useSession } from '@kortix/sdk/react';
import { SessionWorkspace } from '@kortix/ui/workspace';

export function Session({ projectId, sessionId }: { projectId: string; sessionId: string }) {
  const session = useSession(projectId, sessionId);
  return <SessionWorkspace session={session} />;
}
```

`SessionWorkspace` takes `session`, `children` (your own column in place of the thread), `hideHeader`, `readOnly` and `className`. `hideHeader` and `readOnly` apply to the default thread only.

#### The thread alone

`SessionThread` (`@kortix/ui/session`) renders the chat of one session: the transcript, the streaming turn, tool rows, permission and question prompts, the send error, and the composer with attachments, slash commands, the model and agent pickers and Stop.

```tsx title="chat.tsx"
import { useSession } from '@kortix/sdk/react';
import { SessionThread } from '@kortix/ui/session';

export function Chat({ projectId, sessionId }: { projectId: string; sessionId: string }) {
  const session = useSession(projectId, sessionId);
  return (
    <div className="flex h-dvh flex-col">
      <SessionThread session={session} hideHeader />
    </div>
  );
}
```

| Prop | Type | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `session` | `UseSessionResult`, required | The `useSession(projectId, sessionId)` result. |
| `hideHeader` | `boolean` | Hides the title row (title, share, menu). Use it when your page has its own header. |
| `readOnly` | `boolean` | Hides the composer. The thread is a transcript to read. |
| `composerPlaceholder` | `string` | The composer's placeholder. Default: the composer's own. |
| `quote` | `{ id: number; text: string } \| null` | Adds `text` to the composer's reply quotes, as a transcript selection's Reply does. Each new `id` adds its text once. |
| `className` | `string` | Classes on the thread's root. |

- The parent sets the height. The thread fills it.
- `SessionThread`, `SessionWorkspace` and `ProjectHome` mount the project scope and `SessionIdsProvider` themselves.
- With no side panel on the page, a file or preview the thread opens shows in a dialog.
- `SessionThread` renders [Generative UI](/docs/developers#generative-ui) blocks by default. You write no code for them.
- `ModelSelector` and `AgentSelector` (`@kortix/ui/session`) are the composer's pickers. They are controlled, so a form outside a session uses them too.

#### Replace one tool row

`@kortix/ui/session` registers every built-in tool row when it loads. `ToolRegistry.register(name, Component)` replaces the row of one tool name. A later registration for the same name wins. The row gets `ToolProps`: `call` is the session-log tool call with `name`, `input`, `status` (`pending`, `running`, `complete`, `error`) and `result.content` (text and attachment parts). `BasicTool` draws the standard row. `GenericTool` is the default row.

```tsx title="src/shell-row.tsx"
import { BasicTool, ToolRegistry, type ToolProps } from '@kortix/ui/session';
import { TerminalWindowIcon } from '@phosphor-icons/react';

function ShellRow({ call }: ToolProps) {
  const command = (call.input as { command?: string } | null)?.command ?? '';
  const output = (call.result?.content ?? [])
    .map((part) => (part.type === 'text' ? part.text : ''))
    .join('');
  return (
    <BasicTool icon={<TerminalWindowIcon />} trigger={{ title: 'Shell', subtitle: command }}>
      <pre className="bg-muted overflow-x-auto rounded-md p-3 font-mono text-xs">
        {output || 'No output yet'}
      </pre>
    </BasicTool>
  );
}

// Replaces the built-in row of the `bash` tool.
ToolRegistry.register('bash', ShellRow);
```

Import the file once at your entry: `import './shell-row'`.

#### Compose a custom workspace

The modules compose at any depth. Pick the level you need.

| You want | Use |
| --- | --- |
| The Kortix session screen in your chrome | `SessionWorkspace` |
| Your own conversation column beside the Kortix side panel | `<SessionWorkspace session={session}>{yourColumn}</SessionWorkspace>` |
| The thread, no panel | `SessionThread`. It mounts its own preview host. A page with no `SessionThread` and no side panel mounts `<FilePreviewHost />` from `@kortix/ui/files` once. |
| Your own panel | `SessionPanelProvider` and `ActionPanel` from `@kortix/ui/session-panel`, or `SessionTerminal` from `@kortix/ui/terminal` |
| Single blocks | `UserMessage`, `ThrottledMarkdown`, `ToolPartRenderer`, `ModelSelector`, `AgentSelector`, `SessionPermissionPrompt`, `QuestionPrompt` from `@kortix/ui/session`; `ComposerChatInput` from `@kortix/ui/composer` |
| Every part drawn by your own components | No `@kortix/ui/session`. Use `useChatTurns` and `renderParts` from `@kortix/sdk/react`: [Render messages yourself](/docs/developers#render-messages-yourself). |

A block outside `SessionThread`, `SessionWorkspace` and `ProjectHome` needs the two scopes those screens mount for you: `KortixProjectProvider` from `@kortix/sdk/react` and `SessionIdsProvider` from `@kortix/ui`. Without `SessionIdsProvider`, `useSessionIds()` returns no ids.

```tsx title="ask.tsx"
import { KortixProjectProvider } from '@kortix/sdk/react';
import { SessionIdsProvider } from '@kortix/ui';
import { ComposerChatInput } from '@kortix/ui/composer';

// The composer before a session exists. You decide what a send does.
export function Ask({ projectId, onAsk }: { projectId: string; onAsk: (text: string) => void }) {
  return (
    <KortixProjectProvider projectId={projectId}>
      <SessionIdsProvider projectId={projectId}>
        <ComposerChatInput projectId={projectId} onSend={(text) => onAsk(text)} />
      </SessionIdsProvider>
    </KortixProjectProvider>
  );
}
```

### Routes

Every URL the components link to or navigate to comes from one route table. Call `setUiRoutes` (`@kortix/ui`) once at module load. An omitted route keeps its default.

- Return your own path for a page you serve at another URL.
- Return `null` for a page you do not serve. Every link to it is hidden and no navigation goes there.
- `project` and `session` cannot return `null`.

| Route | Arguments | Default |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `project` | `projectId` | `/projects/<id>` (the new-session composer) |
| `session` | `projectId`, `sessionId` | `/projects/<id>/sessions/<sessionId>` |
| `sessions` | `projectId`, `view?: 'archived' \| 'deleted'` | `/projects/<id>/sessions`, `?view=<view>` |
| `files` | `projectId`, `panel?: 'proposed-changes'` | `/projects/<id>/files`, `?panel=<panel>` |
| `drive` | `projectId` | `/projects/<id>/drive` |
| `apps` | `projectId` | `/projects/<id>/apps` |
| `members` | `projectId` | `/projects/<id>/members` |
| `sandboxSettings` | `projectId` | `/projects/<id>/config?section=sandbox` |
| `customize` | `projectId`, `path?` | `/projects/<id>/customize/<path>` (agents, skills, connectors, triggers, reminders, models, secrets, review, settings) |

```ts
import { setUiRoutes } from '@kortix/ui';

setUiRoutes({
  session: (projectId, sessionId) => `/w/${projectId}/chat/${sessionId}`,
  drive: () => null,
  customize: () => null,
});
```

`uiRoutes()` returns the active table. `defaultUiRoutes` holds the defaults.

### Theming

White-label means CSS variable overrides. Every value a host varies is a CSS variable: colors in light and dark, the radius, border width, fonts, the type scale, spacing density, shadows and motion. `@kortix/ui/theme` turns a typed theme object into those variables. Every field is optional. An unset field keeps the Kortix default. An unset `dark` color falls back to the Kortix dark default, not to your `light` value.

```tsx title="app/layout.tsx"
import { themeToCss, type KortixTheme } from '@kortix/ui/theme';
import type { ReactNode } from 'react';

const brand: KortixTheme = {
  light: { blue: '#4f46e5', ring: '#4f46e5', border: '#e0e7ff' },
  dark: { blue: '#818cf8', ring: '#818cf8', border: '#312e81' },
  radius: '0.25rem',
  fonts: { sans: 'Inter, system-ui, sans-serif' },
};

export default function RootLayout({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
  return (
    <html lang="en">
      <head>
        <style>{themeToCss(brand)}</style>
      </head>
      <body>{children}</body>
    </html>
  );
}
```

`themeToCss` is a pure function. It runs on the server, so the first paint has your theme. Its rules out-rank `tokens.css`, so the `<style>` element can sit anywhere in the page. The white-label examples theme this way: `apps/white-label/lumen/src/config/theme.ts` and `apps/white-label/reskin/src/config/theme.ts`.

| Export | Signature | Use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `KortixTheme` | `{ light?, dark?, radius?, spacing?, borderWidth?, fonts?, text?, lineHeights?, shadows?, durations?, easing?, vars? }` | The theme. `light` and `dark` take the color keys below plus any `--custom` variable. `vars` sets any other variable in both modes. An unknown field, color or step is a type error and throws at runtime. |
| `themeToCss` | `(theme: KortixTheme, selector = ':root') => string` | A stylesheet: light outside `.dark`, dark on or inside `.dark`. Pass a selector to theme one element. It throws on a value that contains `;`, `{`, `}`, `<`, `>`, `\` or a comment, and on a variable name that does not match `--[A-Za-z0-9_-]+`. A theme built from tenant data cannot add CSS rules. |
| `themeToCssVars` | `(theme: KortixTheme) => { light: Record<string, string>; dark: Record<string, string> }` | The same variables as data, for a host that writes its own CSS or sets them from JavaScript. Mode-independent values appear in both. |
| `ThemeScope` | `<ThemeScope theme={...} className? {...divProps}>` | Themes one subtree: an embedded widget, a customer preview, a settings pane. |
| `THEME_COLOR_VARS` | `Record<ThemeColor, string>` | The color key to CSS variable map. |

#### ThemeScope

```tsx title="themed-chat.tsx"
import type { UseSessionResult } from '@kortix/sdk/react';
import { SessionThread } from '@kortix/ui/session';
import { ThemeScope } from '@kortix/ui/theme';

export function ThemedChat({ session }: { session: UseSessionResult }) {
  return (
    <ThemeScope theme={{ light: { primary: '#0f766e' }, radius: '1rem' }} className="bg-background h-dvh">
      <SessionThread session={session} />
    </ThemeScope>
  );
}
```

- Dialogs, popovers, menus, tooltips, selects, sheets and drawers opened inside the scope portal into a host at the end of `<body>` that carries the scope's theme. A nested scope's overlays carry both themes. A `transform`, `filter`, `backdrop-filter`, `contain` or `overflow: hidden` ancestor of the scope does not clip or offset the overlays.
- The scope sets `font-family` and `color` on its element. It paints no background: add `bg-background`.
- `className="dark"` forces dark for the subtree. A scope cannot force light inside a dark page.
- The scope element holds only your children. Its `<style>` renders as the sibling before it. `flex`, `grid`, `space-y-*`, `first:` and `last:` on the children behave as without the scope.
- Toasts do not portal. To theme toasts, mount `<Toaster />` inside the `ThemeScope`, at its top level, not inside a transformed panel.

#### Tokens

Colors take any CSS color. Defaults are light / dark.

| Key | Variable | Controls | Default |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `background` | `--background` | The page | `#ffffff` / `#0b0b0b` |
| `pane`, `surface` | `--pane`, `--surface` | Base and raised panes | `#ffffff` / `#0c0c0c`, `#fcfcfc` / `#141414` |
| `foreground` | `--foreground` | Text | `#1f1f1f` / `#ffffff` |
| `foregroundStrong`, `foregroundWeak` | `--foreground-strong`, `--foreground-weak` | Emphasized and quiet text | follow `foreground` / `mutedForeground` |
| `action`, `actionForeground` | `--action`, `--action-foreground` | The default `Button` fill and its text (primary actions, Send) | follow `foreground` / `background` |
| `card`, `cardForeground` | `--card`, `--card-foreground` | `Card`, chat tool cards and questions | `#f4f4f4` / `#141414` |
| `popover`, `popoverForeground` | `--popover`, `--popover-foreground` | Menus, popovers, inputs | `#ffffff` / `#141414` |
| `primary`, `primaryForeground` | `--primary`, `--primary-foreground` | `Progress`, `Slider`, `Calendar` selection, menu highlight | `#1f1f1f` / `#ffffff` |
| `secondary`, `secondaryForeground` | `--secondary`, `--secondary-foreground` | Secondary buttons | `#ededed` / `#1c1c1c` |
